He was aware that legal proceedings were taken as regards the presumption of his death and the administration of the estates; he was also aware of the death of his younger brother and that title and estates were now in possession of his nephew﻿—His Lordship there. In fact, he was very well up in the whole story, according to Methley and Woodlesford,” said Mr. Carless, with a smile. “And Lord Ellingham believed that Methley and Woodlesford were genuinely convinced by him.”
“Seemed so, anyway, both of ’em,” agreed Lord Ellingham.
“However,” continued Mr. Carless, “Methley and Woodlesford, like you and I, Pawle, are limbs of the law. They asked two very pertinent questions. First﻿—why had he come forward after this long interval? Second﻿—what evidence had he to support and prove his claim?”
“Good!” muttered Mr. Pawle. “And I’ll be bound he had some excellent replies ready for them.”
“He had,” said Mr. Carless. “He answered as regards the first question that of late things had not gone well with him. He was still comfortably off, but he had lost a lot of money in Australia through speculation. He replied to the second by producing certain papers and documents.”

===Chinese===

“据他说，他从少年时代起就同父亲合不来：父子俩素来彼此厌恶。他长大成人之后，父亲更是千方百计地掣肘他。他自己呢，年纪轻轻染上了激进民主的思想——这就使父子间的裂痕愈发深了。终于，他打定主意，索性一走了之，彻底离开英国。他自己有一笔不算多的钱，是母亲留给他的几千镑。于是他带上这笔钱，悄没声儿地失踪了。
“照他自己的说法，他成了一个十足的漂泊者，走遍世上种种偏僻角落，而且每到一处，都格外小心地隐匿自己的身份。他告诉梅思利和伍德斯福德这两个人，说自己先后在南非、印度、中国、日本和马来居留地（the Malay Settlement）居住经商——最后在澳大利亚落了脚。他一直留意着英国国内的事情——知道父亲去世的消息，也看到过数不清的寻找自己的启事。他晓得家里人已经就推定他死亡、管理产业等事提起过法律程序；也晓得弟弟已经去世，爵位和产业如今都在他侄子——就是那边那位大人——的手里。总之，照梅思利和伍德斯福德的说法，这整个故事他熟得很呢，”卡利斯先生含笑说道，“而埃灵厄姆勋爵相信，梅思利和伍德斯福德是真真切切信了他的话。”
“反正他们两个瞧着是信了，”埃灵厄姆勋爵附和道。
“不过，”卡利斯先生继续道，“梅思利和伍德斯福德同你我一样，波尔，都是吃法律饭的。他们提了两个非常切中要害的问题。第一——隔了这么久，他为什么偏偏这时候才站出来？第二——他有什么证据来支持并证明自己的声索？”
“问得好！”波尔先生咕哝道，“而且我敢打赌，他还备好了一套顶呱呱的答复来应付他们。”
“备好了，”卡利斯先生说，“对第一个问题，他回答说，近年来他手头不顺利。日子过得依旧宽裕，只是在澳大利亚搞投机亏掉了许多钱。对第二个问题，他的回答是拿出了若干信件和文书。”

===Original===

“Ah!” exclaimed Mr. Pawle, nudging Viner. “Now we’re warming to it!”
“And according to what Methley and Woodlesford told Lord Ellingham,” continued Mr. Carless, “these papers and documents are of a very convincing nature. They said to His Lordship frankly that they were greatly surprised by them. They had thought that this man might possibly be a bogus claimant, who had somehow gained a thorough knowledge of the facts he was narrating, but the papers he produced, which, he alleged, had never been out of his possession since his secret flight from London, were﻿—well, staggering. After inspecting them, Methley and Woodlesford came to the conclusion that their caller really was what he claimed to be﻿—the missing man!”
“What were the papers?” demanded Mr. Pawle.
“Oh!” replied Mr. Carless, looking at his client. “Letters, certificates, and the like﻿—all, according to Methley and Woodlesford, excellent proofs of identity.”
“Did they show them to Your Lordship?” asked Mr. Pawle.
“Oh, no! they only told me of them,” answered Lord Ellingham. “They said, of course, that they would be shown to me, or to Mr. Carless.”

===Chinese===

“啊！”波尔先生叫道，用胳膊肘碰了碰维纳，“这下子渐入佳境了！”
“而按梅思利和伍德斯福德告诉埃灵厄姆勋爵的说法，”卡利斯先生继续道，“这些信件和文书极具说服力。他们坦率地对大人说，自己看了大为吃惊。他们原本以为，此人多半是个假冒的主张人，不知怎么打听清楚了他所讲述的全部事实；可他拿出的这些文件——据他声称，自他当年秘密逃离伦敦起，就从没离开过他的身边——嗯，实在令人震惊。仔细查验之后，梅思利和伍德斯福德得出结论：来客确如他自己所声称的那样——正是那个失踪的人！”
“都是些什么文件？”波尔先生追问。
“哦！”卡利斯先生答道，一面望着自己的当事人，“信件、证明文书之类——据梅思利和伍德斯福德说，全都是证明身份的绝好凭据。”
“他们把这些文件给大人看了吗？”波尔先生问。
“哦，没有！他们只是对我提了提有这些文件，”埃灵厄姆勋爵答道，“他们当然说了，这些文件会拿给我本人或者卡利斯先生过目。”

===Original===

“Aye!” muttered Mr. Pawle. “Just so! Yes, and they will have to be shown!”
“That follows as a matter of course,” observed Mr. Carless. “But now, Pawle, we come to the real point of the case. Methley and Woodlesford, having informed His Lordship of all this when they called on him yesterday afternoon then proceeded to tell him precisely what their client, the claimant, as we will now call him, really wanted, for he had been at some pains, considerable pains, to make himself clear on that point to them, and he desired them to make themselves clear to Lord Ellingham, whom he throughout referred to as his nephew. He had no desire, he told them, to recover his title, nor the estates. He did not care a cent﻿—his own phrase﻿—for the title. He was now sixty years of age. The life he had lived had quite unfitted him for the positions and duties of an English nobleman. He wanted to go back to the country in which he had settled. But as title and estates really were his, he wanted his nephew, the present holder, to make him a proper payment, in consideration of the receipt of which he would engage to preserve the silence which he had already kept so thoroughly and effectively for thirty-five years. Eh?”
“In plain language,” said Mr. Pawle, “he wanted to be bought.”
“Precisely!” agreed Mr. Carless. “Of course, Methley and Woodlesford didn’t quite put it in that light. They put it that their client had no wish to disturb his nephew, but suggested, kindly, that his nephew should make him a proper payment out of his abundance.”
Mr. Pawle turned to Lord Ellingham.
“Did they mention a sum to Your Lordship?” he asked.

===Chinese===

“可不是！”波尔先生咕哝道，“正是这话！对，这些文件迟早得摆出来见人！”
“那是自然，”卡利斯先生说，“可是现在，波尔，咱们说到本案真正的要害了。梅思利和伍德斯福德昨天下午拜会大人，把这一切禀明之后，接着便一五一十地告诉他，他们的委托人——咱们如今不妨就称他为主张人吧——真正想要的是什么。因为在这件事上，他曾费了不少心思，相当不少的心思，向他们两位把话交代明白，并且嘱咐他们向埃灵厄姆勋爵原原本本转述清楚——他从头到尾都把勋爵称作自己的侄子。他告诉他们，他无意收回爵位，也无意收回产业。爵位在他眼里一文不值——这是他的原话。他如今已年届六十。这些年过惯的生活，使他全然不适应英国贵族的身份和职分。他想回到他定居的那个国家去。不过，爵位和产业毕竟本来就是他的，他要他的侄子、如今的持有人，付给他一笔像样的款项；作为收下这笔款项的交换，他保证继续保守那个秘密——这秘密他已经彻彻底底、行之有效地守了三十五年。怎么样？”
“说白了，”波尔先生说，“他是想让人花钱收买。”
“一点不错！”卡利斯先生赞同道，“当然，梅思利和伍德斯福德没有说得这么难听。他们的说法是：委托人无意惊扰他的侄子，只是客客气气地提议，做侄子的不妨从自己的丰厚家财里拨出一笔像样的款项给他。”
波尔先生转向埃灵厄姆勋爵。
“他们向大人提过数目吗？”他问。

===Original===

“Yes,” replied Lord Ellingham, with a smile at Carless. “They did﻿—tentatively.”
“How much?” asked Mr. Pawle.
“One hundred thousand pounds!”
“On receipt of which, I suppose,” observed Mr. Pawle dryly, “nothing would ever be heard again of your lordship’s long-lost uncle, the rightful owner of all that Your Lordship possesses?”
Lord Ellingham laughed.
“So I gathered!” he answered.

===Chinese===

“提过，”埃灵厄姆勋爵答道，朝卡利斯笑了笑，“提了——只是试探着说的。”
“多少？”波尔先生问。
“十万英镑！”
“想必是钱一入手，”波尔先生冷冷地说，“大人那位失踪多年的叔父——大人全部家当的正主儿——从此就再无音信了？”
埃灵厄姆勋爵哈哈一笑。
“我听出来的正是这个意思！”他答道。

===Original===

“I wish I’d been present when Methley and Woodlesford put forward that proposition,” exclaimed the old lawyer. “Did they seem serious?”
“Oh, I think they were quite serious,” replied Lord Ellingham. “They seemed so; they spoke of it as what they called a domestic arrangement.”
“Excellent phrase!” remarked Mr. Pawle. “And what said your lordship to their﻿—or the claimant’s proposition?”
“I told them that the matter was so serious that they and I must see my solicitors about it,” answered Lord Ellingham, “and I arranged to meet them here at one o’clock today. They quite agreed that that was the proper thing to do, and went away. Then﻿—you and Mr. Viner called.”
“With, I understand, another extraordinary story,” remarked Mr. Carless. “The particulars of which His Lordship has also told me. Now, Pawle, what do you really say about all this?”
Mr. Pawle smote his clenched right fist on the palm of his open left hand.

===Chinese===

“梅思利和伍德斯福德提出这项提议的时候，我真恨不得在场，”老律师嚷道，“他们看样子是认真的吗？”
“哦，我看他们相当认真，”埃灵厄姆勋爵答道，“瞧着是认真的；他们把这件事说成是——用他们的话说——一桩家事安排。”
“好一个说法！”波尔先生评论道，“那么大人对他们——或者说对主张人——的这项提议，是怎么答复的？”
“我告诉我们，此事非同小可，他们和我必须来见见我的事务律师，”埃灵厄姆勋爵答道，“于是我约定今天一点钟在这儿同他们会面。他们满口同意这是正当的做法，便告辞了。随后——您和维纳先生就登门了。”
“而且据我所知，还带来了另一个离奇的故事，”卡利斯先生说，“其中的详情大人也都告诉我了。那么，波尔，这一切您到底怎么看？”
波尔先生攥起右拳，猛地击在摊开的左掌上。

===Original===

“I will tell you what I say, Carless!” he exclaimed with emphasis. “I say that whatever the papers and documents were which were produced by this man to Methley and Woodlesford, they were stolen from the body of John Ashton, who was foully murdered in Lonsdale Passage only last week. I’ll stake all I have on that! Now, then, did this claimant steal them? Did he murder John Ashton for them? No﻿—a thousand times no, for no man would have been such a fool as to come forward with them so soon after his victim’s death! This claimant doesn’t know how or where or when they were obtained﻿—he doesn’t suspect that murder’s in it. Now, then﻿—where did he get them? Who’s at the back of him? Who﻿—to be plain﻿—who’s making a cat’s-paw of him? Find that out, and we shall know who murdered John Ashton!”
Viner, glancing at Lord Ellingham and at Mr. Carless, saw that Mr. Pawle’s words had impressed them greatly, the solicitor especially. He nodded sympathetically, and Mr. Pawle went on speaking.
“Listen here, Carless!” he continued. “Mr. Viner and I have been investigating this case as far as we could, largely to save a man whom we both believe to be absolutely innocent of murder. I have come to certain conclusions. John Ashton, many years ago, fell in with the missing Lord Marketstoke, then living under the name of Wickham, in Australia, and they became close friends. At some time or other, Wickham told Ashton the real truth about himself, and when he died, left his little daughter﻿—”
Carless looked sharply round.
“Ah!” he exclaimed. “So there’s a daughter?”
“There is a daughter, and her name is Avice﻿—a name borne by a good many women of the Cave-Gray family,” answered Mr. Pawle with a significant glance at his fellow-practitioner. “But let me go on: Wickham left his daughter, her mother being dead, in Ashton’s guardianship. She was then about six years of age. Ashton sent her to school here in England. About twelve or thirteen years later, he came home and settled in Markendale Square. He brought Avice Wickham to live with him. He handed over to her a considerable sum, which, he said, her father had left in his hands for her. And then, secretly, Ashton went down to Marketstoke and evidently made certain inquiries and investigations. Whether he was going to reveal the truth as to what I have just told you, we don’t know﻿—probably he was. But he was murdered, and we all know when and where. And I say he was murdered for the sake of these very papers which we now know were produced to Methley and Woodlesford by this claimant. Now, then﻿—”

===Chinese===

“我告诉您我是怎么看的，卡利斯！”他加重语气嚷道，“我说，此人拿给梅思利和伍德斯福德看的那些信件和文书，不管究竟是什么，都是从约翰·阿什顿（John Ashton）身上偷来的——就在上礼拜，阿什顿在朗斯代尔过道（Lonsdale Passage）被人残忍地谋害了。这一点，我拿全部家当打赌！好，那么，是这个主张人偷的吗？是他为了这些文件谋害了约翰·阿什顿吗？不——一千个不是，一万个不是，因为谁也不会蠢到这步田地：刚害死被害人没几天，就揣着赃物站出来！这位主张人根本不知道这些文件是怎么弄来的、在哪儿弄来的、什么时候弄来的——他压根不疑心这里面还搭着一条人命。那么——他是从哪儿弄到这些文件的？他背后是谁？说白了——是谁在拿他当枪使？查出这一点，咱们就知道是谁谋杀了约翰·阿什顿！”
维纳瞧了瞧埃灵厄姆勋爵，又瞧了瞧卡利斯先生，看出波尔先生这番话给了他们极深的印象，那位律师尤其是如此。他深以为然地点着头，波尔先生便接着说了下去。
“您听着，卡利斯！”他继续道，“维纳先生和我一直在尽力查这个案子，主要是为了搭救一个人——我们俩都坚信他绝无可能杀人。我已经得出了几点结论。多年以前，约翰·阿什顿在澳大利亚结识了那位失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵——他当时化名威克姆（Wickham）——两人成了莫逆之交。在某个时候，威克姆把自己的真实身世告诉了阿什顿；临死之前，又把自己的小女儿留给了他——
卡利斯敏锐地朝四下里看了看。
“啊！”他叫道，“这么说，还有个女儿？”
“是有个女儿，她的名字叫艾维斯（Avice）——凯夫-格雷（Cave-Gray）家好多位女眷都叫这个名字，”波尔先生答道，意味深长地朝同行瞥了一眼，“不过让我说下去：威克姆把女儿——她母亲当时已经过世——交托给阿什顿监护。她那时大约六岁。阿什顿把她送回英国上学。大约十二三年后，他回到英国，在马肯代尔广场（Markendale Square）定居下来。他把艾维斯·威克姆接来同住。他交给她一大笔钱，说这是她父亲生前存在他手里留给她的。再后来，阿什顿悄悄跑了一趟马克特斯托克（Marketstoke），分明做了一番查访问询。他是否正打算把我方才告诉您的这些真相公之于众，我们不得而知——多半是的。可他遇害了，何时何地遇害，咱们大家都很清楚。而我断言，他就是为了这些文件才丧的命——如今我们已知道，主张人正是把这些文件拿给了梅思利和伍德斯福德看。那么，接下来——”

===Original===

Mr. Carless suddenly bent forward.
“A moment, Pawle!” he said. “If this man Wickham really was the lost Lord Marketstoke, and he’s dead, and he left a daughter, and the daughter’s alive﻿—”
“Well?” demanded Mr. Pawle. “Well?”
“Why, then, of course, that daughter,” said Mr. Carless slowly, “that daughter is﻿—”
A clerk opened the door and glanced at his employer.
“Mr. Methley and Mr. Woodlesford, sir,” he announced. “By appointment.”

===Chinese===

卡利斯先生猛地俯身向前。
“等一下，波尔！”他说，“倘若这个威克姆当真就是失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵，而他已经死了，又留下了一个女儿，而这女儿还活着——
“怎么样？”波尔先生追问，“怎么样？”
“嗐，那样的话，那个女儿，”卡利斯先生慢吞吞地说，“那个女儿就是——”
一个书记员推开门，望了望自己的东家。
“梅思利先生和伍德斯福德先生到了，先生，”他通报道，“是约好的。”

## XVIII / 十八

===Original===

Let Him Appear!

The meeting between the solicitors suggested to Viner and to Lord Ellingham, who looked on curiously while they exchanged formal greetings and explanations, a certain solemnity﻿—each of them seemed to imply in look and manner that this was an unusually grave occasion. And Mr. Carless, assuming the direction of things, became almost judicial in his deportment.

“Well, gentlemen,” he said, when they had all gathered about his desk. “Lord Ellingham has informed me of what passed between you and himself at his house yesterday. In plain language, the client whom you represent claims to be the Lord Marketstoke who disappeared so completely many years ago, and therefore the rightful Earl of Ellingham. Now, a first question﻿—do you, as his legal advisers, believe in his claim?”

“Judging by the proofs with which he has furnished us, yes,” answered Methley. “There seems to be no doubt of it.”

“We’ll ask for these proofs presently,” remarked Mr. Carless. “But now a further question: Your client﻿—whom we’ll now call the claimant﻿—had, I understand, no desire to take up his rightful position, and suggests that the secret shall remain a secret, and that he shall be paid a hundred thousand pounds to hold his tongue?”

“If you put it that way﻿—yes,” replied Methley.

===Chinese===

让他现身！

两位律师会面时客套地互致问候、说明来意，维纳和埃灵厄姆勋爵在一旁好奇地旁观，只觉这场面自有一种庄严的意味——他们每个人的一颦一笑、举手投足，似乎都在示意这是一次非同寻常的重大场合。卡利斯先生当仁不让地主起事来，那做派几乎像法官问案一般。

“好吧，两位先生，”待众人围拢到他的写字台前后，他开口说道。“埃灵厄姆勋爵已经把昨天在他府上你们二位同他谈的话告诉我了。说白了，你们所代表的那位委托人，自称是多年前销声匿迹的马克特斯托克勋爵，也就是名正言顺的埃灵厄姆伯爵。那么，第一个问题——作为他的法律顾问，你们相信他的主张吗？”

“就他提供给我们的那些凭证来看，相信，”梅思利（Methley）答道。“此事似乎没有什么可怀疑的。”

“这些凭证我们回头就要过目，”卡利斯先生说。“不过眼下还有个问题：你们的委托人——咱们从现在起就称他为‘主张人’——据我所知，并不想恢复他名正言顺的地位，反而提议让这个秘密继续保守下去，由他守口如瓶，换取十万镑的酬金？”

“你要是这么说的话——是的，”梅思利答道。

===Original===

“I don’t know in what other way it could be put,” said Mr. Carless grimly. “It’s the plain truth. But now, if Lord Ellingham refuses that offer, does your client intend to commence proceedings?”

“Our instructions are﻿—yes,” answered Methley.

“Very good,” said Mr. Carless. “Now, then﻿—what are these proofs?”

Methley turned to his partner, who immediately thrust a hand in his breastpocket and produced a long envelope.

“I have them here,” said Woodlesford. “Our client entrusted them to us so that we might show them to Lord Ellingham, if necessary. There are not many documents﻿—they all relate to the period of our client’s life before he left England. There are one or two important letters from his father, the seventh Earl, two or three from his mother; there is also his mother’s will. There is one letter from his younger brother, to whom he had evidently, more than once, announced his determination of leaving home for a considerable time. There are two letters from your own firm, relating to some property which Lord Marketstoke disposed of before he left London. There is a schedule or memorandum of certain personal effects which he left in his rooms at Ellingham Hall: there is also a receipt from his bankers for a quantity of plate and jewellery which he had deposited with them before leaving﻿—these things had been left him by his mother. There are also two documents which he seems to have considered it worth while to preserve all these years,” concluded Woodlesford with a smile. “One is a letter informing him that he had been elected a member of the M.C.C.; the other is his commission as a justice of the peace for the county of Buckinghamshire.”

As he detailed these things, Woodlesford laid each specified paper before Mr. Carless, and then they all gathered round, and examined each exhibit. The various documents were somewhat faded with age, and the edges of some were worn as if from long folding and keeping in a pocketbook. Mr. Carless hastily ran his eye over them.

===Chinese===

“我想不出还能换成什么别的说法，”卡利斯先生冷冷地说。“事实就是这么回事。那么再问一句：倘若埃灵厄姆勋爵拒绝这项提议，你们的委托人打算提起诉讼吗？”

“我们接到的授意是——要起诉，”梅思利答道。

“很好，”卡利斯先生说。“那么——那些凭证都是些什么？”

梅思利转向他的合伙人，后者立刻把手伸进胸前口袋，取出一只长长的信封。

“东西都在我这里，”伍德斯福德（Woodlesford）说。“我们的委托人把它们托付给我们，以备必要时代我们出示给埃灵厄姆勋爵。文件并不多——全都关系到我们委托人离开英国之前的那段生活。有他父亲、也就是第七代伯爵写来的一两封重要信件，有他母亲的两三封；还有他母亲的遗嘱。有一封他弟弟的信——看得出来，他曾不止一次向这位弟弟宣告过自己打算离家相当长一段时间。有两封信出自贵事务所之手，涉及马克特斯托克勋爵离开伦敦之前处置的某项财产。还有一份清单，或者说备忘录，开列着他留在埃灵厄姆庄园自己房里的若干随身物件；另有一张他的银行出具的收条，证明他行前存放在银行里的一批金银器皿和珠宝——这些东西都是他母亲留给他的。另外还有两份文件，他似乎觉得值得为它们保存这么多年，”伍德斯福德含笑收尾道。“一份是通知他已当选为 M.C.C.（马里波恩板球俱乐部）会员的信函；另一份是他出任白金汉郡治安法官的委任状。”

伍德斯福德一边逐件报明，一边把提到的每一纸文书摆到卡利斯先生面前；随后众人围拢过来，一件件查验这些证物。各样文件都因年深日久而有些褪色，有几件的边缘已然磨损，像是长久折叠、收在记事本里的缘故。卡利斯先生匆匆把它们浏览了一遍。

===Original===

“Very interesting, gentlemen,” he remarked. “But you know, as well as I do, that these things don’t prove your client to be the missing Lord Marketstoke. A judge and jury would want a lot more evidence than that. The mere fact that your man is in possession of all these documents proves nothing whatever. He may have stolen them!”

“From what we have seen of our client, Mr. Carless,” observed Methley, with some stiffness of manner, “there is no need for such a suggestion.”

“I dare say we shall all see a good deal of your client before this matter is settled, Mr. Methley,” retorted Mr. Carless. “And even when I have seen a lot of him, I should still say the same﻿—he *may* have stolen them! What else has he to prove that he’s what he says he is?”

“He is fully conversant with his family history,” said Woodlesford. “He can give a perfectly full and﻿—so far as we can judge﻿—accurate account of his early life and of his subsequent doings. He evidently knows all about Ellingham Hall, Marketstoke and the surroundings. I think if you were to examine him on these points, you would find that his memory is surprisingly fresh.”

“I have no doubt that it will come to his being examined on a great many points and in much detail,” said Mr. Carless with a dry smile. “Of course, I shall be much interested in seeing him. You see, I remember the missing Lord Marketstoke very well indeed﻿—he was often in here when I, as a lad of nineteen or twenty, was articled to my own father. And now, gentlemen, I’ll ask you a question and commend it to your intelligence and common sense: if your client is this man he claims to be, why didn’t he come straight to Carless and Driver, whom he would remember well enough, instead of going to Methley and Woodlesford? Come, now?”

Neither visitor answered this question, and Mr. Pawle suddenly turned on them with another.

===Chinese===

“很有意思，两位先生，”他说。“可是你们跟我一样清楚，这些东西并不能证明你们的委托人就是失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵。法官和陪审团要的证据远不止这些。你们的当事人手里握有这全部文件，仅凭这一点什么也证明不了。这些文件说不定是他偷来的！”

“就我们对委托人的了解而言，卡利斯先生，”梅思利说道，神色略见僵硬，“这样的揣测大可不必。”

“这件事了结之前，咱们大家少不了要跟你们的委托人打交道，梅思利先生，”卡利斯先生反唇相讥。“到时候就算我见了他许多回，我还是这句话——这些文件他也*可能*是偷来的！除此之外，他还拿什么证明自己确如本人所说的那个人？”

“他对本家世系了如指掌，”伍德斯福德说。“他能够把自己的早年生活和此后的经历原原本本、而且——就我们所能判断的——准确无误地讲述出来。他显然对埃灵厄姆庄园、马克特斯托克和周围一带的一切都熟悉得很。我想，倘若你们在这些地方考问他一番，就会发现他的记忆出奇地清晰。”

“毫无疑问，将来会有许许多多的问题要细细考问他，”卡利斯先生带着干笑说道。“当然，见着他我是很感兴趣的。要知道，那位失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵我记得真真切切——当年我十九二十岁，在我父亲手下做见习生的时候，他常到这间办公室里来。那么，两位先生，我要向你们提个问题，也请二位的智力和常识来评判评判：倘若你们的委托人真是他自称的那个人，他为什么不径直来找他断然记得的卡利斯与德赖弗事务所，却偏偏去找梅思利和伍德斯福德？说说看？”

两位来客都没能回答这个问题，这时波尔先生突然转向他们，抛出了另一个问题。

===Original===

“Did your client mention to you that he knew Carless and Driver as the family solicitors?” he asked.

“No, I can’t say that he did,” admitted Methley. “After all, thirty-five years’ absence, you know﻿—”

“You said just now that his memory was surprisingly fresh,” interrupted Mr. Pawle.

“Surely,” replied Woodlesford, “surely you can’t expect a man who has been away from England all that time to remember everything!”

“I should have expected Lord Marketstoke to have gone straight to the family solicitors, anyway,” retorted Mr. Pawle. “Obvious thing to do﻿—if his story is a true one.”

Woodlesford glanced at his partner, and repossessing himself of the documents, began to arrange them in the envelope from which he had drawn them.

===Chinese===

“你们的委托人可曾向你们提起，他知道卡利斯与德赖弗事务所是本家的律师？”他问。

“没有，这我倒不能说他提过，”梅思利承认道。“毕竟离乡三十五年，你们知道——”

“你方才还说他的记忆出奇地清晰呢，”波尔先生插话道。

“得了，”伍德斯福德答道，“一个离开英国那么多年的人，总不能指望他把什么都记得一清二楚吧！”

“不管怎么说，换了马克特斯托克勋爵，我以为他准会径直去找本家的律师，”波尔先生反驳道。“明摆着该做的事——倘若他讲的是实话。”

伍德斯福德瞧了合伙人一眼，收回那些文件，动手把它们理回原先取出的那只信封里去。

===Original===

“We cannot, of course, say positively who our client is or who he is not,” he said. “All we can say is that he came to us with an introduction from an old client of ours whom we knew very well, and that his story seems to us to be quite credible. No doubt he can bring further proof. That he did not come here in the first instance﻿—”

“I’ll tell you why I, personally, am very much surprised that he didn’t,” interrupted Mr. Carless. “You told Lord Ellingham yesterday that your client saw no end of advertisements for him at the time of his father’s death. Now, we, Carless and Driver, sent out those advertisements﻿—our name was appended to every one of them, wherever they appeared. Why, then, when this man﻿—if he is the real man﻿—returned home, did he not come to us? For there are three persons in this office who﻿—but wait!”

He touched a bell; the clerk who had announced Methley and Woodlesford put his head in at the door.

“Ask Mr. Portlethwaite to come here,” commanded Mr. Carless. “And just find out if Mr. Driver is in his room. Portlethwaite can tell me when he comes.”

An elderly, grey-haired man presently appeared and closed the door behind him as if aware of the sacred nature of the proceedings.

“Mr. Driver is out, Mr. Carless,” he said. “You wanted me, I think?”

===Chinese===

“当然，我们的委托人究竟是谁、不是谁，我们无法断言，”他说。“我们只能说，他是由一位我们知之甚深的老主顾引荐来的，他讲的情况在我们听来颇为可信。毫无疑问，他还能拿出进一步的凭据。至于他当初没有先上这里来——”

“我来告诉你们，他为什么没有先来，这一点我个人是大为诧异的，”卡利斯先生插话道。“你们昨天对埃灵厄姆勋爵说过，你们的委托人在他父亲去世那阵子，见到的寻人启事不知有多少。喏，那些启事正是我们卡利斯与德赖弗事务所登的——不管登在哪里，每一条后面都署着我们的名字。那么，这个人——倘若他是真货——回到家门之后，为什么不来找我们？要知道，这间事务所里有三个人能够——且慢！”

他按了按铃；方才通报梅思利和伍德斯福德到访的那个书记员从门口探进头来。

“请波特尔思韦特（Portlethwaite）先生到这里来，”卡利斯先生吩咐道。“再去看一看德赖弗先生在不在他屋里。波特尔思韦特来了自会告诉我。”

不一会儿，一个头发花白的老者走了进来，随手关上身后的房门，仿佛深知眼前这场合干系重大、不容旁听。

“德赖弗先生出去了，卡利斯先生，”他说。“您找我有事？”

===Original===

“Our senior clerk,” observed Mr. Carless, by way of introduction. “Portlethwaite, you remember the Lord Marketstoke who disappeared some thirty-five years ago?”

Mr. Portlethwaite smiled.

“Quite well, Mr. Carless!” he answered. “As if it were yesterday. He used to come here a good deal, you know.”

“Do you think you’d know him again, Portlethwaite, after all these years?” asked Mr. Carless. “Thirty-five years, mind!”

The elderly clerk smiled﻿—more assuredly than before. Then he looked significantly at a corner of the room, and Mr. Carless took the hint, and rising from his chair, went aside with him. Portlethwaite whispered something in his employer’s ear, and Carless suddenly laughed and nodded.

“To be sure﻿—to be sure﻿—I remember now!” he said aloud. “Thank you, Portlethwaite: that’s all. Well, gentlemen,” he continued, returning to his desk when the clerk had gone. “I think the best thing you can do is to bring your client here﻿—if he is the real and genuine article, he will, I am sure, be very glad indeed to meet three persons who knew him quite intimately in the old days﻿—Mr. Driver, Mr. Portlethwaite and myself. And I really don’t know that there’s any more to do or say.”

===Chinese===

“我们的资深书记员，”卡利斯先生顺势介绍道。“波特尔思韦特，你还记得大约三十五年前失踪的那位马克特斯托克勋爵吗？”

波特尔思韦特先生微微一笑。

“记得清楚得很，卡利斯先生！”他答道。“就像昨天的事一样。他从前常到这儿来，您是知道的。”

“波特尔思韦特，隔了这么多年，你觉得你还认得出他来吗？”卡利斯先生问。“三十五年哪，别忘了！”

老书记员笑了——比刚才更有把握似的。随即他会意地朝房间一角递了个眼色，卡利斯先生心领神会，从椅子上起身，同他走到一边去。波特尔思韦特对着东家的耳朵低语了几句，卡利斯忽然笑了起来，点了点头。

“可不是——可不是——我如今想起来了！”他出声说道。“谢谢你，波特尔思韦特：没你的事了。那么，两位先生，”书记员退下后，他回到写字台旁继续说道。“我看你们最好是带你们的委托人到这儿来一趟——他若是货真价实的正主儿，我相信，他一定非常乐于见到三位当年与他相熟不过的人——德赖弗先生、波特尔思韦特先生和我本人。此外还要做什么、说什么，我实在想不出了。”

===Original===

The two visitors rose, and Methley looked at Mr. Carless in a questioning fashion.

“Am I to go away with the impression that you believe our client to be an impostor?” he said quietly.

“Frankly I do!” answered Mr. Carless.

“So do I!” exclaimed Mr. Pawle. “Emphatically so!”

“In that case,” said Methley, “I see no advantage in bringing him here.”

“Not even anything to your own advantage?” suggested Mr. Carless, with a keen glance which passed from one partner to the other. “You, as reputable practitioners of our profession, don’t want to be mixed up with an impostor?”

===Chinese===

两位来客站起身来，梅思利用探询的目光望着卡利斯先生。

“我是否可以这么理解：你们认定我们的委托人是个冒牌货？”他不动声色地说。

“恕我直言，正是！”卡利斯先生答道。

“我也这么看！”波尔先生叫道。“而且是斩钉截铁地这么看！”

“既然如此，”梅思利说，“我看带他来也没有什么益处。”

“对你们自己也没有益处么？”卡利斯先生意味深长地说道，犀利的目光从一位合伙人扫到另一位。“二位是我们这一行里声望素著的执业者，总不愿意跟冒牌货搅在一起吧？”

===Original===

“We should be very sorry to be mixed up in any way with an impostor, Mr. Carless!” said Methley.

Mr. Carless pursed his lips for a moment as if he were never going to open them again; then he suddenly relaxed them.

“I tell you what it is, gentlemen!” he said. “I’m only anticipating matters in saying what I’m going to say, and I’m saying it because I feel sure you are quite sincere and genuine in this affair and are being deceived. If you will bring your client here, there are three of us in this office who, as my old clerk has just reminded me, can positively identify him on the instant if he is the man he claims to be. Positively, I say, and at once! There!”

“May one ask how?” said Woodlesford.

“No!” exclaimed Mr. Carless. “Bring him! Telephone an appointment﻿—and we’ll settle the matter as soon as he sets foot inside that door.”

“May we tell him that?” asked Methley.

===Chinese===

“无论如何，我们都不愿意跟冒牌货有什么瓜葛，卡利斯先生！”梅思利说。

卡利斯先生把嘴唇抿了片刻，好像从此再也不会张开似的；随即又猛地松开了。

“听我说，两位先生！”他说。“我下面要说的话，不过是把事情提前说破；我之所以要说，是因为我确信你们在这桩事上真心实意、并无虚假，却受人蒙骗。只要你们肯把委托人带来，这间事务所里有三个人——方才我的老书记员提醒了我——只要他真是他自称的那个人，我们当场就能把他认出来，确凿无疑。我说的是当场认出，即刻分明！就这么着！”

“能否请教，怎么个认法？”伍德斯福德说。

“不能！”卡利斯先生大声说。“把人带来！先打电话约个时间——只等他一脚踏进那扇门，我们就能把这事定夺下来。”

“这话我们可以告诉他吗？”梅思利问。

===Original===

“You can do as you like,” answered Mr. Carless. “Between ourselves, I shouldn’t! But I assure you﻿—we can tell in one glance! That’s a fact!”

The two solicitors went away; and Viner, who had closely watched Methley during the interview, followed them out and hailed Methley in the corridor outside Mr. Carless’ room.

“May I have a word with you?” he asked, drawing him aside. “I don’t know if you remember, but I saw you the other night in the parlour of that old tavern in Notting Hill﻿—you came in while I was there?”

“I had some idea that I remembered your face when we were introduced just now,” said Methley. “Yes, I think I do remember﻿—you were sitting in a corner near the hearth?”

“Just so,” agreed Viner. “And I heard you ask the landlord a question about a gentleman whom you used to meet there sometimes﻿—you left some specimen cigars with the landlord for him.”

“Yes,” assented Methley wonderingly.

===Chinese===

“随你们的便，”卡利斯先生答道。“说句体己话，换了我可不说！不过我向你们保证——我们一眼就能看出来！这是实情！”

两位律师走了；维纳在整个会谈期间一直紧盯着梅思利，此时也跟了出去，在卡利斯先生房间外的走廊里叫住了梅思利。

“能同您讲一句话吗？”他问着，把他拉到一旁。“不知您还记得不在，前几天晚上，我在诺丁山那家老酒馆的店堂里见过您——我正在那儿坐着，您就进来了？”

“方才别人给我们引见的时候，我就觉得您的面熟，”梅思利说。“对了，我想我记起来了——您当时坐在壁炉边上的一个角落里？”

“正是，”维纳应道。“我还听见您向店主打听一位先生的事——您从前有时在那儿同他会面——您还留了几支样品雪茄在店主那里，托他转交。”

“是的，”梅思利答应着，满脸诧异。

===Original===

“You never knew that man’s name?” continued Viner. “Nor who he was? Just so﻿—so I gathered. Then I’ll tell you. There was a good reason why he had not been to that tavern for some nights. He was John Ashton, the man who was murdered in Lonsdale Passage!”

Viner was watching his man with all the keenness of which he was capable, and he saw that this announcement fell on Methley as an absolute surprise. He started as only a man can start who has astounding news given to him suddenly.

“God bless me!” he exclaimed. “You don’t mean it! Of course, I know about that murder﻿—our own district. And I saw Ashton’s picture in the paper﻿—but then there are so many elderly men of that type﻿—broad features, trimmed grey beard! Dear me, dear me! A very pleasant, genial fellow. I’m astonished, Mr. Viner.”

Viner resolved on a bold step﻿—he would take it without consulting Mr. Pawle or anybody. He drew Methley further aside.

“Mr. Methley,” he said. “You’re a man of honour, and I trust you with a secret, to be kept until I release you from the obligation of secrecy. I have reasons for getting at the truth about Ashton’s murder﻿—so has Mr. Pawle. He and I have been making investigations and inquiries, and we are convinced, we are positive, that these papers which your partner now has in his pocket were stolen from Ashton’s dead body﻿—that, in fact, Ashton was murdered for the possession of them. And I tell you, for your own sake﻿—find out who this client of yours is! That he was the actual murderer I don’t believe for a second﻿—he is probably a mere cat’s-paw. But﻿—who’s behind him? If you can do anything to find out the truth, do it!”

That Methley was astonished beyond belief was so evident that Viner was now absolutely convinced of his sincerity. He stood staring open-mouthed for a moment: then he glanced at Woodlesford, who was waiting at some distance along the corridor.

===Chinese===

“您一直不知道那位先生的姓名？”维纳接着说。“也不知道他是干什么的？正是——我料想如此。那就由我来告诉您吧。他这几个晚上没有再去那家酒馆，是有一重大缘由的。他就是约翰·阿什顿，就是在朗斯代尔过道被人谋杀的那个人！”

维纳竭尽目力地观察着对方，看出这个消息落在梅思利身上，完完全全出乎意料。他猛然一震——只有骤然听到惊人之讯的人，才会那样震动。

“天哪！”他失声道。“您不是说着玩的吧！那桩凶案我自然知道——就出在我们那一区。阿什顿的照片我也在报上见过——可上了年纪、那副相貌的人实在太多——宽脸盘，修得整整齐齐的花白胡子！哎呀，哎呀！他是位非常和气、亲切的人。我真没想到，维纳先生。”

维纳决意行一步大胆的棋——不同波尔先生商量，也不同任何人商量，径自去做。他把梅思利又拉远了些。

“梅思利先生，”他说。“您是位君子，我以一桩秘密相托，在我解除您保密的义务之前，务请守口如瓶。为了查清阿什顿被杀的真相，我有我的缘由——波尔先生也有。他和我一直在暗中查访、多方打听，我们深信，确切不移地深信，您合伙人此刻揣在口袋里的这些文件，是从阿什顿的尸体上偷来的——事实上，阿什顿正是为了占有这些东西才遭了毒手。我奉劝您一句，为您自己着想——查一查你们这位委托人到底是什么人！要说他是动手行凶的凶手，我一秒钟也不相信——他多半只是个被人当枪使的角色。可是——他背后是谁？您若能有办法查明真相，就去查吧！”

梅思利那份难以置信的惊愕是那样明显，维纳此刻对他的一片真诚已确信无疑。他张着嘴，怔怔地站了片刻；随后朝走廊那头望了望伍德斯福德——他正候在稍远的地方。

===Original===

“Mr. Viner!” he said. “You amaze me! Listen: my partner is as sound and honest a fellow as there is in all London. Let me tell him this﻿—I’ll engage for his secrecy. If you’ll consent to that, I’ll see that, without a word from us as to why, this man who claims to be the missing Lord Marketstoke is brought here. If what you say is true, we are not going to be partners to a crime. Let me tell Woodlesford﻿—I’ll answer for him.”

Viner considered this proposition for a moment.

“Very well!” he said at last. “Tell him﻿—I shall trust you both. Remember﻿—it’s between the three of us. I shan’t say a word to Pawle, nor to Carless. You know there’s a man’s life at stake﻿—Hyde’s! Hyde is as innocent as I am﻿—he’s an old schoolfellow of mine.”

“I understand,” said Methley. “Very well, trust to me, Mr. Viner.”

He went off with a reassuring nod, and Viner returned to Mr. Carless’ room. The three men he had left there were deep in conversation, and as he entered, Mr. Carless smote his hand on the desk before him.

“This is certain!” he exclaimed. “We must have this Miss Avice Wickham here﻿—at once!”

===Chinese===

“维纳先生！”他说。“您真叫我吃惊！听着：我的合伙人是全伦敦数得着的正派老实人。请容我把这件事告诉他——我担保他守口如瓶。您若应允，我保证不透露一句缘由，就把这位自称失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵的人带到这儿来。倘若您说的属实，我们决不肯伙同一桩罪案。让我告诉伍德斯福德吧——我为他打包票。”

维纳把这项提议掂量了片刻。

“好吧！”他终于说道。“告诉他——你们两位我都信得过。记住——此事只限于我们三人之间。我对波尔一个字不提，对卡利斯也不提。你们知道，这可是人命关天——关系到海德！海德跟我一样清白——他是我从前的一位老同学。”

“我明白，”梅思利说。“好，交给我吧，维纳先生。”

他点了点头，令人安心地离去了；维纳回到卡利斯先生的房间。他先前留在那里的三个人正谈得入神，他刚一进门，就见卡利斯先生一掌拍在面前的写字台上。

“这就定了！”他嚷道。“我们必须把这位艾维斯·威克姆（Avice Wickham）小姐请到这儿来——立刻！”

## XIX / 十九

===Original===

Under Examination

Mr. Pawle nodded assent to this proposition and rose from his chair.

“It’s the only thing to do,” he said. “We must get to the bottom of this as quickly as possible﻿—whether Miss Wickham can tell us much or little, we must know what she can tell. Let us all meet here again at three o’clock﻿—I will send one of my clerks to fetch her. But let us be clear on one point﻿—are we to tell this young lady what our conclusions are, regarding herself?”

“Your conclusions!” said Mr. Carless, with a sly smile. “We know nothing yet, you know, Pawle.”

===Chinese===

接受盘问

波尔（Pawle）先生点头赞成这项提议，从椅子里站起身来。

“只有这一个办法，”他说。“我们必须尽快把这件事查个水落石出——无论威克姆小姐（Miss Wickham）能告诉我们的或多或少，她能说的，我们都必须知道。咱们三点钟再在这儿碰头——我派一个书记员去接她。不过有一点咱们得先说明白——咱们要不要把我们对这位小姐本人身世的结论告诉她？”

“您的结论！”卡利斯（Carless）先生狡黠地笑道。“要知道，咱们眼下还什么都不知道呢，波尔。”

===Original===

“My conclusions, then,” assented Mr. Pawle. “Are we﻿—”

Lord Ellingham quietly interrupted the old lawyer.

“Pardon me, Mr. Pawle,” he said, “but before we go any further, do you mind telling me, briefly, what your conclusions really are!”

“I will tell your lordship in a few words,” answered Mr. Pawle, readily. “Wrong or right, my conclusions are these: From certain investigations which Mr. Viner and I have made since this affair began﻿—with the murder of Ashton﻿—and from certain evidence which we have unearthed, I believe that Ashton’s friend Wickham, the father of the girl we are going to produce this afternoon, was in reality your lordship’s uncle, the missing Lord Marketstoke. I believe that Ashton came to England in order to prove this, and that he was probably about to begin proceedings when he was murdered﻿—for the sake of those papers which we have just seen. And I believe, too, that we have not seen all the papers which were stolen from his dead body. What was produced to us just now by Methley and Woodlesford was a selection﻿—the probability is that there are other and more important papers in the hands of the murderer, whose cat’s-paw or accomplice this claimant, whoever he may be, is. I believe,” concluded Mr. Pawle, with emphasis, “that my conclusions will be found to be correct ones, based on indisputable fact.”

===Chinese===

“那就说是我的结论，”波尔先生附和道。“咱们到底要不要——”

埃灵厄姆勋爵（Lord Ellingham）不动声色地打断了老律师。

“恕我冒昧，波尔先生，”他说，“不过在咱们再往下谈之前，可否请您简要地告诉我，您的结论究竟是什么？”

“三言两语就可以禀明大人，”波尔先生欣然答道。“无论对错，我的结论是这样：自打这桩案子发端——也就是阿什顿（Ashton）遇害——以来，我和维纳（Viner）先生做了几番查访，也挖出了一些证据；据此我相信，阿什顿的朋友威克姆——也就是我们今天下午要请来的那位姑娘的父亲——实际上就是大人的叔父、失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵（Lord Marketstoke）。我相信，阿什顿来英国就是为了证明这一点，而他遇害之时，多半正要着手兴讼——为了那些我们方才看过的文件。我还相信，从他遗体上偷走的文件，我们并没有看全。梅思利（Methley）和伍德斯福德（Woodlesford）方才出示给我们的，只是挑出来的一部分——很可能还有别的更要紧的文件握在凶手手里，而这个主张人，不管他是谁，不过是凶手的爪牙或帮凶。我相信，”波尔先生加重语气收束道，“我的结论终将被证明是正确的，是以无可辩驳的事实为依据的。”

===Original===

Lord Ellingham looked from one solicitor to the other.

“Then,” he said, with something of a smile, “if Wickham was really my uncle, Lord Marketstoke, and this young lady you tell me of is his daughter﻿—what, definitely, is my position?”

Mr. Pawle looked at Mr. Carless, and Mr. Carless shook his head.

“If Mr. Pawle’s theory is correct,” he said, “and mind you, Pawle, it will take a lot of proving. If Mr. Pawle’s theory is correct, the position, my lord, is this. The young lady we hear of is Countess of Ellingham in her own right! She would not be the first woman to succeed to the title: there was a Countess of Ellingham in the time of George the Third. She would, of course, have to prove her claim before the House of Lords﻿—if made good, she succeeds to titles and estates. That’s the plain English of it﻿—and upon my honour,” concluded Mr. Carless, “it’s one of the most extraordinary things I ever heard of. This other affair is nothing to it!”

===Chinese===

埃灵厄姆勋爵的目光从一位律师脸上移到另一位律师脸上。

“那么，”他带着几分笑意说，“倘若威克姆真是我的叔父马克特斯托克勋爵，而你们跟我说的这位小姐又是他的女儿——我的处境究竟算是什么呢？”

波尔先生望着卡利斯先生，卡利斯先生摇了摇头。

“倘若波尔先生的理论正确，”他说，“不过你可听着，波尔，这理论要坐实可大不容易。倘若波尔先生的理论正确，那么大人，情形便是这样：我们所说的这位小姐，凭她自身的权利就是埃灵厄姆伯爵夫人（Countess of Ellingham）！她不会是头一位承袭这个爵位的女子——乔治三世（George the Third）年间就出过一位埃灵厄姆伯爵夫人。当然，她还得上议院（House of Lords）证明自己的主张——一旦证明成立，爵号和产业便都归她承袭。说白了就是这么回事——凭我的名誉起誓，”卡利斯先生收尾道，“这是我平生所闻最离奇的事情之一。眼下那桩事跟它一比，简直不算什么！”

===Original===

Lord Ellingham again inspected the legal countenances.

“I see nothing at all improbable about it,” he said. “We may as well face that fact at once. I will be here at three o’clock, Mr. Carless. I confess I should like to meet my cousin﻿—if she really is that!”

“Your Lordship takes it admirably!” exclaimed Mr. Carless. “But really﻿—well, I don’t know. However, we shall see. But, ’pon my honour, it’s most odd! One claimant disposed of, another, a more formidable one, comes on!”

“But we have not disposed of the first, have we?” suggested Lord Ellingham.

===Chinese===

埃灵厄姆勋爵又端详起那两张法律家的面孔。

“我看这事毫无难以置信之处，”他说。“我们不妨索性直面这个事实。三点钟我会到这儿来，卡利斯先生。我承认，我倒想见见我这位堂妹——倘若她真是的话！”

“大人您真是泰然得很！”卡利斯先生嚷道。“不过说真的——唉，我也说不好。反正，咱们走着瞧。可是，凭我的名誉起誓，这事情实在太蹊跷了！打发掉一个主张人，又来了一个，而且更难对付！”

“可我们还没打发掉头一个呢，不是吗？”埃灵厄姆勋爵提醒道。

===Original===

“I don’t anticipate any trouble in that quarter,” answered Mr. Carless. “As I said to those two who have just gone out﻿—send or bring the man here, and we’ll tell in one minute if he’s what he claims to be!”

“But﻿—how?” asked Lord Ellingham. “You seem very certain.”

“Dead certain!” asserted Mr. Carless. He looked round his callers and laughed. “I may as well tell you,” he said. “Portlethwaite drew me aside to remind me of it. The real Lord Marketstoke, if he were alive, could easily be identified. He lost a finger when a mere boy.”

“Ah!” exclaimed Mr. Pawle. “Good﻿—excellent! Best bit of evidence I’ve heard of. Hang this claimant! Now we can tell if Wickham really was Lord Marketstoke. If necessary, we can have his body exhumed and examined.”

“It was a shooting accident,” continued Mr. Carless. “He was out shooting in the park at Ellingham when a boy of fourteen or fifteen; he was using an old muzzle-loading gun; it burst, and he lost his second finger﻿—the right hand. It was, of course, very noticeable. Now, that small but very important fact is most likely not known to Methley and Woodlesford’s client﻿—but it’s known to Driver and to Portlethwaite and to me, and now to all of you. If this man comes here﻿—look at his right hand! If he possesses his full complement of fingers, well﻿—”

===Chinese===

“那一头我不担心会有什么麻烦，”卡利斯先生答道。“我方才对那两位——刚出去的那两位——就是这么说的：把那人送来，或者带到这儿来，一分钟之内我们就能断定，他究竟是不是他自称的那个人！”

“可是——怎么个断定法？”埃灵厄姆勋爵问。“您似乎十分有把握。”

“十拿九稳！”卡利斯先生言之凿凿。他环视满座来客，笑了。“不妨告诉你们吧，”他说。“波特尔思韦特（Portlethwaite）方才把我拉到一边提醒了我。真正的马克特斯托克勋爵若是还在人世，轻易就能验明正身。他还是个小娃娃的时候，就丢了一根手指。”

“啊！”波尔先生叫了起来。“好——好极了！这是我听说过的最有力的一条证据。让那个主张人见鬼去吧！这下我们能查明威克姆究竟是不是马克特斯托克勋爵了。必要时，还可以把他的遗体开棺检验。”

“那是一桩打猎出的意外，”卡利斯先生继续道。“他十四五岁上，在埃灵厄姆的庄园里打猎，使的是一支旧式前膛枪；枪炸了膛，他丢了右手的第二根手指。这事当然非常显眼。喏，这个虽小却极关紧要的事实，梅思利和伍德斯福德的那位当事人多半不知道——可德赖弗（Driver）知道，波特尔思韦特知道，我知道，如今连你们诸位也知道了。那人若是到这儿来——瞧他的右手！他若十指齐全，那么——”

===Original===

Mr. Carless ended with a significant grimace, and Mr. Pawle, nodding assent, returned to the question which he was putting when Lord Ellingham interrupted him.

“Now let us settle the point I raised,” he said. “Are we to tell Miss Wickham what my conclusions are, or are we to leave her in ignorance until we get proof that they are correct?”

“Or﻿—incorrect!” answered Mr. Carless with an admonitory laugh. “I should say﻿—at present, tell her nothing. Let us find out all we can from her; there are several questions I should like to ask her, myself, arising out of what you have told us. Leave all the rest until a later period. If your theory is correct, Pawle, it can be established, if it isn’t, the girl may as well be left in ignorance that you ever raised it.”

“Until three o’clock, then,” said Mr. Pawle.

===Chinese===

卡利斯先生意味深长地做了个鬼脸，就此打住；波尔先生点头称是，又回到方才被埃灵厄姆勋爵打断的那个问题上。

“现在来把我提的那个问题定下来吧，”他说。“我们是要把我的结论告诉威克姆小姐呢，还是让她蒙在鼓里，直到我们拿到证明这些结论属实的确据？”

“也说不定——是不属实的！”卡利斯先生答道，笑着告诫了一句。“依我说——眼下先什么也别告诉她。我们先尽量从她那儿把情况问出来；听了您方才这番话，我自己就有好几个问题想问她。其余的都留到日后再说。倘若您的理论是对的，波尔，它总归能坐实；倘若不对，这姑娘索性一直不知道您提出过这个理论，反倒更好。”

“那就三点钟见，”波尔先生说。

===Original===

Three o’clock found the old lawyer and Viner pacing the pavement of Lincoln’s Inn Fields in expectation of Miss Wickham’s arrival. She came at last in the taxicab which Mr. Pawle had sent for her, and her first words on stepping out of it were of surprise and inquiry.

“What is it, Mr. Pawle?” she demanded as she shook hands with her two squires. “More questions? What’s it all about?”

Mr. Pawle nudged Viner’s arm.

“My dear young lady,” he answered in grave and fatherly fashion, “you must bear in mind that a man’s life is in danger. We are doing all we can to clear that unfortunate young fellow Hyde of the dreadful charge which has been brought against him, and to do that we must get to know all we can about your late guardian, you know.”

===Chinese===

三点钟的时候，老律师和维纳已在林肯律师学院广场（Lincoln’s Inn Fields）的人行道上踱来踱去，等候威克姆小姐驾到。她终于坐着波尔先生派去接她的出租汽车来了，一下车，开口头一句便是又惊又问。

“这是怎么了，波尔先生？”她一面同她那两位护花使者握手，一面追问。“又有问题要问？这到底是怎么回事？”

波尔先生用胳膊肘轻轻碰了碰维纳。

“亲爱的姑娘，”他神色庄重、慈父一般地答道，“您务必记住，有条人命悬于一线。我们正在竭力洗清那个不幸的年轻人海德（Hyde）身上被控的可怕罪名，而要做到这一点，您知道，我们就必须尽量弄清您那位已故监护人的种种情形。”

===Original===

“I know so little about Mr. Ashton,” said Miss Wickham, looking apprehensively at the building towards which she was being conducted. “Where are you taking me?”

“To a solicitor’s office﻿—friends of mine,” answered Mr. Pawle. “Carless and Driver﻿—excellent people. Mr. Carless wants to ask you a few questions in the hope that your answers will give us a little more light on Ashton’s history. You needn’t be afraid of Carless,” he added as they began to climb the stairs. “Carless is quite a pleasant fellow﻿—and he has with him a very amiable young gentleman, Lord Ellingham, of whom you needn’t be afraid, either.”

“And why is Lord Ellingham, whoever he may be, there?” inquired Miss Wickham.

“Lord Ellingham is also interested in your late guardian,” replied Mr. Pawle. “In fact, we are all interested. So now, rub up your memory﻿—and answer Mr. Carless’ questions.”

===Chinese===

“我对阿什顿先生知之甚少，”威克姆小姐说，一面惴惴不安地望着人家正领她去的那座楼房。“你们这是要带我上哪儿去？”

“上一家律师事务所——都是我的朋友，”波尔先生答道。“卡利斯与德赖弗事务所（Carless and Driver）——人都极好。卡利斯先生想问您几个问题，指望您的回答能让我们对阿什顿的来历再多知道一点。您不用怕卡利斯，”他们开始上楼梯时，他又添了一句。“卡利斯是个顶和气的人——他身边还有位非常和蔼的年轻绅士，埃灵厄姆勋爵，您对他也尽可不必害怕。”

“那这位埃灵厄姆勋爵——不管他是什么人——为什么也在那儿？”威克姆小姐问道。

“埃灵厄姆勋爵对您那位已故监护人也很关心，”波尔先生答道。“其实，我们大家都关心。所以呢，您把记忆好好理一理——痛痛快快回答卡利斯先生的问题吧。”

===Original===

Viner remained in the background, quietly watching, while Mr. Pawle effected the necessary introductions. He was at once struck by what seemed to him an indisputable fact﻿—between Lord Ellingham and Miss Wickham there was an unmistakable family likeness. And he judged from the curious, scrutinizing look which Mr. Carless gave the two young people as they shook hands that the same idea struck him﻿—Mr. Carless wound up that look in a significant glance at Mr. Pawle, to whom he suddenly muttered a few words which Viner caught.

“By Jove!” he whispered. “I shouldn’t wonder if you’re right.”

Then he placed Miss Wickham in an easy-chair on his right hand, and cast a preliminary benevolent glance on her.

“Mr. Pawle,” he began, “has told us of your relationship with the late Mr. Ashton﻿—you always regarded him as your guardian?”

===Chinese===

维纳退在一旁，静静观望波尔先生把该有的引见一一办妥。他立刻被一个在他看来无可置疑的事实打动了——埃灵厄姆勋爵和威克姆小姐之间，有一种一望而知的家族相似。他又从卡利斯先生打量这两个年轻人握手时那既好奇又审视的目光看出，同样的念头也击中了他——卡利斯先生最后意味深长地朝波尔先生瞥了一眼，突然对他低声咕哝了几句，恰好叫维纳听了去。

“天哪！”他悄声道。“你要是说对了，我一点也不奇怪。”

随后他把威克姆小姐安置在自己右手边的一把安乐椅里，先朝她和颜悦色地打量了一番。

“波尔先生，”他开口道，“已经把您同已故的阿什顿先生的关系告诉我们了——您一向把他看作您的监护人，是吗？”

===Original===

“He was my guardian,” answered Miss Wickham. “My father left me in his charge.”

“Just so. Now, have you any recollection of your father?”

“Only very vague recollections. I was scarcely six, I think, when he died.”

“What do you remember about him?”

“I think he was a tall, handsome man﻿—I have some impression that he was. I think, too, that he had a fair complexion and hair. But it’s all very vague.”

===Chinese===

“他本来就是我的监护人，”威克姆小姐答道。“我父亲把我托付给了他。”

“正是如此。那么，您对自己的父亲还有记忆吗？”

“只有很模糊的记忆了。他去世的时候，我想我还不到六岁。”

“您还记得他些什么呢？”

“我想他是个高个子、相貌堂堂的男人——我隐约记得是这样。我还记得，他皮肤白皙，头发颜色也浅。可这一切都很模糊了。”

===Original===

“Do you remember where you lived?”

“Only that it was in a very big town﻿—Melbourne, of course. I have recollections of busy streets﻿—I remember, too, that when I left there it was very, very hot weather.”

“Do you remember Mr. Ashton at that time?”

“Oh, yes﻿—I remember Mr. Ashton. I had nobody else, you see; my mother had died when I was quite little; I have no recollection whatever of her. I remember Mr. Ashton’s house, and that he used to buy me lots of toys. His house was in a quiet part of the town, and he had a big, shady garden.”

“How long, so far as you remember, did you live with Mr. Ashton there?”

===Chinese===

“您记得你们当时住在哪儿吗？”

“只记得是在一座很大的城市里——当然是墨尔本（Melbourne）。我还记得那些熙熙攘攘的街道——还记得离开那儿的时候，天气非常非常热。”

“您记得那时候的阿什顿先生吗？”

“哦，记得——我记得阿什顿先生。您知道，我身边再没有别人了；我母亲在我很小的时候就去世了，我对她毫无印象。我记得阿什顿先生的房子，记得他常给我买许许多多玩具。他的房子在城里僻静的地段，还有一座浓荫匝地的大花园。”

“照您记忆所及，您在那儿跟阿什顿先生一起住了多久？”

===Original===

“Not very long, I think. He told me that I was to go to England, to school. For a little time before we sailed, I lived with Mrs. Roscombe, with whom I came to England. She was very kind to me; I was very fond of her.”

“And who was Mrs. Roscombe?”

“I didn’t know at the time, of course﻿—I only knew she was Mrs. Roscombe. But Mr. Ashton told me, not long before his death, who she was. She was the widow of some government official, and she was returning to England in consequence of his death. So she took charge of me and brought me over. She used to visit me regularly at school, every week, and I used to spend my holidays with her until she died.”

“Ah!” said Mr. Carless. “She is dead?”

“She died two years ago,” answered Miss Wickham.

===Chinese===

“我想不算久。他告诉我，我要到英国去上学。启航前有一小段日子，我住在罗斯科姆太太（Mrs. Roscombe）家里，来英国就是她带我来的。她待我非常好，我也很喜欢她。”

“那么，这位罗斯科姆太太是什么人？”

“当时我自然不知道——我只晓得她是罗斯科姆太太。可是就在阿什顿先生去世前不久，他把她的来历告诉了我。她是一位政府官员的遗孀，正因为丈夫死了，才要回英国去，便顺路照管我，把我带了过来。她从前每星期都定时到学校来看我，我的假期也一向是在她那儿度过的，直到她去世。”

“啊！”卡利斯先生说。“她去世了？”

“她两年前去世的，”威克姆小姐答道。

===Original===

“I wish she had been living,” observed Mr. Carless, with a glance at Mr. Pawle. “I should have liked to see Mrs. Roscombe. Well,” he continued, turning to Miss Wickham, “so Mrs. Roscombe brought you to England, to school. What school?”

“Ryedene School.”

“Ryedene! That’s one of the most expensive schools in England, isn’t it?”

“I don’t know. I﻿—perhaps it is.”

“I happen to know it is,” said Mr. Carless dryly. “Two of my clients have daughters there, now. I’ve seen their bills! Do you know who paid yours?”

===Chinese===

“她要是还活着就好了，”卡利斯先生说着，朝波尔先生望了一眼。“我本想见见这位罗斯科姆太太。好吧，”他转向威克姆小姐，接着说道，“这么说，罗斯科姆太太把您带到英国来上学。上的是哪所学校？”

“赖德恩学校（Ryedene School）。”

“赖德恩！那是全英国收费最昂贵的学校之一，不是吗？”

“我不知道。我——也许是的吧。”

“我恰好知道正是如此，”卡利斯先生干巴巴地说。“眼下我就有两个当事人的女儿在那儿念书。她们的账单我都见识过！您知道您自己的账单是谁付的吗？”

===Original===

“No,” she answered, “I don’t know. Mr. Ashton, I suppose.”

“You had everything you wanted, I dare say? Clothes, pocket-money, and so on?”

“I’ve always had everything I wanted,” replied Miss Wickham.

“And you were at Ryedene twelve years?”

“Except for the holidays﻿—yes.”

===Chinese===

“不知道，”她答道，“我不知道。我想是阿什顿先生吧。”

“想必您要什么有什么吧？衣裳啦，零花钱啦，诸如此类？”

“我要的东西向来样样不缺，”威克姆小姐答道。

“那么，您在赖德恩待了十二年？”

“除去假期——是的。”

===Original===

“You must be a very learned young lady,” suggested Mr. Carless.

Miss Wickham looked round the circle of attentive faces.

“I can play tennis and hockey very well,” she said, smiling a little. “And I wasn’t bad at cricket the last season or two﻿—we played cricket there. But I’m not up to much at anything else, except that I can talk French decently.”

“Physical culture, eh?” observed Mr. Carless, smiling. “Very well! Now, then, in the end Mr. Ashton came home to England, and of course came to see you, and in due course you left school, and came to his house in Markendale Square, where he got a Mrs. Killenhall to look after you. All that correct? Yes? Well, then, I think, from what Mr. Pawle tells me, Mr. Ashton handed over a lot of money to you, and told you it had been left to you, or left in his charge for you, by your father? That is correct too? Very well. Now, did Mr. Ashton never tell you anything much about your father?”

“No, he never did. Beyond telling me that my father was an Englishman who had gone out to Australia and settled there, he never told me anything. But,” here Miss Wickham paused and hesitated for a while, “I have an idea,” she continued in the end, “that he meant to tell me something﻿—what, I, of course, don’t know. He once or twice﻿—hinted that he would tell me something, some day.”

===Chinese===

“那您必定是位很有学问的小姐了，”卡利斯先生打趣道。

威克姆小姐环顾四周一张张专注的面孔。

“网球和曲棍球我打得很好，”她略带笑意地说。“这一两季板球我打得也不赖——我们在那儿是打板球的。可别的我就不怎么行了，只是法语还能说得像样。”

“体育锻炼，嗯？”卡利斯先生含笑说。“很好！那么，后来阿什顿先生回了英国，自然来看您；再往后您出了校门，住进他在马肯代尔广场（Markendale Square）的宅子，他还请了一位基伦霍尔太太（Mrs. Killenhall）来照管您。这些都属实吧？是吗？好，那么据波尔先生告诉我的，阿什顿先生交给您一大笔钱，还说这钱是您父亲留给您的，或是托他替您保管的？这也属实吧？很好。那么，阿什顿先生就从没跟您讲过什么有关您父亲的详情吗？”

“没有，他从没讲过。除了告诉我，我父亲是个去澳大利亚（Australia）定居的英国人之外，他什么也没跟我说过。只是——”说到这里，威克姆小姐顿住，迟疑了片刻。“我总觉得，”她最后接着说，“他是有一件事要告诉我的——什么事，我当然不知道。他有一两回——暗示过，说将来哪一天要告诉我一件事情。”

===Original===

“You didn’t press him?” suggested Mr. Carless.

“I don’t think I am naturally inquisitive,” replied Miss Wickham. “I certainly did not press him. I knew he’d tell me, whatever it was, in his own way.”

“One or two other questions,” said Mr. Carless. “Do you know who your mother was?”

“Only that she was someone whom my father met in Australia.”

“Do you know what her maiden name was?”

===Chinese===

“您没有追问他？”卡利斯先生提示道。

“我想我天生不是爱刨根问底的人，”威克姆小姐答道。“我确实没有追问过。我知道，不管那是件什么事，他总会照他自己的方式告诉我。”

“还有一两个问题，”卡利斯先生说。“您知道您母亲是什么人吗？”

“只知道她是父亲在澳大利亚认识的一个人。”

“您知道她娘家的姓氏吗？”

===Original===

“No, only her Christian name; that was Catherine. She and my father are buried together.”

“Ah!” exclaimed Mr. Carless. “That is something else I was going to ask. You know where they are buried?”

“Oh, yes! Because, before we sailed, Mrs. Roscombe took me to the churchyard, or cemetery, to see my father’s and mother’s grave. I remembered that perfectly. Her own husband was buried there too, close by. I remember how we both cried.”

Mr. Carless suddenly pointed to the ornament which Miss Wickham was wearing.

“Will you take that off, and let me look at it?” he asked. “Thank you,” he said, as she somewhat surprisedly obeyed. “I believe,” he continued, as he quietly passed the ornament to Lord Ellingham, “that Mr. Ashton gave you this and told you it had belonged to your father? Just so! Well,” he concluded, handing the ornament back, “I think that’s all. Much obliged to you, Miss Wickham. You won’t understand all this, but you will, later. Now, one of my clerks will get you a car, and we’ll escort you down to it.”

===Chinese===

“不知道，只知道她的教名，叫凯瑟琳（Catherine）。她和我父亲合葬在一处。”

“啊！”卡利斯先生叫道。“这本来也是我要问的。您知道他们葬在哪儿吗？”

“哦，知道！因为启航之前，罗斯科姆太太带我上过教堂墓地——还是公墓来着——去看过我父母的坟。这个我记得清清楚楚。她自己的丈夫也葬在那儿，就在近旁。我记得我们俩当时哭成了什么样。”

卡利斯先生忽然指向威克姆小姐身上佩戴着的那件饰物。

“劳驾把它取下来，让我看看好吗？”他问。她略带惊讶地依言摘了下来。“多谢，”他说。“我相信，”他一边不动声色地把那件饰物递给埃灵厄姆勋爵，一边继续说道，“这件东西是阿什顿先生给您的，他还告诉您，它原先属于您父亲？正是如此！好啦，”他把饰物递还给她，收尾道，“我想问的就这些了。多谢您，威克姆小姐。这一切您眼下不会明白，往后会明白的。现在，我的一名书记员去给您叫车，我们送您下去上车。”

===Original===

“No,” said Lord Ellingham, promptly jumping to his feet. “Allow me﻿—I’m youngest. If Miss Wickham will let me﻿—”

The two young people went out of the room together, and the three men left behind looked at each other. There was a brief and significant silence.

“Well, Carless?” said Mr. Pawle at last. “How now?”

“ ’Pon my honour,” answered Mr. Carless, “I shouldn’t wonder if you’re right!”

===Chinese===

“不，”埃灵厄姆勋爵说着，霍地站起身来。“让我来——我年纪最轻。要是威克姆小姐肯让我效劳——”

两个年轻人一道走出了房间，留下的三个人面面相觑。一时静默无言，意味深长。

“怎么样，卡利斯？”波尔先生终于开口。“如今怎么看？”

“凭我的名誉起誓，”卡利斯先生答道，“你要是说对了，我一点也不奇怪！”

## XX / 二十

===Original===

Surprising Readiness

Mr. Pawle made a gesture which seemed to denote a certain amount of triumphant self-satisfaction.

“I’m sure I’m right!” he exclaimed. “You’ll find out that I’m right! But there’s a tremendous lot to do, Carless. If only that unfortunate man, Ashton, had lived, he could have cleared this matter up at once. I feel convinced that he possessed papers which would have proved this girl’s claim beyond dispute. Those papers, of course﻿—”

“Now, what particular papers are you thinking of?” interrupted Mr. Carless.

“Well,” replied Mr. Pawle, “such papers as proofs of her father’s marriage, and of her own birth. According to what she told us just now, her father was married in Australia, and she herself was born there. There must be documentary proof of that.”

“Her father was probably married under his assumed name of Wickham,” observed Mr. Carless. “You’ll have to prove that Wickham and Lord Marketstoke were identical﻿—were one and the same person. The fact is, Pawle, if this girl’s claim is persisted in, there’ll have to be a very searching inquiry made in Australia. However much I may feel that your theory may be﻿—probably is﻿—right, I should have to advise my client, Lord Ellingham, to insist on the most complete investigation.”

===Chinese===

出人意料的爽快

波尔先生做了个手势，那样子透着几分得胜似的自满。

“我敢肯定我是对的！”他扬声道，“你们终会发现我是对的！不过要做的事情实在太多了，卡利斯。要是那位不幸的阿什顿还活着，他本可以立刻把这件事弄个水落石出。我深信他手上有一些文件，足以无可争议地证明这姑娘的继承主张。那些文件，当然——”

“那么，你具体想到的是哪些文件？”卡利斯先生插嘴道。

“唔，”波尔先生答道，“比如能证明她父亲婚姻、证明她本人出生的那类文件。照她方才对我们所说，她父亲是在澳大利亚结的婚，她本人也生在那里。此事必定有书面证据可查。”

“她父亲多半是用威克姆这个化名结的婚，”卡利斯先生指出，“那你就得证明威克姆与马克特斯托克勋爵本是同一个人——完完全全是一个人。实情是，波尔，倘若这姑娘坚持她的继承主张，就得在澳大利亚进行一番极为严密的调查。无论我私下多么觉得你的推测可能——多半就是——对的，我也必须建议我的委托人埃灵厄姆勋爵坚持做最彻底的查验。”

===Original===

“To be sure, to be sure!” assented Mr. Pawle. “That’s absolutely necessary. But my own impression is that as we get into the secret of Ashton’s murder, as I make no doubt we shall, there will be more evidence forthcoming. Now, as regards this man, whoever he is, who claims to be the missing Lord Marketstoke﻿—”

At that moment a clerk entered the room and glanced at Mr. Carless.

“Telephone message from Methley and Woodlesford, sir,” he announced. “Mr. Methley’s compliments, and if agreeable to you, he can bring his client on to see you this afternoon﻿—at once, if convenient.”

Mr. Carless looked at Mr. Pawle, and Mr. Pawle nodded a silent assent.

“Tell Mr. Methley it’s quite agreeable and convenient,” answered Mr. Carless. “I shall be glad to see them both﻿—at once. Um!” he muttered when the clerk had withdrawn. “Somewhat sudden, eh, Pawle? You might almost call it suspicious alacrity. Evidently the gentleman has no fear of meeting us!”

===Chinese===

“那是自然，那是自然！”波尔先生连声附和，“这绝对必要。不过我个人的感觉是，随着我们逐步揭开阿什顿遇害案的内情——我毫不怀疑我们一定办得到——还会有更多证据浮出水面。好了，至于那个自称是失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵的人，管他究竟是谁——”

正在这时，一名书记员走进房间，朝卡利斯先生望了一眼。

“梅思利与伍德斯福德事务所来电话，先生，”他禀报道，“梅思利先生向您致意，如果您同意，他可以带他的委托人今天下午过来见您——方便的话，马上就来。”

卡利斯先生看了看波尔先生，波尔先生默默点头表示同意。

“请告诉梅思利先生，我完全同意，也完全方便，”卡利斯先生答道，“我很乐意见他们两位——即刻便可见面。嗯！”书记员退出后，他咕哝道，“来得有点突然，是吧，波尔？简直可以说是爽快得可疑。显然，这位先生毫不怕见我们！”

===Original===

“You may be quite certain, Carless, if my theory about the whole thing is a sound theory, that the gentleman will have no fear of meeting anybody, not even a judge and jury!” answered Mr. Pawle sardonically. “If I apprehend things rightly, he’ll have been very carefully coached and prepared.”

“You think there’s a secret conspiracy behind all this?” suggested Mr. Carless. “With this claimant as cat’s-paw﻿—well tutored to his task?”

“I do!” affirmed Mr. Pawle. “Emphatically, I do!”

“Aye, well!” said Mr. Carless. “Don’t forget what I told you about the missing finger﻿—middle finger of the right hand. And I’ll have Driver in here, and Portlethwaite, too; we’ll see if he knows which is which of the three of us. I’ll go and prepare them.”

===Chinese===

“你尽管放心，卡利斯，倘若我对整件事的推测靠得住，那位先生就谁都不怕见，连法官和陪审团也不怕！”波尔先生讥诮地答道，“要是我对情势判断得不错，他早已被人仔仔细细地调教、预演过了。”

“你认为这一切背后藏着个秘密阴谋？”卡利斯先生试探道，“拿这个主张人当枪使——受足了调教来办这桩差事？”

“正是！”波尔先生断然道，“千真万确，我就是这么认为的！”

“唉，好吧！”卡利斯先生说，“别忘了我说过的那根断指——右手中指。我把德赖弗叫进来，波特尔思韦特也叫上；咱们倒要看看，他认不认得我们三人谁是谁。我去知会他们一声。”

===Original===

He returned presently with his partner, a quiet, elderly man; a few minutes later Portlethwaite, evidently keenly interested, joined them. They and Mr. Pawle began to discuss certain legal matters connected with the immediate business, and Viner purposely withdrew to a corner of the room, intent on silently watching whatever followed on the arrival of the visitors. A quarter of an hour later Methley was shown into the room, and the five men gathered there turned with one accord to look at his companion, a tall, fresh-coloured, slightly grey-haired man of distinctly high-bred appearance, who, Viner saw at once, was much more self-possessed and assured in manner than any of the men who rose to meet him.

“My client, Mr. Cave, who claims to be Earl of Ellingham,” said Methley, by way of introduction. “Mr. Car﻿—”

But the other man smiled quietly and immediately assumed a lead.

“There is no need of introduction, Mr. Methley,” he said. “I remember all three gentlemen perfectly! Mr. Carless﻿—Mr. Driver﻿—and﻿—yes, to be sure, Mr. Portlethwaite! I have a good memory for faces.” He bowed to each man as he named him, and smiled again. “Whether these gentlemen remember me as well as I remember them,” he remarked, “is another question!”

“May I offer you a chair?” said Mr. Carless.

The visitor bowed, sat down, and took off his gloves. And in the silence which followed, Viner saw that the eyes of Driver, Carless, Pawle and Portlethwaite were all steadily directed on the claimant’s right hand﻿—he himself turned to it, too, with no small interest. The next instant he was conscious that an atmosphere of astonishment and surprise had been set up in that room. For the middle finger of the man’s right hand was missing!

===Chinese===

他不多时便带着合伙人回来了，那是个沉静的上了年纪的人；几分钟后，显然兴致极高的波特尔思韦特也加入进来。几人同波尔先生开始商谈与眼前这桩事相关的法律问题，维纳则有意识地退到房间一角，打算默默静观来客到场后的一切情形。一刻钟后，梅思利被引进屋来，屋里聚集的五个人不约而同地转身打量他的同伴——那是个身材高大、面色红润、头发微微花白的男子，一望便知出身名门；维纳立刻看出，比起起身上前迎他的任何一位，此人都镇定自若得多、更有一副胸有成竹的气度。

“我的委托人凯夫先生，自称埃灵厄姆伯爵，”梅思利介绍道，“卡利斯先——”

但那位先生微微一笑，立即抢了话头。

“不必介绍了，梅思利先生，”他说，“这三位先生我记得清清楚楚！卡利斯先生——德赖弗先生——还有——啊，对了，波特尔思韦特先生！我对面孔的记性一向很好。”他一边报着名字，一边向每人颔首致意，随即又微微一笑。“至于这几位先生记不记得我，像我记得他们那样清楚，”他说道，“那就是另一回事了！”

“请上坐，好吗？”卡利斯先生说。

来客欠了欠身，坐下，脱下手套。在随之而来的沉默中，维纳看见德赖弗、卡利斯、波尔和波特尔思韦特的目光都目不转睛地落在主张人的右手上——他自己也怀着不小的兴趣朝那只手望去。下一刻他便察觉，屋里已经弥漫起一片惊愕诧异的气氛。因为这人的右手中指不见了！

===Original===

Viner felt, rather than saw, that the three solicitors and the elderly clerk were exchanging glances of amazement. And he fancied that Mr. Carless’ voice, which had sounded cold and noncommittal as he offered the visitor a seat, was somewhat uncertain when he turned to address him.

“You claim, sir, to be the Lord Marketstoke who disappeared so many years ago?” he asked, eyeing the claimant over.

“I claim to be exactly what I am, Mr. Carless,” answered the visitor with another ready and pleasant smile. “I hope your memory will come to your aid.”

“When a man has disappeared﻿—absolutely﻿—for something like thirty-five years,” remarked Mr. Carless, “those whom he has left behind may well be excused if their memories don’t readily respond to sudden demands. But I should like to ask you some questions? Did you see the advertisements which were issued, broadcast, at the time of the seventh Earl of Ellingham’s death?”

“Yes﻿—in several English and Colonial papers,” answered the claimant.

“Why did you not reply to them?”

===Chinese===

维纳与其说是看见，不如说是感觉到，三位律师和老书记员正在交换惊异的眼色。他还觉得，卡利斯先生让座时那副冷淡含混的嗓音，此刻转过身来对来客说话时，已有些拿不稳了。

“先生，你自称是多年前失踪的那位马克特斯托克勋爵？”他问道，一面上下打量着主张人。

“我自称的正是我本人，卡利斯先生，”来客答道，脸上又浮起那副应景而讨喜的微笑，“但愿您的记性能帮上您的忙。”

“一个人销声匿迹了——彻彻底底——差不多有三十五年，”卡利斯先生说道，“那么被他留在身后的人，记性一时应付不了突如其来的盘问，也情有可原。不过我想向你提几个问题。第七代埃灵厄姆伯爵去世时四处广为刊布的启事，你见到过吗？”

“见过——在好几家英国本土和殖民地的报纸上，”主张人答道。

“你为什么没有回应？”

===Original===

“At that time I still persevered in my intention of never again having anything to do with my old life. I had no desire﻿—at all﻿—to come forward and claim my rights. So I took no notice of your advertisements.”

“And since then﻿—of late, to be exact﻿—you have changed your mind?” suggested Mr. Carless dryly.

“To a certain extent only,” replied the visitor, whose calm assurance was evidently impressing the legal practitioners around him. “I have already told Mr. Methley and his partner, Mr. Woodlesford, that I have no desire to assume my title nor to require possession of the estates which are certainly mine. I have lived a free life too long to wish for﻿—what I should come in for if I established my claim. But I have a right to a share in the property which I quite willingly resign to my nephew﻿—”

“In plain language,” said Mr. Carless, “if you are paid a certain considerable sum of money, you will vanish again into the obscurity from whence you came? Am I right in that supposition?”

“I don’t like your terminology, Mr. Carless,” answered the visitor with a slight frown. “I have not lived in obscurity, and﻿—”

“If you are what you claim to be, sir, you are Earl of Ellingham,” said Mr. Carless firmly, “and I may as well tell you at once that if you prove to us that you are, your nephew, who now holds title and estates, will at once relinquish both. There will be no bargaining. It is all or nothing. Our client, whom we know as Earl of Ellingham, is not going to traffic. If you are what you claim to be, you are head of the family and must take your place.”

===Chinese===

“那时候我还执意抱着原来的打算，再也不跟旧日的生活发生任何瓜葛。我毫无意愿——丝毫没有——站出来主张我的权利。所以你们那几则启事，我根本没理会。”

“那么后来——确切说，是近来——你改了主意？”卡利斯先生干巴巴地提示道。

“只是在一定程度上而已，”来客答道，他那份从容笃定显然正镇住在场的法律界人士。“我已经告诉过梅思利先生和他的合伙人伍德斯福德先生，我无意领受爵位，也不想收回那些无疑属于我的产业。我自由自在的日子过得太久，不愿去希求——一旦主张成立便会落到我头上的那些东西。但财产中属于我的一份，我有权取得——这一份我甘愿让给我的侄儿——”

“说白了，”卡利斯先生说，“只要付给你一笔相当可观的数目，你就会重新隐回你出来的那个无名角落里去，对吗？我这样理解不错吧？”

“我不喜欢您的措辞，卡利斯先生，”来客答道，微微皱了皱眉，“我并没有生活在无名的角落里，况且——”

“先生，倘若你真是你所自称的那个人，你就是埃灵厄姆伯爵，”卡利斯先生斩钉截铁地说，“我不妨立刻告诉你：你若向我们证明了你真是，你那位如今兼有爵位和产业的侄儿，就得马上把两者一并交出。这里头没有讨价还价的余地。要就是全盘，要就是一场空。我们的委托人——我们所认定的埃灵厄姆伯爵——绝不做交易。你若真是你所自称的人，你便是一家之长，必须回到你的位子上。”

===Original===

“We could have told you that once for all, if you had come to us in the first instance,” remarked Mr. Driver. “Any other idea is out of the question. It seems to me most remarkable that such a notion as that which you suggest should ever enter your head, sir. If you are Earl of Ellingham, you are!”

“And that reminds me,” said Mr. Carless, “that there is another question I should like to ask. Why, knowing that we have been legal advisers to your family for several generations, did you not come straight to us, instead of going﻿—Mr. Methley, I’m sure, will pardon me﻿—to a firm of solicitors which, as far as I know, has never had any connection with it?”

“I thought it best to employ absolutely independent advice,” replied the visitor. “And I still think I was right. For example, you evidently do not admit my claim?”

“We certainly admit nothing, at present!” declared Mr. Carless with a laugh. “It would be absurd to expect it. The proofs which your solicitors showed us this morning are no proofs at all. That those papers belonged to the missing Lord Marketstoke there is no doubt, but your possession of them at present does not prove that you are Lord Marketstoke or Lord Ellingham. They may have been stolen!”

The claimant rose from his chair with a good deal of dignity. He glanced at Methley.

“I do not see that any good can come of this interview, Mr. Methley,” he remarked in quiet, level tones. “I am evidently to be treated as an impostor. In that case,”﻿—he bowed ceremoniously to the men gathered around Mr. Carless’ desk﻿—“I think it best to withdraw.”

===Chinese===

“你当初要是径直来找我们，我们本可以一劳永逸地把这话说死，”德赖弗先生说，“别的念头想都不用想。先生，你脑子里竟然装进这样一个念头，在我看来真是咄咄怪事。你若是埃灵厄姆伯爵，你就是埃灵厄姆伯爵！”

“这倒提醒了我，”卡利斯先生说，“还有一个问题想请教。你明知我们几代人以来一直是你家的法律顾问，为什么不径直来找我们，反倒去找——梅思利先生，相信他会原谅我这样说——一家据我所知同贵府素无往来的事务所？”

“我认为最好聘请绝对独立的顾问，”来客答道，“至今我仍认为我没有做错。比方说，你们显然并不承认我的主张？”

“我们目前当然什么都不承认！”卡利斯先生笑着声明道，“指望我们承认，未免荒唐。你的律师们今早给我们看的那些证明，根本算不上证明。那些文件属于失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵，这一点毫无疑问；但你眼下持有它们，并不能证明你就是马克特斯托克勋爵，或者埃灵厄姆伯爵。它们也可能是偷来的！”

主张人带着十足的尊严从椅子上站起身来。他瞥了梅思利一眼。

“梅思利先生，我看不出这场会面还有什么好处，”他以平静沉稳的口吻说道，“显然，你们是要把我当成骗子来对待了。既然如此，”——他向围在卡利斯先生书桌旁的众人规规矩矩地鞠了一躬——“我想我还是告退为好。”

===Original===

Therewith he walked out of the room; and Methley, after a quiet word with Carless, followed﻿—to be stopped in the corridor, for a second time that day, by Viner, who had hurried after him.

“I’m not going to express any opinion on what we’ve just heard,” whispered Viner, drawing Methley aside, “but in view of what I told you this morning, there’s something I want you to do for me.”

“Yes?” said Methley. “What?”

“That unlucky fellow Hyde, who is on remand, is to be brought before the magistrate tomorrow morning,” answered Viner. “Get him﻿—this claimant there, to attend the court as a spectator﻿—go with him! Use any argument you like, but get him there! I’ve a reason﻿—which I’ll explain later.”

“I’ll do my best,” promised Methley. “And I’ve an idea of what’s on your mind. You want to find out if Hyde can recognize him as the man whom he met at the Markendale Square end of Lonsdale Passage?”

“Well, that is my idea!” assented Viner. “So get him there.”

===Chinese===

说罢他便走出了房间；梅思利同卡利斯低声交谈了一句，也跟着出去——却在走廊里被维纳拦住，这一天里已是第二回，维纳是急急忙忙追出来的。

“对刚才听到的一切，我不打算发表任何意见，”维纳把梅思利拉到一旁，低声道，“但鉴于我今天早上对你说过的话，有件事想请你替我办。”

“哦？”梅思利说，“什么事？”

“那个倒霉的海德，眼下还押候审，明天上午就要被带去见治安法官了，”维纳答道，“你要设法让他——就是这位主张人——以旁观者的身份到庭——你陪他一起来！随便用什么理由都行，总之要把他弄到庭上去！我有我的道理——过后再向你解释。”

“我尽力去办，”梅思利应承道，“而且我大致猜到了你打的是什么主意。你想弄清楚，海德能不能认出他就是自己在朗斯代尔过道马肯代尔广场一端碰到的那个男人，对吧？”

“正是，我正是这个主意！”维纳表示同意，“所以，务必把他带到。”

===Original===

Methley nodded and turned away; then he turned back and pointed at Carless’ room.

“What do they really think in there?” he whispered. “Tell me﻿—between ourselves?”

“That he is an impostor, and that there’s a conspiracy,” replied Viner.

Methley nodded again, and Viner went back. The men whom he had left were talking excitedly.

“It was the only course to take!” Mr. Carless was declaring. “Uncompromising hostility! We could do no other. You saw﻿—quite well﻿—that he was all for money. I will engage that we could have settled with him for one half of what he asked. But﻿—who is he?”

===Chinese===

梅思利点了点头，转身要走；随即又回过身来，指了指卡利斯的那间屋子。

“他们屋里的人究竟怎么想？”他压低声音问，“告诉我——咱们私下说说？”

“他们认为他是个骗子，这背后有个阴谋，”维纳答道。

梅思利又点了点头，维纳便回身进屋。他先前离开的那几个人正谈得激动。

“只有这一条路可走！”卡利斯先生正慷慨陈词，“毫不妥协的敌对！我们别无选择。你们都看见了——看得清清楚楚——他满心只想着钱。我敢担保，他要的数目，我们出一半就能把他打发掉。可是——他究竟是谁？”

===Original===

“The middle finger of his right hand is gone!” said Mr. Pawle, who had been very quiet and thoughtful during the recent proceedings. “Remember that, Carless!”

“A most extraordinary coincidence!” exclaimed Mr. Carless excitedly. “I don’t care twopence what anybody says﻿—we all know that the most surprising coincidences do occur. Nothing but a coincidence! I assert﻿—what is it, Portlethwaite?”

The elderly clerk had been manifesting a strong desire to get in a word, and he now rapped his senior employer’s elbow.

“Mr. Carless,” he said earnestly, “you know that before I came to you, now nearly forty years ago, I was a medical student: you know, too, you and Mr. Driver, why I gave up medicine for the law. But﻿—I haven’t forgotten all of that I learned in the medical schools and the hospitals.”

“Well, Portlethwaite,” demanded Mr. Carless, “what is it? You’ve some idea?”

“Gentlemen,” answered the elderly clerk. “I was always particularly interested in anatomy in my medical student days. I’ve been looking attentively at what I could see of that man’s injured finger since he sat down at that desk. And I’ll lay all I have that he lost the two joints of that finger within the last three months! The scar over the stump had not long been healed. That’s a fact!”

===Chinese===

“他右手的中指没了！”波尔先生说，方才那一幕里他一直沉默而若有所思，“记住这一点，卡利斯！”

“真是天下少有的巧合！”卡利斯先生激动地叫道，“随别人怎么说，我半点不在乎——我们都知道，最惊人的巧合确确实实会发生。无非是巧合罢了！我敢断言——什么事，波特尔思韦特？”

老书记员早就显出一副急欲插话的样子，这时便伸手敲了敲自己这位资深雇主的胳膊肘。

“卡利斯先生，”他恳切地说，“您知道，将近四十年前我来您这里之前，是学医的：您和德赖弗先生也知道，我为什么弃医从法。可是——我在医科学校和医院里学来的那些东西，并没有全忘掉。”

“那么，波特尔思韦特，”卡利斯先生催问，“什么事？你有什么想法？”

“诸位先生，”老书记员答道，“我读医科时，对人体解剖学一向特别有兴趣。从他在那张书桌旁坐下起，我就一直在留心观察他那只伤指上能看出来的情形。我敢拿我的全部家当打赌，那根手指的两节是在最近三个月之内才失去的！断指残端上的疤，愈合还没多久。这是实情！”

===Original===

Mr. Carless looked round with a triumphant smile.

“There!” he exclaimed. “What did I tell you? Coincidence﻿—nothing but coincidence!”

But Portlethwaite shook his head.

“Why not say design, Mr. Carless?” he said meaningly. “Why not say design? If this man, or the people who are behind him, knew that the real Lord Marketstoke had a finger missing, what easier﻿—in view of the stake they’re playing for﻿—than to remove one of this man’s fingers? Design, sir, design. All part of the scheme!”

The elderly clerk’s listeners looked at each other.

===Chinese===

卡利斯先生环顾众人，脸上带着得胜的微笑。

“怎么样！”他叫道，“我怎么说来着？巧合——无非是巧合！”

波特尔思韦特却摇了摇头。

“为什么不说是蓄意安排呢，卡利斯先生？”他意味深长地说，“为什么不说是蓄意安排？倘若这个人，或者他背后的人，知道真正的马克特斯托克勋爵少了一根手指，那么——想想他们赌的是多大的输赢——把这人的一根手指除掉，岂不是再容易不过？蓄意安排，先生，蓄意安排。全是整个计划的一部分！”

听了老书记员这番话，众人面面相觑。

===Original===

“I’ll tell you what it is!” exclaimed Mr. Pawle with sudden emphasis. “The more we see and hear of this affair, the more I’m convinced that it is, as Portlethwaite says, a conspiracy. You know, that fellow who has just been here was distinctly taken aback when you, Carless, informed him that it was going to be a case of all or nothing. He﻿—or the folk behind him﻿—evidently expected that they’d be able to effect a money settlement. Now, I should say that the real reason of his somewhat hasty retirement was that he wanted to consult his principal or principals. Did you notice that he was not really affronted by your remark? Not he! His personal dignity wasn’t ruffled a bit. He was taken aback! He’s gone off to consult. Carless, you ought to have that man carefully shadowed, to see where and to whom he goes.”

“Good idea!” muttered Mr. Driver. “We might see to that.”

“I can put a splendid man on to him, at once, Mr. Carless,” remarked Portlethwaite. “If you could furnish me with his address﻿—”

“Methley and Woodlesford know it,” said Mr. Carless. “Um﻿—yes, that might be very useful. Ring Methley’s up, Portlethwaite, and ask if they would oblige us with the name of Mr. Cave’s hotel﻿—some residential hotel in Lancaster Gate, I believe.”

===Chinese===

“我来说句实在话！”波尔先生突然加重语气道，“这桩事我们见得听得越多，我越相信，正像波特尔思韦特说的，这是个阴谋。你们知道，方才来的那个家伙，听你卡利斯告诉他要就是全盘要就是一场空的时候，分明吃了一惊。他——或者他背后那伙人——显然原本指望能弄成一桩拿钱了结的交易。照我看，他之所以退得有些仓促，真正的原因就是他要去请示他的主使——一位也好，几位也好。你们注意到没有，你那句话其实并没有真正冒犯他？才不会呢！他的个人体面一点没受损伤。他是吃了一惊！他是跑出去请示去了。卡利斯，你该派人对那家伙严加盯梢，看看他去什么地方、见什么人。”

“好主意！”德赖弗先生咕哝道，“这事我们可以安排。”

“我可以马上派一个顶好的人去盯他，卡利斯先生，”波特尔思韦特说，“只要您能把他的地址告诉我——”

“梅思利和伍德斯福德知道地址，”卡利斯先生说，“嗯——不错，这或许大有用处。波特尔思韦特，给梅思利事务所打个电话，问问他们可否把凯夫先生下榻的旅馆告诉我们——我记得是兰开斯特门的哪家公寓旅馆。”

===Original===

Mr. Pawle and Viner went away, ruminating over the recent events, and walked to the old lawyer’s offices in Bedford Row. Mr. Pawle’s own particular clerk met them as they entered.

“There’s Mr. Roland Perkwite, of the Middle Temple, in your room, sir,” he said, addressing his master. “You may remember him, sir﻿—we’ve briefed him once or twice in some small cases. Mr. Perkwite wants to see you about this Ashton affair﻿—he says he’s something to tell you.”

Mr. Pawle looked at Viner and beckoned him to follow.

“Here a little, and there a little!” he whispered. “What are we going to hear this time?”

===Chinese===

波尔先生和维纳离开那里，一路回味着方才的种种事端，走到这位老律师位于贝德福德罗的事务所。两人进门时，波尔先生自己那位专随的书记员迎了上来。

“先生，罗兰·佩尔怀特先生（Roland Perkwite）正在您屋里等您，他是中殿律师学院（Middle Temple）的人，”他对主人说道，“您也许还记得他，先生——我们有过一两回小案子请他出过庭。佩尔怀特先生为阿什顿那桩事想见您——他说有话要告诉您。”

波尔先生看了看维纳，招手叫他跟上来。

“这儿一点，那儿一点！”他低声道，“这回咱们又要听到什么呢？”

## XXI / 二十一


===Original===

The Marseilles Meeting

The man who was waiting in Mr. Pawle’s room, and who rose from his chair with alacrity as the old lawyer entered with Viner at his heels, was an alert, sharp-eyed person of something under middle-age, whose clean-shaven countenance and general air immediately suggested the Law Courts. And he went straight to business before he had released the hand which Mr. Pawle extended to him.

“Your clerk has no doubt already told you what I came about, Mr. Pawle?” he said. “This Ashton affair.”

“Just so,” answered Mr. Pawle. “You know something about it? This gentleman is Mr. Richard Viner, who is interested in it﻿—considerably.”

“To be sure,” said the barrister. “One of the witnesses, of course. I read the whole thing up last night. I have been on the Continent﻿—the French Riviera, Italy, the Austrian Tyrol﻿—for some time, Mr. Pawle, and only returned to town yesterday. I saw something, in an English newspaper, in Paris, the other day, about this Ashton business, and as my clerk keeps the *Times* for me when I am absent, last night I read over the proceedings before the magistrate and before the coroner. And of course I saw your request for information about Ashton and his recent movements.”

“And you’ve some to give?” asked Mr. Pawle.


===Chinese===

马赛会晤

在波尔（Pawle）先生房里等候的那个人，见老律师带着维纳（Viner）走进门来，连忙从椅子上起身。他是个机警而目光锐利的人物，年岁未及中年，面庞刮得干干净净，通身的气派一望便知出自法律界。波尔先生伸手过去，他握着那只手还没放开，就开门见山说起正事来。

“您的书记员想必已经把我的来意告诉您了吧，波尔先生？”他说。“就是阿什顿（Ashton）这桩案子。”

“正是，”波尔先生答道。“您对这事知道些内情？这位是理查德·维纳（Richard Viner）先生，他对这件案子很关切——关切得很呢。”

“可不是，”这位大律师说。“自然是证人之一喽。昨天晚上我把这整桩事情翻来覆去读了一遍。我先前有一阵子人在欧陆——法国里维埃拉（the French Riviera）、意大利、奥地利蒂罗尔（the Austrian Tyrol）——好些日子了，波尔先生，昨天才回城。前几天在巴黎，我在一份英文报纸上看到关于阿什顿这件事的报道；我的书记员又替我保留着我不在期间的《泰晤士报》（Times），所以昨晚我把治安法官面前和验尸官面前的诉讼程序都过了一遍。当然，你们那则征求阿什顿及其近日行踪的消息的启事，我也见到了。”

“那么您有消息可以提供？”波尔先生问。


===Original===

“I have some to give,” assented Mr. Perkwite, as the three men sat down by Mr. Pawle’s desk. “Certainly﻿—and I should say it’s of considerable importance. The fact is I met Ashton at Marseilles, and spent the better part of the week in his company at the Hotel de Louvre there.”

“When was that?” asked Mr. Pawle.

“About three months ago,” replied the barrister. “I had gone straight to Marseilles from London; he had come there from Italy by way of Monte Carlo and Nice. We happened to get into conversation on the night of my arrival, and we afterwards spent most of our time together. And finding out that I was a barrister, he confided certain things to me and asked my advice.”

“Aye﻿—and on what, now?” enquired the old lawyer.

“It was the last night we were together,” replied Mr. Perkwite. “We had by that time become very friendly, and I had promised to renew our acquaintance on my return to London, where, Ashton told me, he intended to settle down for the rest of his life. Now on that last evening at Marseilles I had been telling him, after dinner, of some curious legal cases, and he suddenly remarked that he would like to tell me of a matter which might come within the law, and on which he should be glad of advice. He then asked me if I had ever heard of the strange disappearance of Lord Marketstoke, heir to the seventh Earl of Ellingham. I replied that I had at the time when application was made to the courts for leave to presume Lord Marketstoke’s death.

“Thereupon, pledging me to secrecy for the time being, Ashton went on to tell me that Lord Marketstoke was well known to him and that he alone knew all the facts of the matter, though a certain amount of them was known to another man, now living in London. He said that Marketstoke, after a final quarrel with his father, left England in such a fashion that no one could trace him, taking with him the fortune which he had inherited from his mother, and eventually settled in Australia, where he henceforth lived under the name of Wickham. According to Ashton, he and Marketstoke became friends, close friends, at a very early period of Marketstoke’s career in Australia, and the friendship deepened and existed until Marketstoke’s death some twelve or thirteen years ago. But Ashton never had the slightest notion of Marketstoke’s real identity until his friend’s last days. Then Marketstoke told him the plain truth; and the fact who he really was at the same time was confided to another man﻿—who, however, was not told all the details which were given to Ashton.


===Chinese===

“我确实有消息可提供，”佩尔怀特（Perkwite）先生应道，这时三人已在波尔先生的书桌旁落座。“确确实实——而且依我看，事关重大。事实是，我在马赛（Marseilles）遇上了阿什顿，并在那边的卢浮旅馆（Hotel de Louvre）与他做伴，度过了那一周的大半时光。”

“那是什么时候的事？”波尔先生问。

“大约三个月前，”大律师答道。“我是从伦敦径直去马赛的；他则从意大利过来，取道蒙特卡洛（Monte Carlo）和尼斯（Nice）。我抵达的那天晚上，我们碰巧攀谈起来，此后大部分时间都待在一处。他得知我是大律师，便向我吐露了几件事，向我讨主意。”

“哦——那么，是关于什么事呢？”老律师问道。

“那是我们在一起的最后一个晚上，”佩尔怀特先生答道。“到那时节我们已经十分要好，我也答应回伦敦后再续交情——阿什顿告诉我，他打算在伦敦安度余生。就在马赛的最后那个晚上，晚饭后我正给他讲几桩离奇的案子，他忽然说，他想告诉我一件或许牵涉法律的事，很乐意听听我的意见。接着他便问我，可曾听说过马克特斯托克勋爵（Lord Marketstoke）——第七代埃灵厄姆伯爵（the seventh Earl of Ellingham）的继承人——离奇失踪的旧事。我回答说，当年法院受理申请、要求推定马克特斯托克勋爵死亡的时候，我听说过。

“于是，阿什顿要我暂时守密，接着便告诉我：马克特斯托克勋爵与他相熟得很，而全部实情唯有他一人尽知，不过其中若干，另一个如今住在伦敦的人也有所了解。他说，马克特斯托克同父亲最后大吵一场之后离开英国，走得那样干净利落，谁也追索不到他的行迹；他带走了从母亲那里继承的财产，最终在澳大利亚落脚，从此顶着威克姆（Wickham）这个姓氏过活。据阿什顿说，早在马克特斯托克初到澳大利亚立业之时，两人便结了交情，而且是深交；这份情谊日久弥笃，一直延续到大约十二三年前马克特斯托克去世。但在朋友临终之前，阿什顿对马克特斯托克的真实身份从未有过丝毫觉察。直到那时，马克特斯托克才把原原本本的真相告诉了他；与此同时，他究竟是谁这一事实也吐露给了另一个人——不过，那人并未获知告诉阿什顿的全部细节。


===Original===

“Now, Marketstoke had married in Australia. His wife was dead. But he had a daughter who was about six years of age at the time of her father’s death. Marketstoke confided her to Ashton, with a wish that she should be sent home to England to be educated. He also handed over to Ashton a considerable sum of money for this child. Further, he gave him a quantity of papers, letters, family documents, and so on. He had a purpose. He left it to Ashton﻿—in whom he evidently had the most absolute confidence﻿—as to whether this girl’s claim to the title and estates should be set up. And when Ashton had finished telling me all this, I found that one of his principal reasons in coming to England to settle down, was the wish to find out how things were with the present holder of the title: if, he said, he discovered that he was a worthy sort of young fellow, he, Ashton, should be inclined to let the secret die with him. He told me that the girl already had some twelve thousand pounds of her own, and that it was his intention to leave her the whole of his own fortune, and as she was absolutely ignorant of her real position, he might perhaps leave her so. But in view of the possibility of his setting up her claim, he asked me some questions on legal points, and of course I asked him to let me see the papers of which he had spoken.”

“Ah!” exclaimed Mr. Pawle, with a sigh of relieved satisfaction. “Then you saw them?”

“Yes﻿—he showed me the whole lot,” replied Mr. Perkwite. “Not so many, after all﻿—those that were really pertinent, at any rate. He carried those in a pocketbook; had so carried them, he told me, ever since Marketstoke had handed them to him; they had never, he added, been out of his possession, day or night, since Marketstoke’s death. Now, on examining the papers, I at once discovered two highly important facts. Although Marketstoke went to and lived in Australia under the name of Wickham, he had taken good care to get married in his own proper name, and there, amongst the documents, was the marriage certificate, in which he was correctly described. Further, his daughter had been correctly designated in the register of her birth; there was a copy, properly attested, of the entry.”

Mr. Pawle glanced at Viner, and Viner knew what he was thinking of. The two documents just described by Mr. Perkwite had not been among the papers which Methley and Woodlesford had exhibited at Carless & Driver’s office.

“A moment,” said Mr. Pawle, lifting an arresting finger. “Did you happen to notice where this marriage took place?”

“It was not in Melbourne,” replied Mr. Perkwite.


===Chinese===

“再说，马克特斯托克在澳大利亚成了婚。妻子已经亡故，但留下一个女儿，父亲死时她大约六岁。马克特斯托克把她托付给阿什顿，希望送她回英国受教育；他还交给阿什顿一笔相当可观的钱，留给这个孩子。此外，他又交给他一批文件、信函、家族文书之类。他是有用意的：这位姑娘对爵位和产业的继承权究竟要不要提出来，全由阿什顿——他显然对阿什顿怀着绝对的信任——自行定夺。等阿什顿把这一切讲完，我这才明白，他来英国定居的主要动机之一，就是想摸清楚如今持有这个爵位的人是个什么情形：他说，倘若查明那是个品性端正的年轻人，他阿什顿便打算让这个秘密随他一起埋进黄土。他告诉我，这姑娘名下已有一万二千英镑上下的财产，而他有意把自己的全部家财都留给她；既然她对自身的真实身份全然无知，他大可以就这样让她一直蒙在鼓里。不过，考虑到将来或许要提出她的继承权，他问了我几个法律上的问题，我自然也请他把所说的那些文件拿给我看看。”

“啊！”波尔先生喊出声来，如释重负，满意地舒了口气。“那么您见到那些文件了？”

“见到了——他把全部都拿给我看了，”佩尔怀特先生答道。“其实为数不算多——至少真正切题的就是那么几件。他把那些东西放在一只皮夹子里随身携带；他告诉我，自马克特斯托克把它们交给他之日起，他便一直这样带着；他还说，自马克特斯托克去世以来，这些文件无论昼夜，从不曾离过他的身。我一检视那些文件，当即发现两桩至关重要的事实：马克特斯托克虽以威克姆之名前往并定居澳大利亚，成婚时却特意用了自己的本名，而那批文书里就有结婚证书，他的名姓在上面写得明明白白、分毫不差。再者，他女儿在出生登记册里也被正确地登了名；那里有一份经正式核证的那条登记记录的抄件。”

波尔先生瞥了维纳一眼，维纳也明白他在想什么。佩尔怀特先生刚才描述的那两份文件，并不在梅思利（Methley）与伍德斯福德（Woodlesford）在卡利斯与德赖弗事务所（Carless & Driver）出示过的那批文件之中。

“且慢，”波尔先生说着，竖起一根手指拦住话头。“您可曾留意，那场婚礼是在什么地方举行的？”

“不在墨尔本（Melbourne），”佩尔怀特先生答道。


===Original===

“My recollection is that it was at some place of a curious name. Ashton told me that Marketstoke’s wife had been a governess in the family of some well-to-do-sheep-farmer﻿—she was an English girl, and an orphan. The child, however, was certainly born in Melbourne and registered in Melbourne.”

“Now, that’s odd!” remarked Mr. Pawle. “You’d have thought that when Lord Marketstoke was so extensively advertised for some years ago, on the death of his father, some of these officials﻿—”

“Ah! I put that point to Ashton,” interrupted Mr. Perkwite. “He said that Marketstoke, though he had taken good care to be married in his own name and had exercised equal precaution about his daughter, had pledged everybody connected with his marriage and the child’s birth to secrecy.”

“Aye!” muttered Mr. Pawle. “He would do that, of course. But continue.”
