“Well,” said the barrister, “after seeing these papers, I had no doubt whatever that the case as presented by Ashton was quite clear, and that his ward Miss Avice Wickham is without doubt Countess of Ellingham (the title, I understand, going in the female as well as the male line) and rightful owner of the estates. And I told him that his best plan, on reaching England, was to put the whole matter before the family solicitors. However, he said that before doing that, there were two things he wanted to do. One was to find out for himself how things were﻿—if the young earl was a satisfactory landlord and so on, and likely to be a credit to the family; the other was that he wanted to consult the man who shared with him the bare knowledge that the man who had been known in Melbourne as Wickham was really the missing Lord Marketstoke. And he added that he had already telegraphed to this man to meet him in Paris.”

“Ah!” exclaimed Mr. Pawle with a look in Viner’s direction. “Now we are indeed coming to something! He was to meet him in Paris! Viner, I’ll wager the world against a China orange that that’s the man whom Armitstead saw in company with Ashton in the Rue Royale, and﻿—no doubt﻿—the man of Lonsdale Passage! Mr. Perkwite, this is most important. Did Ashton tell you the name of this man?”


===Chinese===

“我记得是在一个名字颇为古怪的地方。阿什顿告诉我，马克特斯托克的妻子先前在一家殷实的牧羊场主府上当家庭教师——她是个英国姑娘，又是个孤儿。不过那孩子肯定生在墨尔本，也在墨尔本登记。”

“嘿，这可就怪了！”波尔先生说。“几年前老伯爵去世，为寻找马克特斯托克勋爵登了那么多寻人启事，您本会以为，这些官员里总该有人——”

“啊！这一点我也向阿什顿提过，”佩尔怀特先生插话道。“他说，马克特斯托克虽然极其周全地用本名结婚，对女儿也做了同等的防范，却让所有同他的婚事和孩子的出生沾边的人都立誓保密。”

“是喽！”波尔先生咕哝道。“这种事他自然要办得滴水不漏。您接着讲。”

“这个嘛，”大律师说，“看过这些文件之后，我毫不怀疑：照阿什顿陈述的情形，此案一清二楚；他的被监护人艾维斯·威克姆小姐（Miss Avice Wickham）毫无疑问是埃灵厄姆伯爵夫人（据我所知，这一爵位女系与男系均可承袭），也是产业的正当主人。我告诉他，抵达英国之后，最稳妥的办法是把整桩事情摆到家族律师面前。可是他说，在那之前，有两件事他想先办：一件是亲自去探探底细——看看那位年轻伯爵是不是个像样的地主等等，日后能否为家族增光；另一件是他要去同一个人商量，那人同他一样，单单知道一件事实：墨尔本那位名叫威克姆的人，其实就是失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵。他还说，他已经拍电报约那人来巴黎会面了。”

“啊！”波尔先生喊道，朝维纳那边递了个眼色。“这下子可真摸到门道了！他要同那人在巴黎会面！维纳，我敢拿整个世界跟一只中国柑橘打赌，那人准是阿米特斯特德（Armitstead）在王家街（Rue Royale）看见同阿什顿走在一起的那个人，而且——十拿九稳——就是朗斯代尔过道（Lonsdale Passage）里的那个人！佩尔怀特先生，这至关重要。阿什顿向您说过那人的姓名吗？”


===Original===

The old lawyer was tremulous with excited interest, and Mr. Perkwite was obviously sorry to disappoint him.

“Unfortunately, he did not!” he replied. “He merely told me that he was a man who had lived in Melbourne for some time and had known Marketstoke and himself very intimately﻿—had left Melbourne just after Marketstoke’s death, and had settled in London. No, he did not mention his name.”

“Disappointing!” muttered Mr. Pawle. “That’s the nearest approach to a clue that we’ve had, Perkwite. If we only knew who that man was! But﻿—what more can you tell us?”

“Nothing more, I’m afraid,” answered the barrister. “I promised to call on Ashton when I returned to London, and when he’d started housekeeping, and we parted﻿—I went on next morning to Genoa, and he set off for Paris. He was a pleasant, kindly, sociable fellow,” concluded Mr. Perkwite, “and I was much grieved to hear of his sad fate.”

“He didn’t correspond with you at all after you left him at Marseilles?” asked Mr. Pawle.

“No,” replied the barrister. “No﻿—I never heard of or from him until I read of his murder.”


===Chinese===

老律师激动得浑身发颤，而佩尔怀特先生显然很过意不去，要让他失望了。

“不巧，没有！”他答道。“他只告诉我，那人在墨尔本住过一段时间，同马克特斯托克和他本人都过从甚密——马克特斯托克死后不久便离开了墨尔本，到伦敦定居。没有，他没有提过那人的名字。”

“真叫人泄气！”波尔先生咕哝道。“这是到目前为止我们所能得到的最接近线索的东西了，佩尔怀特。要是我们知道那人是谁就好了！不过——您还能告诉我们些什么？”

“恐怕没有别的了，”大律师答道。“我答应过，等回伦敦、他安顿好家宅之后，就去拜访他，随后我们便分了手——第二天早晨我继续去了热那亚（Genoa），他则动身去巴黎。他是个和气、亲切、爱交游的人，”佩尔怀特先生收尾道，“听到他的惨死，我心里非常难过。”

“您同他在马赛分手之后，他完全不曾与您通过书信？”波尔先生问。

“没有，”大律师答道。“没有——直到读到他遇害的消息之前，我从没听人说起过他，也没收到过他的只字。”


===Original===

Pawle turned to Viner.

“I think we’d better tell Perkwite of all that’s happened, within our own ken,” he said, and proceeded to give the visitor a brief account of the various important details. “Now,” he concluded, “it seems to me there’s only one conclusion to be arrived at. The man who shared the secret with Ashton is certainly the man whom Armitstead saw with him in Paris. He is probably the man whom Hyde saw leaving Londsdale Passage, just before Hyde found the body. And he is without doubt the murderer, and is the man to whom this claimant fellow is acting as cat’s-paw. And﻿—who is he?”

“There must be some way of finding that out,” observed Mr. Perkwite. “If your theory is correct, that this claimant is merely a man who is being put forward, then surely the thing to do is to get at the person or persons behind him, through him!”

“Aye, there’s that to be thought of,” asserted Mr. Pawle. “But it may be a tougher job than we think for. It would have been a tremendous help if Ashton had only mentioned a name to you.”

“Sorry, but he didn’t,” said Mr. Perkwite. “You feel,” he continued after a moment’s silence, “you feel that this affair of the Ellingham succession lies at the root of the Ashton mystery﻿—that he was really murdered by somebody who wanted to get possession of those papers?”


===Chinese===

波尔转向维纳。

“我想，我们不妨把这桩案子在我们视野之内的全部进展都告诉佩尔怀特，”他说，随即向来客简要叙述了各项重要的细节。“那么，”他末了说道，“依我看，结论只有一个。同阿什顿共享这个秘密的人，肯定就是阿米特斯特德在巴黎看见与他同行的那位；多半也是海德（Hyde）发现尸体之前一刻看见走出朗斯代尔过道的那位。他毫无疑问就是凶手，而那个自称‘主张人’（the claimant）的家伙，正是给他当傀儡。可是——他究竟是谁呢？”

“总会有办法查出来的，”佩尔怀特先生说。“倘若您的推断不错，这个主张人不过是个被推到台前的人，那么当务之急，自然就是通过他，揪出他背后的一个人或几个人！”

“是呀，这一层值得考虑，”波尔先生断言道。“可这桩差事没准比我们想的要难办。当初阿什顿哪怕只向您提过一个名字，也等于帮了我们天大的忙。”

“抱歉，他没提过，”佩尔怀特先生说。沉默片刻之后，他又接着说：“您是觉得，埃灵厄姆爵位继承这桩纠纷才是阿什顿疑案的根子——觉得他其实是被某个想夺取那些文件的人谋杀的？”


===Original===

“And to remain sole repository of the secret,” declared Mr. Pawle. “Isn’t it established that beyond yourself and this unknown man nobody but Ashton knew the secret?”

“There is another matter, though,” remarked Viner. He turned to the visitor. “You said that you and Ashton became very friendly and confidential during your stay in Marseilles. Pray, did he never show you anything of a valuable nature which he carried in his pocketbook?”

The barrister’s keen eyes suddenly lighted up with recollection.

“Yes!” he exclaimed. “Now you come to suggest it, he did! A diamond!”

“Ah!” said Mr. Pawle. “So you saw that!”


===Chinese===

“还要让这个秘密只剩他一个保管人，”波尔先生宣称。“如今难道不是已经查明：除了您本人和这个不知名的人，知道内情的只有阿什顿一个？”

“不过还有一桩事，”维纳说。他转向来客。“您方才说，您同阿什顿在马赛逗留期间十分要好、无话不谈。请问，他可曾给您看过他那只皮夹子里携带的什么值钱东西？”

大律师那双锐利的眼睛忽然因记起了什么而亮起来。

“看过的！”他失声道。“经您这么一提——他确实给看过！一颗钻石！”

“啊！”波尔先生说。“原来您也见过那东西！”


===Original===

“Yes, I saw it,” assented Mr. Perkwite. “He showed it to me as a sort of curiosity﻿—a stone which had some romantic history attaching to it. But I was not half as much interested in that as in the other affair.”

“All the same,” remarked Mr. Pawle, “that diamond is worth some fifty or sixty thousand pounds, Perkwite﻿—and it’s missing!”

Mr. Perkwite looked his astonishment.

“You mean﻿—he had it on him when he was murdered?” he asked.

“So it’s believed,” replied Mr. Pawle.


===Chinese===

“是的，我见过，”佩尔怀特先生应道。“他把它当件稀罕物拿给我看——一颗带着几分传奇来历的宝石。不过我对它的兴趣，还抵不上对另一件事的一半。”

“话虽如此，”波尔先生说，“那颗钻石可值五六万英镑呢，佩尔怀特——而它如今下落不明！”

佩尔怀特先生满面惊愕之色。

“您是说——他遇害的时候，那钻石就在他身上？”他问。

“据信如此，”波尔先生答道。


===Original===

“In that case it might form a clue,” said the barrister.

“When it’s heard of,” admitted Mr. Pawle, with a grim smile. “Not till then!”

“From what we have heard,” remarked Viner, “Ashton carried that diamond in the pocketbook which contained his papers﻿—the papers you have told me of, and some of which have certainly come into possession of this claimant person. Now, whoever stole the papers, of course got the diamond.”

Mr. Perkwite seemed to consider matters during a moment’s silence; finally he turned to the old lawyer.

“I have been thinking over something that might be done,” he said. “I see that the coroner’s inquest was adjourned. Now, as that inquest is, of course, being held to inquire into the circumstances of Ashton’s death, I suggest that I should come forward as a witness and should prove that Ashton showed certain papers relating to the Ellingham peerage to me at Marseilles; I can tell the story, as a witness. It can then be proved by you, or by Carless, that a man claiming to be the missing Lord Marketstoke showed these stolen papers to you. In the meantime, get the coroner to summon this man as a witness, and take care that he’s brought to the court. Once there, let him be asked how he came into possession of these papers? Do you see my idea?”


===Chinese===

“那样的话，它倒可能构成一条线索，”大律师说。

“等有了它的音讯再说吧，”波尔先生板着脸笑了笑，承认道。“在那之前，谈不上！”

“照我们听到的这些情形，”维纳说，“阿什顿那颗钻石就放在装文件的那只皮夹子里——就是您说的那些文件，其中若干肯定已经落到了这个主张人手里。那么，偷了文件的人，自然也把钻石弄到了手。”

佩尔怀特先生似乎在片刻的沉默里掂量着这些事；末了，他转向老律师。

“我一直在盘算一件可以着手的事，”他说。“我注意到验尸官的死因研讯已经延期。既然这次研讯的目的当然是要查明阿什顿死亡的种种情节，我建议由我出面充当证人，证明阿什顿在马赛给我看过若干与埃灵厄姆爵位有关的文件；作为证人，我可以把这段经过讲出来。随后再由您，或者由卡利斯（Carless），证明一个自称失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵的人，向你们出示过这批失窃的文件。与此同时，请验尸官传唤此人到庭作证，务必把他带到法庭。他一到庭，就问他这些文件是怎么落到他手里的！您明白我的意思吗？”


===Original===

“Capital!” exclaimed Mr. Pawle. “An excellent notion! Much obliged to you, Perkwite. It shall be done﻿—I’ll see to it at once. Yes, to be sure, that will put this fellow in a tight corner.”

“Don’t be surprised if he hasn’t some very clever explanation to give,” said the barrister warningly. “The whole thing is evidently a well-concocted conspiracy. But when is the adjourned inquest?”

“Day after tomorrow,” replied Mr. Pawle, after glancing at his desk-diary.

“And tomorrow morning,” remarked Viner, “Hyde comes up before the magistrate again, on remand.”

He was half-minded to tell Mr. Pawle there and then of his secret dealings with Methley that day, but on reflection he decided that he would keep the matter to himself. Viner had an idea which he had not communicated even to Methley. It had struck him that the mysterious deux ex machina who was certainly at the back of all this business might not improbably be so anxious about his schemes that he would, unknown and unsuspected, attend the magistrates’ court. Would Hyde, his wits sharpened by danger, be able to spot him as the muffled man of Lonsdale Passage?



===Chinese===

“妙极！”波尔先生喊道。“好主意！多谢您了，佩尔怀特。这事一定照办——我马上去安排。是啊，这准能把那家伙逼得走投无路。”

“不过他要是有了一套非常圆滑的说辞，您也不必吃惊，”大律师告诫道。“这整件事明摆着是一场精心炮制的阴谋。话说回来，延期后的研讯定在哪天？”

“后天，”波尔先生瞥了一眼案头的日程簿，答道。

“还有明天上午，”维纳说，“还押中的海德将再度被提堂，到治安法官面前受审。”

他一时想当场就把那天与梅思利秘密接洽的事告诉波尔先生，但转念一想，还是决定把这件事闷在自己心里。维纳有一个主意，连梅思利都不曾告知。他忽然想到：那位必然藏在这整桩事情背后的神秘莫测的“解围之神”，未必不会因为太放心不下自己的种种计谋，而隐姓埋名、无人起疑地亲临治安法庭。届时海德凭着危险磨砺出来的机警，能不能把他认出来，认出他就是朗斯代尔过道里那个围围巾的男人呢？

## XXII / 二十二

===Original===

On Remand

When Langton Hyde was brought up before the magistrate next morning, the court was crowded to its utmost limits; and Viner, looking round him from his seat near the solicitors’ table saw that most of the people interested in the case were present. Mr. Carless was whispering with Mr. Pawle; Lord Ellingham had a seat close by; in the front of the public gallery Miss Penkridge, grim and alert, was in charge of the timid and shrinking sisters of the unfortunate prisoner. There, too, were Mr. Armitstead and Mr. Isidore Rosenbaum, and Mr. Perkwite, all evidently very much alive to certain possibilities. But Viner looked in vain for either Methley or Woodlesford or their mysterious client; they were certainly not present when Hyde was put into the dock, and Viner began to wonder if the events of the previous day had warned Mr. Cave and those behind him to avoid publicity.

Instructed by Viner, who was determined to spare neither effort nor money to clear his old schoolmate, Felpham had engaged the services of one of the most brilliant criminal barristers of the day, Mr. Millington-Bywater, on behalf of his client; and he and Viner had sat up half the night with him, instructing him in the various mysteries and ramifications of the case. A big, heavy-faced, shrewd-eyed man, Mr. Millington-Bywater made no sign, and to all outward appearance showed no very great interest while the counsel who now appeared on behalf of the police, completed his case against the prisoner.

The only new evidence produced by the prosecution was that of the greengrocer on whose premises Hyde had admitted that he passed most of the night of the murder, and in whose shed the missing valuables had been found. The greengrocer’s evidence as to his discovery was given in a plain and straightforward fashion﻿—he was evidently a man who would just tell what he actually saw, and brought neither fancy nor imagination to bear on his observation. But when the prosecution had done with him, Mr. Millington-Bywater rose and quietly asked the police to produce the watch, chain and ring which the greengrocer had found, in their original wrappings. He held up the wrapping-papers to the witness and asked him if he could swear that this was what he had found the valuables in and had given to the police. The greengrocer was positive as to this; he was positive, too, that the other wrappings which Felpham had carefully preserved were those which had been on the outside of the parcel and had been thrown aside by himself on its discovery and afterwards picked up by Viner. Mr. Millington-Bywater handed all these papers up to the magistrate, directing his attention to the strong odour of drugs or chemicals which still pervaded them, and to the address of the manufacturing chemists which appeared on the outer wrapping. The magistrate seemed somewhat mystified.

“What is the object of this?” he asked, glancing at the defending counsel. “It is admitted that these are the wrappings in which the watch, and chain and ring were found in the witness’s shed, but”﻿—he paused, with another inquiring look﻿—“you propose to﻿—what?” he asked.

“I propose, Your Worship, to prove that these things were never put there by the prisoner at all!” answered Mr. Millington-Bywater, promptly and with an assurance which was not lost on the spectators. “I intend to show that they were purposely placed in that outhouse by the real murderer of John Ashton after the statement made by the prisoner at the inquest became public﻿—placed there, of course, to divert any possible suspicion of himself.

===Chinese===

还押候审

第二天上午，兰顿·海德被押到治安法官面前提堂，法庭里挤得水泄不通；维纳从律师席旁自己的座位上环顾四周，看见与此案有关的人士大多到场。卡利斯先生正与波尔先生低声耳语；埃灵厄姆勋爵的座位就在近旁；旁听席前排，彭克里奇小姐面色冷峻、神色警觉，照看着那位不幸被告两个怯懦畏缩的姐妹。在场的还有阿米特斯特德先生、伊西多·罗森鲍姆先生（Mr. Isidore Rosenbaum）和佩尔怀特先生，个个显然都对某些可能的变数极为敏感。可是维纳找遍了也没有见到梅思利、伍德斯福德或他们那位神秘的委托人，任何一位都不见踪影；海德被押进被告席时他们确实不在场，维纳不禁开始怀疑，前一天发生的事是否已让凯夫先生和他背后的那些人有所警觉，避开了这等抛头露面的场合。

维纳下定决心，不惜气力、不惜钱财，也要洗清老同学的冤屈；费尔法姆奉其嘱托，已为当事人延聘了当今最出色的刑事大律师之一——米林顿-拜沃特先生（Mr. Millington-Bywater）；随后他与维纳又陪这位大律师熬了半夜，把此案的重重疑团与种种枝节向他一一讲明。米林顿-拜沃特先生身材魁伟，面孔厚重，目光锐利；此刻，代表警方出面的律师正在完结其对被告的指控，他却始终不动声色，从外表上看不出有多大的兴致。

控方拿出的唯一新证据，来自那个蔬果商——海德已承认命案当晚大部分时间是在他的店里度过的，失踪的贵重财物也正是在他的棚屋里找到的。蔬果商就自己发现财物的经过所作的证词，讲得平实直白——他显然是个有一说一的人，叙述所见时不掺半点虚想与臆测。可是等控方问完了他，米林顿-拜沃特先生便起身，从容地请警方出示蔬果商找到的那块怀表、表链和戒指，并须保持原有的包装。他把包装纸举到证人面前，问他能否起誓：这确是他发现财物时包裹着它们、并交给警方的那些纸。对此蔬果商一口咬定；他还同样一口咬定，费尔法姆细心保存起来的另几张包装纸，正是当时裹在包裹最外层、被他本人发现时随手丢在一旁、后来又由维纳捡起的那些。米林顿-拜沃特先生把这些纸张全部呈递给治安法官，提请他留意纸上至今仍弥漫的浓烈药品或化学药剂气味，并留意外层包装上印着的那家制药商行的地址。治安法官看来有些摸不着头脑。

“这么做到底是何用意？”他问，目光扫向辩护律师，“大家已经公认，这些正是怀表、表链和戒指在证人那间棚屋里被发现时的包装纸，可是——”他顿了顿，又投来一道探询的目光，“你打算——做什么？”

“庭上，我打算证明，这些东西根本就不是被告放进去的！”米林顿-拜沃特先生应声答道，干脆利落，那份成竹在胸的笃定，旁听席上人人看在眼里，“我要指出：它们是约翰·阿什顿案的真凶，在被告于死因研讯所作供述公之于众之后，蓄意放进那间棚屋的——放在那里，当然是为了把任何可能落到自己头上的嫌疑引开。

===Original===

“And now,” he continued, after the greengrocer had left the box and the prosecuting counsel had intimated that he had no more evidence to bring forward at present, “now I will outline the defence which I shall set up on behalf of my client. I intend to prove that John Ashton was murdered by some man not yet discovered, who killed him in order to gain possession of certain papers which he carried on him﻿—papers of extreme importance, as will be shown. We know where certain of those papers are, and we hope before very long to know where the rest are, and also where a certain very valuable diamond is, which the murdered man had on him at the time of his death. I shall, indeed, prove that the prisoner﻿—certainly through his own foolishness﻿—is wrongly accused. It will be within your worship’s recollection that when the prisoner was first before you, he very unwisely refused to give his name and address or any information﻿—he subsequently repented of that and made a statement, not only to the police but before the coroner. Now, I propose to put him into that box so that he may give evidence, and I shall then call certain witnesses who will offer evidence which will go to prove that what I say as regards the murder of Ashton is more than probable﻿—namely, that he was murdered for the sake of the documents he had on him, and that the spoiling of his money and valuables was a mere piece of bluff, intended to mislead. Let the prisoner go into the box!”

There was a continued deep silence in court while Hyde, under examination, repeated the story which he had told to Viner and Drillford and before the coroner and his jury. It was a plain, consecutive story, in which he set forth the circumstances preceding the evening of the murder and confessed his picking up of the ring which lay on the pavement by Ashton’s body. He kept his eyes steadily fixed on Mr. Millington-Bywater under this examination, never removing them from him save when the magistrate interposed with an occasional remark or question. But at one point a slight commotion in court caused him to look among the spectators, and Viner, following the direction of his eyes, saw him start, and at the same instant saw what it was that he started at. Methley, followed by the claimant, was quietly pushing a way through the throng between the door and the solicitor’s table.

Viner leaned closer to Mr. Pawle.

“Do you see?” he whispered. “Hyde evidently recognizes one of those two! Now﻿—which?”

Mr. Pawle glanced at the prisoner. Hyde’s face, hitherto pale, had flushed a little, and his eyes had grown bright; he looked as if he had suddenly seen a friend’s face in a hostile crowd. But Mr. Millington-Bywater, who had been bending over his papers, suddenly looked up with another question, and Hyde again turned his attention to him.

“All that you really know of this matter,” asked Mr. Millington-Bywater, “is that you chanced to turn up Lonsdale Passage, saw a man lying on the pavement and a ring close by, and that, being literally starving and desperate, you snatched up that ring and ran away as fast as you could?”

===Chinese===

“现在，”待蔬果商退下证人席、控方律师示意眼下再无证据可提之后，他接着说道，“现在我来概略谈谈我将为我的当事人提出的辩护。我要证明，约翰·阿什顿是被某个尚未查获的人杀害的，此人行凶，为的是夺取他随身携带的某些文件——那些文件极端重要，日后自会见分晓。其中几份文件现在何处，我们已有所知；其余的在何处，还有那颗极其贵重的钻石在何处——死者遇害时它正带在他身上——我们希望不久之后也能查明。而且我确实要证明，被告——固然是由于他自己的愚行——是受了诬枉的指控。庭上想必记得，被告初次到您面前时，极不明智地拒绝交代自己的姓名住址，拒绝提供任何情况——他后来为此后悔了，不但向警方作了供述，还在验尸官面前也作了供述。现在，我打算让他进入证人席作证，然后我将传唤若干证人，他们提出的证据将足以证明，我关于阿什顿遇害一案的论断绝非仅止于可能——即：他是为了身上的文件才遭杀害的，他那笔钱财财物之遭劫，不过是虚晃一枪，意在惑人耳目。请让被告入证人席！”

海德在讯问之下，把他对维纳和德里尔福德讲过、又在验尸官及其陪审团面前讲过的那段经历重述了一遍，法庭里始终一片深沉的寂静。那是一段平实连贯的陈述，他交代了命案当晚之前的种种情由，也坦白承认自己捡起了躺在阿什顿尸体旁人行道上的那枚戒指。受讯时他目不转睛地盯着米林顿-拜沃特先生，除了治安法官偶尔插话或提问之外，视线片刻不离对方。可是在某个当口，庭内一阵轻微的骚动引得他朝旁听席望去，维纳顺着他的目光看去，只见他猛然一怔，同时也看清了令他发怔的东西：梅思利在前，主张人随后，正悄然从门口向律师席这边的人丛里挤过来。

维纳朝波尔先生俯过身去。

“看见了吗？”他悄声道，“海德显然认出了那两个人中的一个！那么——是哪一个？”

波尔先生瞥了一眼被告。海德原本苍白的脸涨红了几分，双眼也亮了起来；他那模样，仿佛在满怀敌意的人群中骤然看见了一张友人的面孔。可一直伏案翻阅卷宗的米林顿-拜沃特先生忽然抬起头来，又提了个问题，海德便又把注意力转回到他身上。

“关于此事，你真正确知的，”米林顿-拜沃特先生问道，“只是你碰巧拐进了朗斯代尔过道，看见一个人躺在人行道上、旁边有一枚戒指，而你当时实在饿得发慌、走投无路，于是抓起那枚戒指，撒腿拼命跑掉了——是这样吗？”

===Original===

“Yes﻿—that is all,” asserted Hyde. “Except that I had met a man, as I have already told you, at the end of the passage by which I entered.”

“You did not even know whether this man lying on the pavement was alive or dead?”

“I thought he might be drunk,” replied Hyde. “But after I had snatched up the ring I never thought at all until I had run some distance. I was afraid of being followed.”

“Now why were you afraid of being followed?”

“I was famishing!” answered Hyde. “I knew I could get something, some money, on that ring, in the morning, and I wanted to stick to it. I was afraid that the man whom I met as I ran out of the passage, whom I now know to have been Mr. Viner, might follow me and make me give up the ring. And the ring meant food.”

Mr. Millington-Bywater let this answer sink into the prevalent atmosphere and suddenly turned to another matter. The knife which had been found in Hyde’s possession was lying with certain other exhibits on the solicitor’s table, and Mr. Millington-Bywater pointed to it.

===Chinese===

“是的——就这些，”海德言之凿凿，“只是正如我已经说过的，在我进去的那条过道口，我还碰上过一个人。”

“你甚至不知道躺在人行道上的那个人是死是活？”

“我当时想他多半是喝醉了，”海德答道，“可我一抓起戒指就什么都没想，直到跑出去一段路才回过神来。我怕被人追上来。”

“那么，你为什么怕被人追上来？”

“我快饿死了！”海德答道，“我知道到了早上，拿那枚戒指总能换点东西、换点钱，所以我死死攥着它不放。我怕在我跑出过道时撞见的那个人——我现在知道那是维纳先生——会追上来逼我交出戒指。那枚戒指，就是活命的口粮啊。”

米林顿-拜沃特先生任由这句回答在满庭的气氛里沉淀了片刻，忽然转到了另一件事上。从海德身上搜出的那把刀正和其他一些物证一起摆在律师席上，米林顿-拜沃特先生朝它指了指。

===Original===

“Now about that knife,” he said. “It is yours? Very well﻿—how long have you had it?”

“Three or four years,” replied Hyde, promptly. “I bought it when I was touring in the United States, at a town called Guthrie, in Oklahoma. And,” he added suddenly and with a triumphant smile as of a man who is unexpectedly able to clinch an argument, “there is a gentleman there who was with me when I bought it﻿—Mr. Nugent Starr!”

From the magistrate on his bench to the policeman at the door every person in court turned to look at the man to whom the prisoner pointed an outstretched finger. And Mr. Pawle let out an irrepressible exclamation.

“Good God!” he said. “The claimant fellow!”

But Viner said nothing. He was staring, as everybody else was, at the man who sat by Methley. He, suddenly aware that Hyde had pointed to him, was obviously greatly taken aback and embarrassed﻿—he looked sharply at the prisoner, knitted his brows, shook his head, and turning to Methley muttered something which no one else caught. Mr. Millington-Bywater looked at him and turned to his client.

“You say there is a gentleman here﻿—that gentleman!﻿—who was with you when you bought that knife?” he asked. “A friend of yours, then?”

===Chinese===

“现在说说那把刀，”他说，“它是你的？很好——你持有它多久了？”

“三四年了，”海德应声而答，“是我在美国巡回演出时买的，在俄克拉何马州（Oklahoma）一个叫格斯里（Guthrie）的镇子上。而且，”他忽然又补上一句，脸上带着得胜似的笑，像个意外抓住铁证、一举定论的人，“这里就有一位先生，我买刀的时候他就在场——纽金特·斯塔尔先生（Mr. Nugent Starr）！”

从法官席上的治安法官到门口的警察，庭上每一个人都转过脸去，望着被告伸指直指的那个人。波尔先生也按捺不住，失声叫了出来。

“我的天哪！”他说，“是那个主张人的家伙！”

维纳却一言不发。他同在场所有人一样，直勾勾地盯着坐在梅思利身旁的那个人。那人猛然察觉海德指的是自己，显然大为错愕，窘迫不已——他狠狠看了被告一眼，皱起眉头，摇了摇头，随即转向梅思利，低声咕哝了句什么，谁也没听清。米林顿-拜沃特先生望了望他，转回自己的当事人。

“你是说，这里有一位先生——就是那位先生！——你买那把刀时他在场？”他问，“那么，是你的朋友了？”

===Original===

“Well﻿—we were playing in the same company,” asserted Hyde. “Mr. Moreby-Bannister’s company. He was heavy lead﻿—I was juvenile. He knows me well enough. He was with me when I bought that knife in a hardware store in Guthrie.”

The magistrate’s eye was on the man who sat by Methley, and there was a certain amount of irritation in it. And suddenly Methley whispered something to his companion and the man shyly but with a noticeable composure stood up.

“I beg Your Worship’s pardon,” he said, quietly, with a polite bow to the bench, “but really, the witness is under a mistaken impression! I don’t know him, and I have never been in the town he mentions﻿—in fact, I have never been in the United States. I am very sorry, but, really, there is some strange mistake﻿—I﻿—the witness is an absolute stranger to me!”

The attention of all present was transferred to Hyde. And Hyde flushed, leaned forward over the ledge of the witness-box and gave the claimant a long, steady stare.

“No mistake at all!” he suddenly exclaimed in a firm voice. “That’s Mr. Nugent Starr! I played with him for over twelve months.”

While this had been going on, Felpham on one side, and Carless on the other, had been whispering to Mr. Millington-Bywater, who listened to both with growing interest, and began to nod to each with increasing intelligence﻿—and then, suddenly, the prosecuting counsel played unexpectedly and directly into his hand.

===Chinese===

“唔——我们同在一个剧团演戏，”海德言之凿凿，“是莫尔比-班尼斯特先生的剧团。他演重头主角——我演少年角色。他对我再熟悉不过了。我在格斯里一家五金铺买那把刀时，他就在旁边。”

治安法官的目光落在坐在梅思利身旁的那人身上，眼神里带着几分愠意。突然间，梅思利对同伴低语了一句，那人略带腼腆、却又不失沉稳地站起身来。

“恕我冒昧，庭上，”他向法官席彬彬有礼地欠了欠身，语气平静地说道，“可这位证人实在是弄错了！我根本不认识他，也从未到过他说的那个镇子——事实上，我从未去过美国。我很抱歉，可这实在是天大的误会——我——这位证人于我完全是素不相识！”

满庭的注意力霎时转到海德身上。海德涨红了脸，从证人席的围栏上探身向前，对主张人投以长久的、逼视的目光。

“根本没有什么误会！”他忽然斩钉截铁地高声道，“他就是纽金特·斯塔尔先生！我同他同台搭档了十二个多月。”

这一幕进行的同时，费尔法姆在一边、卡利斯在另一边，先后凑近米林顿-拜沃特先生低语；他听着两边的话，兴致越来越浓，开始频频点头，一副越来越心领神会的样子——就在这时，控方律师出人意料地、正正地把机会送到了他手里。

===Original===

“If Your Worship pleases,” said the prosecuting counsel, “I should like to have the prisoner’s assertion categorically denied﻿—it may be of importance. Perhaps this gentleman will go into the box and deny it on oath.”

Mr. Millington-Bywater sat down as quickly as if a heavy hand had forced him into his seat, and Viner saw a swift look of gratification cross his features. Close by, Mr. Pawle chuckled with joy.

“By the Lord Harry!” he whispered, “the very thing we wanted! No need to wait for the adjourned coroner’s inquest, Viner﻿—the thing’ll come out now!”

Viner did not understand. He saw Hyde turned out of the box; he saw the claimant, after an exchange of remarks with Methley, step into it; he heard him repeat on oath the denial he had just uttered, after stating that his name was Cave, and that he lived at the Belmead Hotel, Lancaster Gate; and he saw Mr. Millington-Bywater, after exchanging a few questions and answers in whispers with Hyde over the ledge of the dock, turn to the witness as he was about to step down.

“A moment, sir,” he said. “I want to ask you a few questions, with the permission of His Worship, who will soon see that they are very pertinent. So,” he went on, “you reside at the Belmead Hotel, in Lancaster Gate, and your name is Edward Cave?”

“At present,” answered the witness, stiffly.

===Chinese===

“启禀庭上，”控方律师说道，“我希望让被告那番断言得到明确的当面否认——此事或许关系重大。可否请这位先生进入证人席，起誓予以否认。”

米林顿-拜沃特先生一屁股坐了下去，快得仿佛有一只大手把他按进了座位；维纳看见一丝飞快而称快的神色掠过他的脸。近旁，波尔先生喜得低笑出声。

“老天爷在上！”他悄声道，“这正是我们求之不得的！不必再等延期举行的验尸官死因研讯了，维纳——真相现在就要水落石出了！”

维纳却没有听懂。他看见海德退出了证人席；看见主张人与梅思利交谈几句之后，跨进了证人席；听见他在自陈名叫凯夫、住在兰开斯特门的贝尔米德旅馆（Belmead Hotel）之后，起誓把方才那番否认又重述了一遍；又看见米林顿-拜沃特先生隔着被告席围栏与海德低声一问一答了几句之后，在那证人正要步下证人席时转向了他。

“请留步，先生，”他说，“我要向你提几个问题，还望庭上俯允——您很快便会看到，这些问题切中要害。那么，”他接着说，“你住在兰开斯特门的贝尔米德旅馆，名叫爱德华·凯夫（Edward Cave）？”

“目前是。”证人僵硬地答道。

===Original===

“Do you mean that your name is Edward Cave﻿—at present?”

“My name is Edward Cave, and at present I live﻿—as I have stated,” replied the witness with dignity.

“You have just stated, on oath, that you are not Nugent Starr, have never been so called, don’t know the prisoner, never met him in America, have never set foot in America! Now, then﻿—mind, you’re on your oath!﻿—is Edward Cave your real or full name?”

“Well, strictly speaking,” answered the witness, after some hesitation, “no, it is not. My full name is Cave-Gray﻿—my family name; but for the present﻿—”

“For the present you wish to be called Mr. Cave. Now, sir, are you not the person who claims to be the rightful Earl of Ellingham?”

A murmur of excited interest ran round the court, and everybody recognized that a new stage of the case had been entered upon. Every eye, especially the observant eyes on the bench, were fixed on the witness, who now looked considerably ruffled. He glanced at Methley﻿—but Methley sat with averted look and made no sign; he looked at the magistrate; the magistrate, it was plain, expected the question to be answered. And the answer came, almost sullenly.

===Chinese===

“你的意思是说，你名叫爱德华·凯夫——只是目前？”

“我名叫爱德华·凯夫，而我目前住在哪里——我已陈述过了。”证人神态俨然地答道。

“你方才已起誓声明：你不是纽金特·斯塔尔，从未叫过这个名字，不认识被告，从未在美国遇见过他，也从未踏上过美国的土地！那么现在——记住，你是在誓言之下！——爱德华·凯夫可是你的真名，或是你的全名？”

“唔，严格说来，”证人迟疑了片刻，答道，“不是。我的全名是凯夫-格雷（Cave-Gray）——那是我的家姓；只是眼下——”

“只是眼下，你愿意让人称你凯夫先生。那么，先生，你难道不就是那位自称是名正言顺的埃灵厄姆伯爵的人吗？”

一阵激动的低语在法庭里传开，人人都意识到，此案已进入了一个新的阶段。所有的目光——尤其是法官席上那几双明察秋毫的眼睛——都盯在证人身上；他此刻显得相当恼乱。他望向梅思利——可梅思利坐在那里，别开了脸，毫无表示；他又望向治安法官；而治安法官，很显然，正等着这个问题得到回答。回答终于来了，几乎带着愠怒。

===Original===

“Yes, I am!”

“That is to say, you are really﻿—or you claim to be really﻿—the Lord Marketstoke who disappeared from England some thirty-five years ago, and you have now returned, though you are legally presumed to be dead, to assert your rights to titles and estates? You absolutely claim to be the ninth Earl of Ellingham?”

“Yes!”

“Where have you been during the last thirty-five years?”

“In Australia.”

“What part?”

===Chinese===

“是的，我就是！”

“这就是说，你确实是——或者你自称确实是——那位大约三十五年前从英国失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵；如今虽然依法已被推定死亡，你却回来了，要主张你对爵位和产业的权利？你断然声称自己是第九代埃灵厄姆伯爵？”

“是的！”

“这三十五年里，你都在什么地方？”

“在澳大利亚。”

“具体在哪个地方？”

===Original===

“Chiefly in Melbourne. But I was for four or five years upcountry.”

“What name did you go under there?”

Mr. Pawle, Mr. Carless and the rest of the spectators who were in these secrets regarded the witness with keen attention when this question was put to him. But his answer came promptly.

“At first, under the name of Wickham. Later under the one I now use﻿—Cave.”

“Did you marry out there?”

“Never!”

===Chinese===

“主要在墨尔本。不过有四五年在内陆乡间。”

“你在那边用的是什么名字？”

这个问题一提出，波尔先生、卡利斯先生以及其余知悉这些内情的旁听者，都全神贯注地盯着证人。可他的回答脱口而出。

“起初，用的是威克姆这个名字。后来就用我如今用的这个——凯夫。”

“你在那边结过婚吗？”

“从未结过！”

===Original===

“And so, of course, you never had a daughter?”

“I have never been married and have never had daughter or son!”

Mr. Millington-Bywater turned to Mr. Carless, at his left elbow, and exchanged two or three whispered remarks with him. At last he looked round again at the witness.

“Yesterday,” he said, “in your character of claimant to the Ellingham title and estates you showed to Messrs. Carless & Driver, of Lincoln’s Inn Fields, and to the present holder of the title, certain documents, letters, papers, which would go some way toward establishing your claim to be what you profess to be. Now, I will say at once that we believe these papers to have been stolen from the body of John Ashton when he was murdered. And I will ask you a direct question, on your oath! Have those papers always been in your possession since you left England thirty-five years ago?”

The witness drew himself up and looked steadily at his questioner.

“No!” he answered firmly. “They were stolen from me almost as soon as I arrived in Australia. I have only just regained possession of them.”

===Chinese===

“那么，你自然也从未有过女儿了？”

“我从未结婚，也从未有过女儿或儿子！”

米林顿-拜沃特先生转向左肘边的卡利斯先生，与他低声交谈了两三句。最后，他重新回头看向证人。

“昨天，”他说，“你以埃灵厄姆爵位与产业主张人的身份，向林肯律师学院广场的卡利斯与德赖弗事务所，并向该爵位如今的持有者，出示了若干文件、信函、文书，它们多少可以佐证你所自称的身份。那么，我立刻直言：我们相信，这些文件是约翰·阿什顿遇害时从其尸体上被偷走的。现在，我要在誓言之下直接问你一个问题！自你三十五年前离开英国以来，这些文件是否一直在你手中？”

证人挺直了身子，目光直视着发问的人。

“没有！”他斩钉截铁地答道，“我一到澳大利亚，它们就几乎立刻被人偷走了。我不过是直到最近才重新拿到手而已。”

## XXIII / 二十三

===Original===

Is This Man Right?

A murmur of astonishment ran through the court as the witness made his last reply, and those most closely interested in him turned and looked at each other with obvious amazement. And for a moment Mr. Millington-Bywater seemed to be at a loss; in the next he bent forward toward the witness-box and fixed the man standing there with a piercing look.

“Do you seriously tell us, on your oath, that these papers﻿—your papers, if you are what you claim to be﻿—were stolen from you many years ago, and have only just been restored to you?” he asked. “On your oath, mind!”

“I do tell you so,” answered the witness quietly. “I am on oath.”

The magistrate glanced at Mr. Millington-Bywater.

“What is the relevancy of this﻿—in relation to the prisoner and the charge against him?” he inquired. “You have some point, of course?”

===Chinese===

此人所言属实吗？

证人答完最后一句话，一阵惊愕的低语顿时传遍法庭；与他利害攸关最深的那几个人转过脸来面面相觑，惊愕之情溢于言表。米林顿-拜沃特（Millington-Bywater）先生一时似乎不知如何是好；转瞬间，他已朝证人席俯身向前，目光灼灼地逼视着站在席上的那个人。

“你可是凭着誓言郑重告诉我们：这些文件——倘若你果真是你所自称的那个人，它们便是你的文件——许多年前就被人从你手里偷走，直到最近才归还到你手上？”他问道，“凭的是誓言，切记！”

“我正是这样告诉你们的，”证人平静地答道，“我是发了誓的。”

治安法官朝米林顿-拜沃特先生瞥了一眼。

“这与被告、与他所受的指控——究竟有何关联？”他问道，“你当然是有用意的吧？”

===Original===

“The relevancy is this, Your Worship,” replied Mr. Millington-Bywater: “Our contention is that the papers referred to were until recently in the custody of John Ashton, the murdered man﻿—I can put a witness in the box who can give absolute proof of that, a highly reputable witness, who is present﻿—and that John Ashton was certainly murdered by some person or persons who, for purposes of their own, wished to gain possession of them. Now, we know that they are in possession of the present witness, or rather, of his solicitors, to whom he has handed them. I mean to prove that Ashton was murdered in the way, and for the reason I suggest, and that accordingly the prisoner is absolutely innocent of the charge brought against him. I should therefore like to ask this witness to tell us how he regained possession of these papers, for I am convinced that in what he can tell us lies the secret of Ashton’s murder. Now,” he continued, turning again to the witness as the magistrate nodded assent, “we will assume for the time being that you are what you represent yourself to be﻿—the Lord Marketstoke who disappeared from England thirty-five years ago. You have just heard what I said to His Worship﻿—about these papers, and what I put forward as regards their connection with the murder of John Ashton? Will you tell us how you lost those papers, and more particularly, how you recently regained possession of them? You see the immense, the vital importance of this to the unfortunate young fellow in the dock?”

“Who,” answered the witness with a calm smile, “is quite and utterly mistaken in thinking that he knew me in America, for I have certainly never set foot in America, neither North nor South, in my life! I am very much surprised indeed to be forced into publicity as I have been this morning﻿—I came here as a merely curious spectator and had no idea whatever that I should be called into this box. But if any evidence of mine can establish, or help to establish, the prisoner’s innocence, I will give it only too gladly.”

“Much obliged to you, sir,” said Mr. Millington-Bywater, who, in Viner’s opinion, was evidently impressed by the witness’s straightforward tone and candid demeanour.

“Well, if you will tell us﻿—in your own way﻿—about these papers, now﻿—always remembering that we have absolute proof that until recently they were in the possession of John Ashton? Let me preface whatever you choose to tell us with a question: Do you know that they were in possession of John Ashton?”

“I have no more idea or knowledge of whose hands they were in, and had been in, for many years, until they were restored to me, than the man in the moon has!” affirmed the witness. “I’ll tell you the whole story﻿—willingly: I could have told it yesterday to certain gentlemen, whom I see present, if they had not treated me as an impostor as soon as they saw me. Well,”﻿—here he folded his hands on the ledge of the witness-box, and quietly fixing his eyes on the examining counsel, proceeded to speak in a calm, conversational tone﻿—“the story is this: I left England about five-and-thirty years ago after certain domestic unpleasantnesses which I felt so much that I determined to give up all connection with my family and to start an absolutely new life of my own. I went away to Australia and landed there under the name of Wickham. I had a certain amount of money which had come to me from my mother. I speculated with it on my arrival, somewhat foolishly, no doubt, and I lost it﻿—every penny.

“So then I was obliged to work for my living. I went up country, and for some time worked as a miner in the Bendigo district. I had been working in this way perhaps fourteen months when an accident occurred in the mine at which I was engaged. There was a serious fall of earth and masonry; two or three of my fellow-workers were killed on the spot, and I was taken up for dead. I was removed to a local hospital﻿—there had been some serious injury to my head and spine, but I still had life in me, and I was brought round. But I remained in hospital, in a sort of semiconscious state, for a long time﻿—months. When I went back, after my discharge, to my quarters﻿—nothing but a rough shanty which I had shared with many other men﻿—all my possessions had vanished. Among them, of course, were the papers I had kept, and a packet of letters written to me by my mother when I was a schoolboy at Eton.

===Chinese===

“关联就在这里，庭上，”米林顿-拜沃特先生答道：“我们的论点是：方才所说的那些文件，直到不久之前都由死者约翰·阿什顿保管着——我可以传唤一位证人到席，对此提出绝对确凿的证明，一位极有声望的证人，此刻就在庭上——而约翰·阿什顿之所以被害，必定是死于某一个人或一些人的毒手，那些人为了自己的目的，一心想把这些文件弄到手。如今我们知道，文件已在当前这位证人手中——或者更确切地说，在他移交给他的律师们的手中。我要证明，阿什顿正是以我提示的那种方式、为我提示的那个缘由而被害的，因而被告对加在他身上的罪名绝对无辜。因此我想请这位证人告诉我们，他是怎样重新得到这些文件的，因为我深信，阿什顿凶案的秘密就藏在他所能讲述的话里。”治安法官点头应允，他便又转向证人，接着说道：“我们不妨姑且认定，你就是你所自称的那个人——三十五年前从英国失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵。你刚才已经听见我对庭上说的话了——关于这些文件，以及我就它们与约翰·阿什顿凶案的关联所提出的见解。你可愿意告诉我们，你是怎样丢失这些文件的，尤其是，最近又是怎样把它们重新拿到手的？你总看得出来，这对被告席上那个不幸的小伙子，有着何等重大、何等性命攸关的意义吧？”

“那位老弟，”证人带着从容的微笑答道，“以为他当年在美国认识我，这可是错到底了，因为我这辈子压根儿没有踏上过美洲的土地，北美南美都没有！今天早晨这样被迫抛头露面，我着实大吃一惊——我来这里本不过是个纯属好奇的看客，压根儿没想到会被传进这个席位。不过，倘若我的证词能够证明、或者有助于证明被告无罪，我是求之不得，非常乐意提供的。”

“多谢您了，先生，”米林顿-拜沃特先生说。在维纳看来，律师显然已被证人坦率的语气和爽直的态度打动了。

“那么，现在就请你讲讲这些文件的事吧——按你自己的方式讲——只是要时刻记住，我们有绝对确凿的证据，证明它们直到不久之前还在约翰·阿什顿手里。在你愿意讲的话之前，容我先问一个问题：你可知道它们曾在约翰·阿什顿手里？”

“这么多年来，这些文件落在了谁的手里、先前又在谁的手里，直到归还给我为止，我知道得并不比月中人多！”证人正色说道，“我可以把全部经过原原本本讲出来——很乐意讲：昨天我就本可以讲给几位先生听的，他们此刻就在庭上，只可惜他们一见到我，就把我当成了骗子。好吧，”——说到这里，他把双手交叠在证人席的栏板上，目光安然地注视着质证律师，用一种从容的、近乎闲谈的语气说了下去——“事情是这样的：大约三十五年前，家里出了些不痛快的事，我心里大为难受，便决意斩断同家族的一切瓜葛，去开创一种完全属于我自己的新生活。我远走澳大利亚，用威克姆这个姓名在那里上了岸。我手头有一笔钱，是母亲传给我的。一到那里，我就拿这笔钱做投机，无疑有些蠢，结果赔了个精光——一个便士也不剩。

“于是我只得自己谋生。我去了内陆腹地，在本迪戈（Bendigo）矿区当了些时候矿工。这样干了大约十四个月，我受雇的那座矿出了事故。土石砖石大批坍塌；两三个工友当场丧命，我也被当成死人抬了起来。我被送进当地一所医院——头部和脊柱都有重伤，可我总算还剩一口气，被救转了过来。但此后很长时间——好几个月——我一直待在医院里，处于半昏迷的状态。等我出院之后回到住处——那不过是同许多伙伴合住的一间粗陋棚屋——我的全部财物早已无影无踪。其中自然有我保存的那些文件，还有一包我念伊顿公学时母亲写给我的信。

===Original===

“Of course, I knew at once what had happened﻿—some one of my mates, believing me to be dead, had appropriated all my belongings and gone off with them. There was nothing at all to be wondered at in that﻿—it was the usual thing in such a society. And I knew there was nothing to do but to accept my loss philosophically.”

“Did you make no effort to recover your possessions?” asked Mr. Millington-Bywater.

“No,” answered the witness with a quiet smile. “I didn’t! I knew too much of the habits of men in mining centers to waste time in that way. A great many men had left that particular camp during my illness﻿—it would have been impossible to trace each one. No﻿—after all, I had left England in order to lose my identity, and now, of course, it was gone. I went away into quite another part of the country﻿—into Queensland. I began trading in Brisbane, and I did very well there, and remained there many years. Then I went farther south, to Sydney﻿—and I did very well there too. It was in Sydney, years after that, that I saw the advertisements in the newspapers, English and Colonial, setting forth that my father was dead, and asking for news of myself. I took no notice of them﻿—I had not the least desire to return to England, no wish for the title, and I was quite content that my youngest brother should get that and the estates. So I did nothing; nobody knew who I really was﻿—”

“One moment!” said Mr. Millington-Bywater. “While you were at the mining-camp, in the Bendigo district, did you ever reveal your secret to any of your fellow-miners?”

“Never!” answered the witness. “I never revealed it to a living soul until I told my solicitor there, Mr. Methley, after my recent arrival in London.”

“But of course, whoever stole your letters and so on, would discover, or guess at, the truth?” suggested Mr. Millington-Bywater.

===Chinese===

“当然，我立刻就明白出了什么事——某个伙伴认定我死了，便把我的东西统统据为己有，带着跑掉了。这里头毫无可怪之处——在那样的人群里，这是司空见惯的事。我也明白，除了达观地认下这笔损失，别无他法。”

“你没有想法子追回你的财物吗？”米林顿-拜沃特先生问。

“没有，”证人带着淡然的一笑答道，“没找！矿区市镇上那些人的习性我见识得太多了，犯不上在那上头浪费时间。我生病期间，离开那座矿营的人多得很——要一个个追查，根本办不到。不——说到底，我离开英国本就是为了把身份丢掉，如今它自然丢掉了。我便远走到国内的另一个地区——去了昆士兰（Queensland）。我在布里斯班（Brisbane）做起买卖，做得很不坏，在那里待了许多年。后来再往南去，到了悉尼（Sydney）——在那儿也做得很不坏。正是多年之后，在悉尼，我在英国和殖民地的报纸上看到那些启事，说我父亲已故，征求我的消息。我没有理会——我丝毫不想回英国，也无意于爵位，由我最小的弟弟承袭爵位和家业，我正称心满意。于是我什么也没有做；谁也不知道我究竟是谁——”

“且慢！”米林顿-拜沃特先生说，“你在本迪戈矿区矿营的那阵子，可曾向哪一位矿工伙伴泄露过你的秘密？”

“从来没有！”证人答道，“直到最近到了伦敦，才把这件事告诉了我的律师梅思利先生；在此之前，我对一个活人也不曾透露过。”

“不过，那个偷走你信件等物的人，自然就会发现、或者猜到真相了？”米林顿-拜沃特先生提点道。

===Original===

“Oh, of course, of course!” said the witness. “Well as I was saying, I did nothing﻿—except to keep an eye on the papers. I saw in due course that leave to presume my death had been given, and that my younger brother had assumed the title, and administered the estate, and I was quite content. The fact was, I was at that time doing exceedingly well, and I was too much interested in my doings to care about what was going on in England. All my life,” continued the witness, with a slight smile, “I have had a﻿—I had better call it a weakness﻿—for speculating; and when I had got a goodly sum of money together by my trading venture in Brisbane and Sydney, I began speculating again, in Melbourne chiefly. And﻿—to cut my story short﻿—last year I had one of my periodic bad turns of fortune: I lost a lot of money. Now, I am, as you see, getting on in life, over sixty﻿—and it occurred to me that if I came over to England and convinced my nephew, the present holder of the title and estates, that I am really who I am, he would not be averse﻿—we have always been a generous family﻿—to giving me enough to settle down on in Australia for the rest of my days. Perhaps I had better say at once, since we are making matters so very public, that I do not want the title, nor the estate; I will be quite candid and say what I do want﻿—enough to let me live in proper comfort in Australia, whither I shall again repair as soon as I settle my affairs here.”

Mr. Millington-Bywater glanced at the magistrate and then at the witness.

“Well, now, these papers?” he said. “You didn’t bring them to London with you?”

“Of course not!” answered the witness. “I had not seen or heard of them for thirty-two years! No I relied, on coming to this country, on other things to prove my identity, such as my knowledge of Marketstoke and Ellingham, my thorough acquaintance with the family history, my recollection of people I had known, like Mr. Carless, Mr. Driver, and their clerk, Mr. Portlethwaite, and on the fact that I lost this finger through a shooting accident when I was a boy, at Ellingham. Curiously,” he added with another smile, “these things don’t seem to have much weight. But no! I had no papers when I landed here.”

“How did they come into your possession, then?” asked Mr. Millington-Bywater. “That is what we most earnestly desire to know. Let me impress upon you, sir, that this is the most serious and fateful question I can possibly put to you! How did you get them?”

“And﻿—from whom?” said the magistrate. “From whom?”

===Chinese===

“哦，那是自然，自然！”证人说，“唔，正像我方才说的，我什么也没做——只是留意着那些文件的动静。后来我如期看到，推定我死亡的许可已经批下，我弟弟承袭了爵位、经管了家业，我心满意足。实情是，我那时营生做得极好，一心专注在自己的事务上，英国的事懒得去管。我这个人，一辈子，”证人带着一丝浅笑接着说道，“都有一种——不妨叫它癖好吧——投机的癖好；等到在布里斯班和悉尼的经营攒下了可观的一笔钱，我便又投机起来，主要在墨尔本。后来——长话短说——去年我赶上了一回周期性的坏运道：赔掉了一大笔钱。如今呢，诸位也看得见，我年事渐长，已过六十——我忽然想到，倘若我到英国来一趟，让我的侄儿——如今爵位和家业的持有人——相信我确实是我本人，他不会不情愿——我们家族向来是慷慨的——给我一笔钱，够我在澳大利亚安顿下来度其余年。既然事情已经弄得这样公开，我不妨索性直说：我不要爵位，也不要家业；我愿意坦白讲明我到底要什么——一笔钱，能让我在澳大利亚过上相当舒适的日子，等这里的事务一了，我就再回到那边去。”

米林顿-拜沃特先生望了治安法官一眼，又望了证人一眼。

“那么，现在说这些文件？”他说，“你没有把它们随身带到伦敦来？”

“当然没有！”证人答道，“我已经有三十二年没见过、也没听说过它们了！不，我到这个国家来，靠的是别的凭据来证明身份：比如我对马克特斯托克和埃灵厄姆的了解，我对家族史的透彻熟谙，我对旧相识的记忆——像卡利斯先生、德赖弗先生，还有他们的书记员波特尔思韦特先生——再加上我幼年在埃灵厄姆因一次走火事故失去这根手指的事实。”“说来奇怪，”他又含笑补了一句，“这些凭据似乎没有多少分量。可是没有！我在这里登陆时，什么文件也没有带。”

“那么，它们是怎么落到你手里的？”米林顿-拜沃特先生问，“这正是我们最恳切地想知道的。先生，请容我郑重提醒您：这是我能向您提出的最严重、最关乎命运的问题！你是怎么把它们弄到手的？”

“还有——是从谁手里？”治安法官说，“从谁手里？”

===Original===

The witness shook his head.

“I can tell you exactly how I got them,” he answered. “But I can’t tell you from whom, for I don’t know! What I can tell you is this: When I arrived at Tilbury from Melbourne, I asked a fellow-passenger with whom I came along to London if he could tell me of a quiet, good hotel in the neighbourhood of the parks﻿—he recommended the Belfield, in Lancaster Gate. I went there and put myself up, and from it I went out and about a good deal, looking up old haunts. I also lunched and dined a good many times at some of the new restaurants which had sprung into being since I left London. I mention this to show you that I was where I could be seen and noticed, as I evidently was. One afternoon, while I was sitting in the smoking-room at my hotel, the pageboy came in with a letter on his tray, approached me, and said that it had been brought by a district messenger. It was addressed simply, ‘Mr. Cave’﻿—the name by which I had registered at the hotel﻿—and was sealed; the enclosure, on a half-sheet of notepaper, was typewritten. I have it here,” continued the witness, producing a pocketbook and taking out an envelope. “I will read its contents, and I shall be glad to let anyone concerned see it. There is no address and no date, and it says this: ‘If you wish to recover the papers and letters which were lost by you when you went into hospital at Wirra-Worra, Bendigo, thirty-two years ago, be at the Speke Monument in Kensington Gardens at five o’clock this afternoon.’ There was no signature.”

Another murmur of intense and excited interest ran round the court as the witness handed the letter up to the magistrate, who, after looking it over, passed it on to the counsel below. They, in their turn, showed it to Mr. Carless, Mr. Pawle and Lord Ellingham, Mr. Pawle, showing it to Viner, whispered in his ear:

“If this man’s telling the truth,” he said, “this is the most extraordinary story I ever heard in my life.”

“It seems to me that it is the truth!” muttered Viner. “And I’m pretty certain that at last we’re on the way-to finding out who killed Ashton. But let’s hear the end.”

Mr. Millington-Bywater handed the letter back with a polite bow﻿—it was very obvious to more than one observer that he had by this time quite accepted the witness as what he claimed to be.

===Chinese===

证人摇了摇头。

“我可以确切地告诉你我是怎么得到它们的，”他答道，“可我说不出是从谁手里，因为我不知道！我能告诉你们的是这么回事：我从墨尔本乘船抵达蒂尔伯里之后，向一位结伴来伦敦的旅伴打听，问他可否给我介绍一家公园左近清静的好旅馆——他推荐了兰开斯特门的贝尔菲尔德（Belfield）旅馆。我便到那里落脚投宿，又时常从旅馆出门四处走走，寻访旧游之地。我还多次在几家新开的餐馆里用午餐、吃晚餐——那些馆子都是我离开伦敦以后才冒出来的。我提这些，是想说明我当时所处的位置容易被看见、被注意，而事实显然也正是如此。一天下午，我正坐在旅馆的吸烟室里，小听差托着一封信走了进来，到我面前说，这信是一位区信差送来的。信封上只写着‘凯夫先生’——那是我在旅馆登记用的名字——信口封着；里面衬的半页信笺，是打字机打的字。信就在我这里，”证人说着，取出一个记事本，从里面拿出一只信封，“我来把内容念一念，也乐意让任何有关的人过目。上面没有地址，也没有日期，写的是：‘你若想找回三十二年前在本迪戈维拉-沃拉（Wirra-Worra）住院时丢失的文件和信件，请于今日下午五点到肯辛顿花园的斯皮克纪念碑（Speke Monument）来。’信上没有署名。”

证人把信呈给治安法官，又一阵紧张热切的低语在法庭里传开；治安法官过目之后，把信传给了堂下的律师们。他们又依次给卡利斯先生、波尔先生和埃灵厄姆勋爵传看；波尔先生把信递给维纳看时，凑到他耳边低声道：

“要是这人讲的是实话，”他说，“这就是我这辈子听过的最离奇的一件事了。”

“依我看，这就是实话！”维纳喃喃道，“而且我十拿九稳，我们终于走上了查明谁是杀害阿什顿的凶手的路。且听他讲完。”

米林顿-拜沃特先生客气地欠了欠身，把信递还回去——不止一位旁观者看得分明，到这时，他已完全把这位证人当作其所自称的那个人接受下来了。

===Original===

“You kept the appointment?” he asked.

“I did, indeed!” exclaimed the witness. “As much out of greatly excited curiosity as anything! It seemed to me a most extraordinary thing that papers stolen from me in Australia thirty-two years ago should be returned to me in London! Yes, I walked down to the Speke Monument. I saw no one about there but a heavily veiled woman who walked about on one side of the obelisk while I patrolled the other. Eventually she approached me, and at once asked me if I had kept secret the receipt of the mysterious letter? I assured her that I had. She then told me that she was the ambassadress of the people who had my letters and papers, and who had seen and recognized me in London and tracked me to my hotel. She was empowered to negotiate with me for the handing over of the papers. There were stipulations. I was to give my solemn word of honour that I would not follow her, or cause her to be followed. I was not to ask questions. And I was to give a postdated check on the bank at which I had opened an account in London, on receipt of the papers. The check was to be postdated one month; it was to be made out to bearer, and the amount was ten thousand pounds. I agreed!”

“You really agreed!” exclaimed Mr. Millington-Bywater.

“I agreed! I wanted my papers. We parted, with an agreement that we were to meet two days later at the same place. I was there﻿—so was the woman. She handed me a parcel, and I immediately took it to an adjacent seat and examined it. Everything that I could remember was there, with two exceptions. The packet of letters from my mother, to which I referred just now, was missing; so was a certain locket, which had belonged to her, and of which I had taken great care since her death, up to the time of my accident in the mining-camp. I pointed out these omissions to the woman: she answered that the papers which she had handed over were all that had been in her principal’s possession. Thereupon I gave her the check which had been agreed upon, and we parted.”

“And that is all you know of her?” asked Mr. Millington-Bywater.

“All!”

===Chinese===

“你如约赴会了？”他问。

“赴了，当然赴了！”证人叫道，“多半还是出于按捺不住的好奇心！三十二年前在澳大利亚被人偷走的文件，竟会在伦敦归还到我手里，这在我看来真是天大的奇事！不错，我步行到了斯皮克纪念碑。那里我只见到一个蒙着厚面纱的女人，在方尖碑的一侧走来走去，我则在另一侧踱步。末了她走近我，劈头就问我，收到那封神秘信件的事可曾守住了秘密？我向她保证了。她于是告诉我，她是那班持有我信件和文件的人的女使节，那班人在伦敦见到了我、认出了我，一路跟踪到我的旅馆。她受全权委托，与我洽商移交文件的事。附有几项条件。我必须以名誉郑重担保：不跟踪她，也不叫人跟踪她。我不得发问。我还须在收到文件时，开一张远期支票，以我在伦敦开户的那家银行为付款行。支票日期填迟一个月，抬头开作持票人，金额一万英镑。我答应了！”

“你居然真答应了！”米林顿-拜沃特先生叫道。

“我答应了！我要我的文件。我们分手时约定，两天后仍在原处相见。届时我到了——那女人也到了。她交给我一个纸包，我立刻拿到旁边一张座椅上查验。我记得的东西样样都在，只缺两样。我方才提到的那包母亲的信不见了；还有她遗下的一只金质小盒坠也不见了——自她去世直到矿营出事，我一直对它珍护备至。我向那女人指出这两件缺失：她回答说，她交出的文件，已是她委托人手中所有的全部。于是我把讲定的那张支票给了她，我们便分手了。”

“关于她，你知道的就只有这些？”米林顿-拜沃特先生问。

“就这些！”

===Original===

“Can you describe her?”

“A tallish, rather well-built woman, but so veiled that I could see nothing of her features; it was, moreover, nearly dark on both occasions. From her speech and manner, she was, I should say, a woman of education and refinement.”

“Did you try to trace her, or her principals, through the district messenger who brought the letter?”

“Certainly not! I told you, just now, that I gave my word of honour: I couldn’t.”

Mr. Millington-Bywater turned to the magistrate.

“I can, if Your Worship desires it, put a witness in the box who can prove beyond doubt that the papers of which we have just heard this remarkable story, were recently in the possession of John Ashton,” he said. “He is Mr. Cecil Perkwite, of the Middle Temple﻿—a member of my own profession.”

===Chinese===

“你能描述她的模样吗？”

“一个身量偏高、体格相当结实的女人，只是面纱蒙得严严实实，容貌一丝也看不出来；何况那两回天色都将近黑了。从她的谈吐和举止看，要我说，她是个受过教育、有教养的女人。”

“你可曾设法通过送信的区信差，去追查她或她那些委托人的下落？”

“当然没有！我方才告诉过你们，我以名誉作过担保：我不能那么做。”

米林顿-拜沃特先生转向治安法官。

“倘若庭上愿意，我可以传唤一位证人到席，毫无疑义地证明：我们方才听到的这个离奇故事所关的那些文件，不久之前确在约翰·阿什顿手中，”他说，“这位证人就是中殿律师学院的塞西尔·佩尔怀特（Cecil Perkwite）先生——与我是同行。”

===Original===

But the magistrate, who appeared unusually thoughtful, shook his head.

“After what we have heard,” he said, “I think we had better adjourn. The prisoner will be remanded﻿—as before﻿—for another week.”

When the magistrate had left the bench, and the court was humming with the murmur of tongues suddenly let free, Mr. Pawle forced his way to the side of the last witness.

“Whoever you are, sir,” he said, “there’s one thing certain﻿—nobody but you can supply the solution of the mystery about Ashton’s death! Come with me and Carless at once.”

===Chinese===

可治安法官摇了摇头，神色显得异乎寻常地深沉。

“听了方才这番话，”他说，“我看我们还是休庭为好。被告将照旧还押——再押一个星期。”

治安法官退庭之后，骤然得到自由的舌头嗡嗡营营，响彻法庭；波尔先生便奋力挤到方才那位证人的身边。

“先生，不管您是谁，”他说，“有一件事是铁定的——能解开阿什顿死亡之谜的，天底下只有您一个人！马上跟我来，一起去找卡利斯。”

## XXIV / 二十四

===Original===

The Broken Letter

The man whose extraordinary story had excited such intense interest had become the object of universal attention. Hyde, hitherto the centre of attraction, was already forgotten, and instead of people going away from the court to canvass his guilt or his innocence, they surged round the witness whose testimony, strange and unexpected, had so altered the probabilities of the case. It was with difficulty that Methley got his client away into a private room; there they were joined by Mr. Carless, Mr. Pawle, Mr. Perkwite, Lord Ellingham and Viner, and behind a locked door these men looked at each other and at this centre of interest with the air of those to whom something extraordinary has just been told. After a moment of silence Mr. Carless spoke, addressing the man whose story had brought matters to an undeniable crisis.

“I am sure,” he said gravely, and with a side glance at Lord Ellingham, “that if your story is true, sir﻿—and after what we have just heard, I am beginning to think that my first conclusions may have been wrong ones﻿—no one will welcome your reappearance more warmly than the young gentleman whom you will turn out of title and property! But you must see for yourself that your claims must be thoroughly investigated﻿—and as what you have now just told affects other people, and we must invite you to full discussion, I propose that, for the time being, we address you as Mr. Cave.”

The claimant smiled, and nodded genially to the young man whose uncle he alleged himself to be.

“I wish to remain Mr. Cave,” he said. “I don’t want to turn my nephew out of title and property, so long as he will do something for his old uncle. Call me Mr. Cave, by all means.”

“We must talk﻿—and at once,” said Mr. Carless. “There are several points arising out of your evidence on which you must give me information. Whoever is at the back of that woman who handed you those papers is probably the murderer of John Ashton﻿—and that is what must be got at. Now, where can we have a conference﻿—immediately?﻿—Your office, Methley, is not far away, I think.”

===Chinese===

残缺的信

这个以离奇经历激起莫大关注的人，一时成了万众瞩目的对象。海德此前本是众人目光的焦点，如今早被忘到一旁；人们离开法庭，谈论的已不是他有罪无罪，而是一窝蜂围住那位证词离奇出人意表、一举扭转了本案形势走向的证人。梅思利费了好大周折，才把当事人带进一间私人房间；卡利斯先生、波尔先生、佩尔怀特先生、埃灵厄姆勋爵和维纳随后也到了。门一锁上，这几个人你看看我、我看看你，又打量着这位新的焦点人物，一个个神情如同刚听完什么骇人奇闻。沉默片刻，卡利斯先生开口了，话锋直指那个把事态推到无可回避关头的人。

“我敢说，”他神色郑重地说道，眼睛朝埃灵厄姆勋爵瞟了一下，“假如您讲的是实情，先生——听罢方才这一切，我已开始觉得，我起初下的结论未必没有弄错——那么，对您的重新现身，没有人会比这位将被您夺去爵位和产业的年轻绅士欢迎得更加热诚！不过您自己也一定明白，您的主张必须经过彻底查证——而您方才所讲的事情牵涉旁人，我们必须请您参加充分的商议。因此我提议，眼下暂且称您为凯夫先生。”

主张人微微一笑，和蔼地朝那位年轻人点了点头——他自称是这位年轻人的叔父。

“我愿意继续做我的凯夫先生，”他说。“只要我那侄儿肯为他老叔父出点力，我并不想把他的爵位和产业夺过来。尽管管我叫凯夫先生好了。”

“我们必须谈一谈——马上就谈，”卡利斯先生说。“您的证词引出了好几个疑点，您必须向我提供情况。那个把文件交给您的女人，不管她背后是谁，那人大半就是谋害约翰·阿什顿的凶手——这正是必须查个水落石出的地方。那么，到哪儿去商议——立刻就去？——梅思利，你的事务所离这儿不远，我想。”

===Original===

“My house is nearer,” said Viner. “Come﻿—we shall be perfectly quiet in my study, and there will be nothing to interrupt us. Let us go now.”

A police official let them out by a side-door, and Viner and Mr. Pawle led the way through some side-streets to Markendale Square, the others coming behind, conversing eagerly about the events of the morning. Mr. Pawle, on his part, was full of excitement.

“If we can only trace that woman, Viner!” he exclaimed. “That’s the next thing! Get hold of her, whoever she is, and then﻿—ah, we shall be in sight of the finishing-part.”

“What about tracing the whole lot through the check he has given?” suggested Viner. “Wouldn’t that be a good way?”

“We should have to wait nearly a month,” answered Mr. Pawle. “And even then it would be difficult﻿—simple though it seems at first sight. There are folk who deal in postdated checks, remember! This may have been dealt with already﻿—aye, and that diamond too; and the man who has got the proceeds may already be many a mile away. Deep, cunning folk they are who have been in this, Viner. And now﻿—speed is the thing!”

===Chinese===

“我家更近，”维纳说。“来吧——我书房里清静得很，绝没有事情来搅扰。我们现在就走。”

一名警方人员领他们从一扇边门出去，维纳和波尔先生在前头带路，穿街过巷往马肯代尔广场去，其余人跟在后面，一路热切谈论着上午的种种事件。波尔先生自己更是兴奋难耐。

“只要能追查到那个女人就好，维纳！”他嚷道。“下一步就是这个！不管她是什么人，先把她抓到手，到那时候——啊，我们就望得见收场的门径了。”

“从他开出的那张支票入手，把这伙人统统查出来，怎么样？”维纳提议道。“这岂不是个好办法？”

“那我们就得等上将近一个月，”波尔先生答道。“而且到那时也未必容易——尽管乍看之下似乎简单。要知道，市面上有专做远期支票买卖的人！这张支票说不定早已转了手——是啊，那颗钻石也一样；得了赃款的家伙，此刻没准儿已经跑出老远去了。卷进这桩案子里的，都是些城府极深、诡计多端的家伙，维纳。眼下——要紧的是抢时间！”

===Original===

Viner led his guests into his library, and as he placed chairs for them round a centre table, an idea struck him.

“I have a suggestion to make,” he said with a shy smile at the legal men. “My aunt, Miss Penkridge, who lives with me, is an unusually sharp, shrewd woman. She has taken vast interest in this affair, and I have kept her posted up in all its details. She was in court just now and heard Mr. Cave’s story. If no one has any objection, I should like her to be present at our deliberations﻿—as a mysterious woman has entered into the case, Miss Penkridge may be able to suggest something.”

“Excellent idea!” exclaimed Mr. Carless. “A shrewd woman is worth her weight in gold! By all means bring Miss Penkridge in﻿—she may, as you say, make some suggestion.”

Miss Penkridge, fetched into the room and duly introduced, lost no time in making a suggestion of an eminently practical nature﻿—that as all these gentlemen had been cooped up in that stuffy police-court for two or three hours, they would be none the worse for a glass of wine, and she immediately disappeared, jingling a bunch of keys, to reappear a few minutes later in charge of the parlourmaid carrying decanters and glasses.

“A very comfortable suggestion, that, ma’am,” observed Mr. Carless, bowing to his hostess over a glass of old sherry. “Your intuition does you credit! But now, gentlemen, and Miss Penkridge, straight to business! Mr. Cave, the first question I want to put to you is this: on what date did you receive the letter which you exhibited in court this morning?”

Mr. Cave produced a small pocket diary and turned over its pages.

===Chinese===

维纳把客人领进书房，正围着中央一张桌子替他们摆椅子时，忽然想起一个主意。

“我有个提议，”他朝那几位法律界人士腼腆地一笑，说道。“跟我同住的姨妈彭克里奇小姐，是个格外机敏精明的女人。她一直极其关心这桩案子，我也随时把其中的种种细节讲给她听。方才她就在法庭上，听到了凯夫先生的陈述。要是没有人反对，我想请她也来出席我们的商议——既然案子里已经出现了一个神秘女人，彭克里奇小姐说不定能提出些见解。”

“好主意！”卡利斯先生高声道。“一个精明的女人，身价赛过同等分量的黄金！务必请彭克里奇小姐过来——正像你说的，她也许真能提出点什么。”

彭克里奇小姐被请进屋来，一一引见之后，立刻不失时机地提出了一个极其务实的建议——诸位先生在那间闷热的治安法庭里关了两三个钟头，喝杯酒决计不无益处。说着她当即转身出去，手里一串钥匙叮当作响，几分钟后重新露面，身后带着捧了酒瓶酒杯的客厅侍女。

“这个建议真是舒服妥帖，夫人，”卡利斯先生端着一杯陈年雪利酒，向女主人欠了欠身，说道。“您的直觉给您增光！不过眼下，诸位先生，还有彭克里奇小姐，马上谈正事！凯夫先生，我要向您提的第一个问题是这样：您今天上午在法庭上出示的那封信，是哪一天收到的？”

凯夫先生掏出一本小小的袖珍日记簿，翻起页来。

===Original===

“I can tell you that,” he answered. “I made a note of it at the time. It was﻿—yes, here we are﻿—on the twenty-first of November.”

“And you received these papers, I think you said, two days later?”

“Yes﻿—on the twenty-third. Here is the entry.”

Mr. Carless looked round at the assembled faces.

“John Ashton was murdered on the night of the twenty-second of November,” he remarked significantly. “Therefore he had not been murdered when the veiled woman first met Mr. Cave for the first time, and he had been murdered when she met Mr. Cave the second time!”

There was a silence as significant as Mr. Carless’ tone upon this﻿—broken at last by Mr. Cave.

===Chinese===

“这我说得上来，”他答道。“当时我就记了一笔。那是在——对，在这儿——十一月二十一日。”

“我记得您说过，那些文件是两天之后收到的？”

“是的——二十三日。记录在这儿呢。”

卡利斯先生环视四周一张张脸。

“约翰·阿什顿是十一月二十二日夜里遇害的，”他意味深长地说道。“这就是说，那位戴面纱的女人头一回遇见凯夫先生的时候，他还没有遇害；而她第二回遇见凯夫先生的时候，他已经遇害了！”

接着是一阵沉默，和卡利斯先生的语气一样意味深长——最后是凯夫先生打破了它。

===Original===

“If I may say a word or two,” he remarked diffidently. “I don’t understand matters about this John Ashton. The barrister who asked me questions﻿—Mr. Millington-Bywater, is it﻿—said that he, or somebody, had positive proof that Mr. Ashton had my papers in his possession for some time previous to his death. Is that really so?”

Mr. Carless pointed to Mr. Perkwite.

“This is the gentleman whom Mr. Millington-Bywater could have put in the box this morning to prove that,” he replied. “Mr. Perkwite, of the Middle Temple﻿—a barrister-at-law, Mr. Cave. Mr. Perkwite met Mr. Ashton some three months ago at Marseilles, and Mr. Ashton then not only asked his advice about the Ellingham affair, alleging that he knew the missing Lord Marketstoke, but showed him the papers which you have recently deposited with Mr. Methley here﻿—which papers, Ashton alleged, were entrusted to him by Lord Marketstoke on his deathbed. Ashton, according to Mr. Perkwite, took particular care of these papers, and always carried them about with him in a pocketbook.”

Mr. Cave appeared to be much exercised in thought on hearing this.

“It is, of course, absurd to say that Lord Marketstoke﻿—myself!﻿—entrusted papers to anyone on his deathbed, since I am very much alive,” he said. “But it is, equally of course, quite possible that Ashton had my papers. Who was Ashton?”

“A man who had lived in Australia for some thirty-five or forty years at least,” replied Mr. Carless, “and who recently returned to England and settled down in London, in this very square. He lived chiefly in Melbourne, but we have heard that for some four or five years he was somewhere up country. You never heard of him out there? He was evidently well known in Melbourne.”

===Chinese===

“容我说一两句，”他怯生生地说道。“这个约翰·阿什顿的事，我弄不明白。向我提问的那位大律师——是米林顿-拜沃特（Millington-Bywater）先生吧——说他们，或是某个人，握有确凿证据，证明阿什顿先生在死前一度把我的文件握在自己手里。真是这样吗？”

卡利斯先生指了指佩尔怀特先生。

“这位先生，就是米林顿-拜沃特先生今早本可以传上证人席、来证明这一点的人，”他答道。“中殿律师学院的佩尔怀特先生——一位执业大律师，凯夫先生。佩尔怀特先生大约三个月前在马赛结识阿什顿先生，当时阿什顿先生不仅自称认识失踪的马克特斯托克勋爵、就埃灵厄姆这件事向他征求意见，还给他看了您新近存放在梅思利先生这里的那批文件——据阿什顿声称，那些文件是马克特斯托克勋爵临终时托付给他的。据佩尔怀特先生说，阿什顿对这些文件格外经心，一直放在一只皮夹子里随身携带。”

听到这话，凯夫先生显得心事重重。

“说什么马克特斯托克勋爵——我自己！——临终把文件托付给什么人，这自然是无稽之谈，因为我还活得好好的，”他说。“不过同样当然，阿什顿手里有我的文件，这倒十分可能。阿什顿是个什么人？”

“一个在澳大利亚至少住了三十五到四十年的人，”卡利斯先生答道，“前不久回英国来，就在伦敦、就在这个广场上安顿下来。他主要住在墨尔本，不过我们听说，有四五年工夫他在内陆某处。您在那边从来没听说过他？他在墨尔本显然是知名人物。”

===Original===

“No, I never heard of him,” replied Mr. Cave. “But I don’t know Melbourne very well; I know Sydney and Brisbane better. However, an idea strikes me﻿—Ashton may have had something to do with the purloining of my letters and effects at Wirra-Worra, when I met with the accident I told you of.”

“So far as we are aware,” remarked Mr. Carless, “Ashton was an eminently respectable man!”

“So far as you know!” said Mr. Cave. “There is a good deal in the saving clause, I think. I have known a good many men in Australia who were highly respectable in the last stages of life who had been anything but that in their earlier ones! Of what class was this Ashton?”

“I met him, occasionally,” said Methley, “though I never knew who he was until after his death. He was a very pleasant, kindly, good-humoured man﻿—but,” he added, “I should say, from his speech and manners, a man who had risen from a somewhat humble position of life. I remember noticing his hands﻿—they were the hands of a man who at some period had done hard manual labour.”

Mr. Cave smiled knowingly.

“There you are!” he said. “He had probably been a miner! Taking everything into consideration, I am inclined to believe that he was most likely one of the men, or the man, who stole my papers thirty-two years ago.”

===Chinese===

“没有，我从来没听说过他，”凯夫先生答道。“不过墨尔本我也不大熟；悉尼和布里斯班我熟一些。可是，我忽然想到一点——我在维拉-沃拉（Wirra-Worra）遭了我跟你们说过的那场事故，信件财物被人偷了，阿什顿说不定跟那桩窃案有些干系。”

“据我们所知，”卡利斯先生说道，“阿什顿可是个极其体面的人！”

“据你们所知！”凯夫先生说。“我想，这个‘但书’里头可大有文章。在澳大利亚，我见过不少人，晚年极其体面，早年却压根不是那么回事！这位阿什顿是什么出身？”

“我偶尔遇见过他，”梅思利说，“不过直到他死后，我才知道他是谁。他是个非常和气、亲切、脾气好的人——不过，”他补充道，“依我看，从他的谈吐举止来说，这是个从相当低微的出身爬起来的人。我记得留意过他那双手——那是一双人生某个时期干过繁重体力活的手。”

凯夫先生会心地一笑。

“这不就对了！”他说。“他八成当过矿工！把种种情形通盘考虑下来，我倾向于认为，三十二年前偷我文件的那伙人里，多半有他一个——或者，正是他本人干的。”

===Original===

“There may be something in this,” remarked Mr. Pawle, glancing uneasily at Mr. Carless. “It is a fact that the packet of letters to which Mr. Cave referred this morning as having been written by the Countess of Ellingham to Lord Marketstoke when a boy at school, was found by Mr. Viner and myself in Ashton’s house, and that the locket which he also mentioned is in existence﻿—facts which Mr. Cave will doubtless be glad to know of. But,” added the old lawyer, shaking his head, “what does all this imply? That Ashton, of whom up to now we have heard nothing but good, was not only a thief, but an impostor who was endeavouring, or meant to endeavour, to palm off a bogus claimant on people, who, but for Mr. Cave’s appearance and evidence, would certainly have been deceived! It is most amazing.”

“Don’t forget,” said Viner quietly, “that Mr. Perkwite says that Ashton showed him at Marseilles a certain marriage certificate and a birth certificate.”

Mr. Carless started.

“Ah!” he exclaimed. “I had forgotten that. Um! However, don’t let us forget, just now, that our main object in meeting was to do something towards tracking these people who gave Mr. Cave these papers. Now, Mr. Cave, you got no information out of the woman?”

“None!” answered Mr. Cave. “I was not to ask questions, you remember.”

“You took her for a gentlewoman?”

===Chinese===

“这话里头也许有些名堂，”波尔先生说着，不安地瞥了卡利斯先生一眼。“凯夫先生今早提到的那包信——埃灵厄姆伯爵夫人写给还在上学的少年马克特斯托克勋爵的那些信——确乎是我和维纳先生在阿什顿家里找到的，他提到的那只金质小盒坠也确实存在——这些事实，凯夫先生知道了想必乐意知晓。可是，”老律师摇着头补充道，“这一切意味着什么？意味着这个至今我们听到的全是好话的阿什顿，不但是个窃贼，还是个骗子——正在企图、或者说存心企图拿一个假主张人到众人跟前去鱼目混珠，倘若不是凯夫先生现身作证，这些人准会上当受骗！这真是匪夷所思。”

“别忘了，”维纳不动声色地说，“佩尔怀特先生说过，阿什顿在马赛给他看过一份结婚证书和一份出生证明。”

卡利斯先生猛地一怔。

“啊！”他失声道。“这件事我倒忘了。嗯！不过，眼下可别忘记，我们这次会面的主要目的，是设法追查把文件交给凯夫先生的那些人。那么，凯夫先生，您没从那女人嘴里问出一点消息？”

“一点儿也没有！”凯夫先生答道。“我是不许问话的，您总还记得。”

“您把她当作一位有身份的太太？”

===Original===

“Yes﻿—from her speech and manner.”

“Did she imply to you that she was an intermediary?”

“Yes﻿—she spoke of someone, indefinitely, you know, for whom she was acting.”

“And she told you, I think, that you had been recognized, in London, since your arrival, by someone who had known you in Australia years before?”

“Yes﻿—certainly she told me that.”

“Just let me look at that typewritten letter again, will you?” asked Mr. Carless. “It seems impossible, but we might get something out of that.”

===Chinese===

“是的——从她的言谈举止看。”

“她有没有向您暗示过，自己是个中间人？”

“有——您知道，她含含糊糊地说起过一个她为之办事的人。”

“还有，我记得她说，您到了伦敦以后，被一个多年前在澳大利亚就认识您的人认出来了？”

“是的——她确实对我这样说过。”

“请让我再看看那封打字的信，可以吗？”卡利斯先生问。“看着像是没指望了，可我们兴许还能从那上面弄出点什么来。”

===Original===

Mr. Cave handed the letter over, and once more it was passed from hand to hand: finally it fell into the hands of Miss Penkridge, who began to examine it with obvious curiosity.

“Afraid there’s nothing to be got out of that!” sighed Mr. Carless. “The rogues were cunning enough to typewrite the message﻿—if there’d been any handwriting, now, we might have had a chance! You say there was nothing on the envelope but your name, Mr. Cave?”

Mr. Cave opened his pocketbook again.

“There is the envelope,” he said. “Nothing but ‘Mr. Cave,’ as you see﻿—that is also typewritten.”

Miss Penkridge picked up the envelope as Mr. Cave tossed it across the table. She appeared to examine it carefully, but suddenly she turned to Mr. Carless.

“There *is* a clue in these things!” she exclaimed. “A plain clue! One that’s plain enough to me, anyway. I could follow it up. I don’t know whether you gentlemen can.”

===Chinese===

凯夫先生把信递了过去，信便又在一双双手里传看，最后落到了彭克里奇小姐手中，她带着显而易见的好奇查看起来。

“恐怕那上面弄不出什么名堂！”卡利斯先生叹道。“这帮骗子奸猾得很，用打字机打这封信——要是换作手写的，嘿，我们或许还有一线指望！您说信封上除了您的名字什么也没有，凯夫先生？”

凯夫先生又打开了那只皮夹子。

“这就是那个信封，”他说。“只有‘凯夫先生’几个字，您瞧——也是打字机打的。”

凯夫先生把信封扔过桌子，彭克里奇小姐把它拾了起来。她像是仔仔细细地查验，忽而却朝卡利斯先生转过身去。

“这些东西里头*确实*有线索！”她扬声道。“一条明明白白的线索！反正对我说来够明白的了。我就能顺着追下去。我就不知道诸位先生能不能了。”

===Original===

Mr. Carless, who had, up to that point, treated Miss Penkridge with good-humoured condescension, turned sharply upon her.

“What do you mean, ma’am?” he asked. “You really see something in﻿—in a typewritten letter?”

“A great deal!” answered Miss Penkridge. “And in the stationery on which it’s typed, and in the envelope in which it’s enclosed. Now look here: This letter has been typed on a half-sheet of notepaper. Hold the half-sheet up to the light﻿—what do you see? One half of the name and address of the stationer who supplied it, in watermark. What is that one half?”

Mr. Carless held the paper to the light and saw on the top line,﻿ ﻿… “sforth,” on the middle line,﻿ ﻿… “nd Stationer” and,﻿ ﻿… “n Hill” on the bottom line.

“My nephew there,” went on Miss Penkridge, “knows what that would be, in full, if the other half of the sheet were here. It would be precisely what it is under the flap of this envelope﻿—there you are! ‘Bigglesforth, Bookseller and Stationer, Craven Hill.’ Everybody in this district knows Bigglesforth﻿—we get our stationery from him. Now, Bigglesforth has not such a very big business in really expensive notepaper like this﻿—the other half of the sheet, of course, would have a finely engraved address on it﻿—and you can trace the owner of this paper through him, with patience and trouble.

“But here’s a still better clue! Look at this typewritten letter. In it, the letter *o* occurs with frequency. Now, notice﻿—the letter is broken, imperfect; the top left-hand curve has been chipped off. Do you mean to tell me that with time and trouble and patience you can’t find out to whom that machine belongs? Taking the fact that this half-sheet of notepaper came from Bigglesforth’s, of Craven Hill,” concluded Miss Penkridge with emphasis, “I should say that this document﻿—so important﻿—came from somebody who doesn’t live a million miles from here!”

===Chinese===

卡利斯先生此前待彭克里奇小姐，一直是一副和气的居高临下姿态，这时却霍地朝她转过身来。

“您这是什么意思，夫人？”他问。“您当真从一封——从一封打字的信里看出了什么？”

“多着呢！”彭克里奇小姐答道。“打字用的信纸上有，装信的信封上也有。听我说：这封信是打在半张信笺上的。把那半张纸举到亮处——您看见了什么？供应这纸的文具商的姓名地址，正好剩下一半水印。那半边是什么？”

卡利斯先生把纸举向亮处，看见头一行是“sforth”，当中一行是“nd Stationer”，底下一行是“n Hill”。

“我那位外甥就在那儿，”彭克里奇小姐接着说，“这半张纸的另一半要是也在，拼全了该是什么，他知道的。拼全了，恰正是这信封翻盖底下印的那几行——喏，您瞧！‘比格尔斯福斯，书商兼文具商，克雷文山。’这一带人人都知道比格尔斯福斯——我们用的文具就打他那儿买。这么说吧，像这种真正昂贵的信纸，比格尔斯福斯做的生意并不算大——这半张纸的另一半，照例会印着一份精刻的地址——你们只要肯花耐心、不辞麻烦，就能顺着他查出这纸的主人。

“可这儿还有一条更妙的线索！瞧这封打字的信。信里字母 *o* 出现得勤。喏，注意看——这个字母是残缺的，不完好；左上角的那道弯口给磕掉了。你们莫非是要说，肯花时间、下工夫、有耐心，还查不出这台机器是谁家的？再把这半张信笺出自克雷文山的比格尔斯福斯店里这一层考虑进去，”彭克里奇小姐加重语气作结道，“我就要说，这份文件——这份顶要紧的文件——出自的那位人物，住得离这儿可没有一百万英里远！”

===Original===

Mr. Carless had followed Miss Penkridge with admiring attention, and he now rose to his feet.

“Ma’am,” he exclaimed, “Mr. Viner’s notion of having you to join our council has proved invaluable! I’ll have that clue followed up instantly! Gentlemen, we can do no more just now﻿—let us separate. Mr. Cave﻿—you’ll continue to be heard of at the Belfield Hotel?”

“I shall be at your service any time, Mr. Carless,” responded Mr. Cave. “A telephone message will bring me at once to Lincoln’s Inn Fields.”

The assembly broke up, and Viner was left alone with Miss Penkridge.

“That was clever of you!” he said, admiringly. “I should never have noticed that. But﻿—there are a lot of typewriting machines in London!”

===Chinese===

卡利斯先生一直满怀钦佩地凝神听着彭克里奇小姐这番话，这时站起身来。

“夫人，”他高声道，“维纳先生想到请您来加入我们的商议，这一着真是无价之宝！我马上就叫人去追这条线索！诸位，眼下我们能做的就到此为止——就此散了吧。凯夫先生——您仍在贝尔菲尔德旅馆（Belfield Hotel），随时找得到吧？”

“我随时听候差遣，卡利斯先生，”凯夫先生应道。“一个电话，我立刻就到林肯律师学院广场。”

众人散去，屋里只剩下了维纳和彭克里奇小姐。

“您这一手真高明！”他赞叹道。“我怎么也留意不到那上头。不过——伦敦城里打字机可多着呢！”

===Original===

“Not so many owned by customers of Bigglesforth’s!” retorted Miss Penkridge. “I’d work it out, if I were a detective!”

The parlourmaid looked in and attracted Viner’s attention.

“Mr. Felpham wants you at the telephone, sir,” she said.

===Chinese===

“比格尔斯福斯店里的主顾，家里有打字机的可没那么多！”彭克里奇小姐反唇相讥。“我要是个侦探，准能把这事查个水落石出！”

客厅侍女探进头来，引起了维纳的注意。

“费尔法姆先生请您听电话，先生，”她说。

## XXV / 二十五

===Original===

Through the Telephone
Events had crowded so thick and fast upon Viner during the last day or two, that he went to the telephone fully expecting to hear of some new development. But he was scarcely prepared for his solicitor’s first words.
“Viner!” said Felpham, whose voice betrayed his excitement. “Is that man Cave still with you?”
“No!” answered Viner. “Why?”
“Listen carefully,” responded Felpham. “In spite of all he asserts, and his long tale this morning at the police-court, I believe he’s a rank impostor! I’ve just had another talk with Hyde.”

===Chinese===

通过电话
这一两天来，种种事变在维纳身上压得又密又急，他去接电话时，满心以为会听到什么新的进展。可是他的律师开口头一句话，仍叫他几乎措手不及。
“维纳！”费尔法姆说，话音里透着按捺不住的激动，“那个自称凯夫的人还在你那儿吗？”
“不在了！”维纳答道，“怎么？”
“你仔细听着，”费尔法姆应道，“任凭他怎么说，也任凭他今天上午在治安法庭上编的那套长篇大论，我都认定他是个彻头彻尾的骗子！我刚才又和海德谈了一次。”

===Original===

“Well?” demanded Viner.
“Hyde,” answered Felpham, “persists that he’s not mistaken. He swears that the man is Nugent Starr. He says there’s no doubt of it! And he’s told me of another actor, a man named George Bellingham, who’s now somewhere in London, who can positively identify him as Starr. I’m going to find Bellingham this afternoon﻿—there’s some deep-laid plot in all this, and that fellow had been cleverly coached in the event of his being unexpectedly tackled.﻿ ﻿… Viner!”
“Well﻿—I’m listening carefully,” replied Viner.
“Where’s this man gone?” demanded Felpham.
“To his hotel, I should think,” answered Viner. “He left here just before one.”
“Listen!” said Felpham. “Do you think it would be wise to post New Scotland Yard on to him﻿—detectives, you know?”

===Chinese===

“谈出什么了？”维纳追问。
“海德，”费尔法姆答道，“一口咬定自己没有认错。他发誓那人就是纽金特·斯塔尔。他说这一点毫无疑问！他还向我提起另一个演员，一个名叫乔治·贝灵汉（George Bellingham）的人，眼下就在伦敦城里，能毫不含糊地指认那人就是斯塔尔。我今天下午就去找贝灵汉——这背后准藏着一场处心积虑的阴谋，那家伙也分明被人细心调教过，以防冷不丁被人当头质问。……维纳！”
“嗯——我正仔细听着呢，”维纳答道。
“那人上哪儿去了？”费尔法姆问。
“我想是回他下榻的旅馆去了，”维纳答道，“他一点钟之前刚离开这儿。”
“听着！”费尔法姆说，“你看要不要把新苏格兰场（New Scotland Yard）的人派去盯住他——就是说，派侦探？”

===Original===

Viner considered swiftly. In the rush of events he had forgotten that Carless had already given instructions for the watching of the pseudo Mr. Cave.
“Why not find this man Bellingham first?” he suggested. “If he can prove, positively, that the fellow is Nugent Starr, you’d have something definite to work on. Where can Bellingham be found?”
“Hyde’s given me the address of a theatrical agent in Bedford Street who’s likely to know of his whereabouts,” replied Felpham. “I’m going over there at once. Hyde saw Bellingham in town three weeks ago.”
“Let me know at once,” said Viner. “If you find Bellingham, take him to the Belfield Hotel and contrive to show him the man. Call me up later.”
He went away from his telephone and sought Miss Penkridge, whom he found in her room, arraying herself for out of doors.

===Chinese===

维纳迅速权衡了一下。连日事变频仍，他倒忘了卡利斯早已吩咐下去，派人监视那个假冒的凯夫先生。
“何不先找到这位贝灵汉？”他提议道，“倘若他能确凿证明那家伙就是纽金特·斯塔尔，你手里便有了实实在在的凭据。贝灵汉能上哪儿去找呢？”
“海德给了我贝德福德街（Bedford Street）一家戏剧经纪人的地址，那人多半知道他的下落，”费尔法姆答道，“我这就过去。三个星期前，海德还在城里见过贝灵汉。”
“一有消息立刻告诉我，”维纳说，“倘若你找到了贝灵汉，就带他到贝尔菲尔德旅馆（Belfield Hotel）去，想法子让他见一见那人。回头再打电话给我。”
他放下电话，去找彭克里奇小姐，只见她正在自己房里穿戴整齐，准备出门。

===Original===

“Here’s a new development!” he exclaimed, shutting the door on them. “Felpham’s just telephoned to say that Hyde persists that the man who calls himself Cave is Nugent Starr! In that case, he won’t﻿—”
Miss Penkridge interrupted her nephew with a sniff.
“My dear Richard,” she said, with a note of contemptuous impatience, “in a case like this, you don’t know who’s who or who isn’t who! It wouldn’t surprise me in the slightest if the man turns out to be Nugent Starr.”
“How did he come by such a straight tale, then?” asked Viner doubtfully.
“Carefully prepared﻿—in case of need,” declared Miss Penkridge as she tied her bonnet-strings with a decisive tug. “The whole thing’s a plant!”

===Chinese===

“又有新的变故了！”他一边随手关上房门，一边嚷道，“费尔法姆刚来电话，说海德一口咬定，那个自称凯夫的人就是纽金特·斯塔尔！倘若如此，他就不会——”
彭克里奇小姐对侄子冷哼一声，打断了他。
“我亲爱的理查德，”她说，语气里带着几分轻蔑的不耐，“这样的案子，你根本分不清谁是谁、谁又不是谁！就算到头来那人真是纽金特·斯塔尔，我也丝毫不觉得奇怪。”
“那么，他那一套滴水不漏的说辞是打哪儿来的？”维纳将信将疑地问。
“早就精心备好——以备不时之需，”彭克里奇小姐断然说道，同时一把系紧了帽带，“整件事就是一场预先布置的把戏！”

===Original===

“That’s what Felpham says,” remarked Viner. “But﻿—where are you going?” he broke off as Miss Penkridge, seizing an umbrella, started for the door. “Lunch is just going in.”
“My lunch can wait﻿—I’ve had a biscuit and a glass of sherry,” asserted Miss Penkridge. “I’m going round to Bigglesforth the stationer’s, to follow up that clue I suggested just now. I dare say I can do a bit of detective work as well as another, and in my opinion, Richard, there’s no time to be lost. I have been blessed and endowed,” continued Miss Penkridge, as she laid hold of the door-handle, “with exceedingly acute perceptions, and I saw something when I made that suggestion which I’m quite sure none of you men, with all your brains, saw!”
“What?” demanded Viner.
“I saw that my suggestion wasn’t at all pleasing to the man who calls himself Cave!” exclaimed Miss Penkridge. “It was only a flash of his eye, a sudden droop at the corners of his lips﻿—but I saw! And I saw something else, too﻿—that he got away as quickly as ever he could after I’d made that suggestion.”

===Chinese===

“费尔法姆也是这么说的，”维纳应道，“可是——你要上哪儿去？”他见彭克里奇小姐抓起一把伞，径直朝门口走去，便把话头打住，“午饭这就摆上桌了。”
“我的午饭等等无妨——我已经吃过一块饼干、喝过一杯雪利酒了，”彭克里奇小姐说得斩钉截铁，“我要去比格斯福思文具店（Bigglesforth the stationer's）走一趟，把方才我提的那条线索追查下去。要说办一点侦探的差事，我自信不比任何人差；况且依我看，理查德，此事一刻也耽搁不得。我这个人得天独厚，”彭克里奇小姐一面握住门把手，一面接着说，“生就一副极其敏锐的观察力；我提那个建议的时候，看出了一些苗头——你们那些男人任凭脑子再多，我敢肯定，没有一个看出来的！”
“看出了什么？”维纳问。
“我看出我那个建议让那个自称凯夫的人大为不快！”彭克里奇小姐大声说，“不过是他的眼睛闪了一下，嘴角蓦地往下一撇——可我就是看见了！我还看出了另一点——我一提出那个建议，他就脚底抹油，尽快溜走了。”

===Original===

Viner looked at his aunt with amused wonder. He thought she was unduly suspicious, and Miss Penkridge guessed his thoughts.
“You’ll see,” she said as she opened the door.
“There are going to be strange revelations, Richard Viner, my boy! You said at the beginning of this that you’d suddenly got plunged into the middle of things﻿—well, in my opinion, we’re now coming to the end of things, and I’m going to do my bit to bring it about.”
With that Miss Penkridge sailed away, her step determined and her head high, and Viner, pondering many matters, went downstairs to entertain his visitors, the unlucky Hyde’s sisters, with stories of the morning’s proceedings and hopes of their brother’s speedy acquittal. The poor ladies were of that temperament which makes its possessors clutch eagerly at any straw of hope floating on the sea of trouble, and they listened eagerly to all that their host could tell.

===Chinese===

维纳又好笑又惊奇地望着姨妈。他觉得她未免疑心太重，而彭克里奇小姐却猜中了他的心思。
“你等着瞧吧，”她一面开门一面说。
“理查德·维纳，我的孩子，眼看就要有离奇的真相大白了！这件事一开头，你说过自己突然被卷进了种种事情的正中心——哼，依我看，我们现在正朝种种事情的尽头走去，我也要尽自己一份力，促成这个收场。”
说罢，彭克里奇小姐步履决然、昂首挺胸地扬长而去。维纳满腹思量，下楼去款待他的客人——那不幸的海德的姐妹们——给她们讲上午开庭的种种经过，说说她们兄弟指日可望无罪开释的话。这两位可怜的太太生就那样的性情：在烦恼的汪洋大海上，凡有半点希望之草漂过，便要急急抓在手中；因此主人能讲的一切，她们无不听得聚精会神。

===Original===

“Langton has an excellent memory!” declared the elder Miss Hyde. “Don’t you remember, sister, what a quantity of poetical pieces he knew by heart when he was quite a child?”
“Before he was seven years of age!” said the younger sister. “And at ten he could recite the whole of the trial scene from *The Merchant of Venice*. Oh, yes, he always had a marvellous memory! If Langton says he remembers this man in America, dear Mr. Viner, I am sure Langton will be right, and that this is the man. But what a very dreadful person to utter such terrible falsehoods!”
“And on oath!” said the elder Miss Hyde, solemnly. “On oath, sister!”
“Sad!” murmured the younger lady. “Most sad! We find London life very disturbing, dear Mr. Viner, after our quiet country existence.”

===Chinese===

“兰顿的记性可好得很！”年长的那位海德小姐说道，“妹妹，你不记得吗？他还是个十足的小娃娃时，就能背多少诗篇了！”
“还不满七岁呢！”妹妹说道，“到十岁上，《威尼斯商人》（The Merchant of Venice）里那整场庭审的戏文，他都能一字不漏地背出来。哦，是呀，他的记性向来好得出奇！亲爱的维纳先生，只要兰顿说他在美国记得这个人，我敢肯定兰顿准没错，这人就是他说的那个。可是，那样一个说出这般可怕谎话的人，多么叫人胆寒啊！”
“而且还是起过誓的！”年长的海德小姐神色庄重地说，“当着誓言说的，妹妹！”
“可悲啊！”妹妹低声叹道，“可悲之至！亲爱的维纳先生，我们过惯了乡间清净的日子，只觉得伦敦的生活实在叫人心绪不宁。”

===Original===

“There are certainly some disturbing elements in it,” admitted Viner.
Just then came another interruption; for the second time since his return from the police-court, he was summoned to the telephone. To his great surprise, the voice that hailed him was Mrs. Killenhall’s.
“Is that Mr. Viner?” the voice demanded in its usual brisk, clear tones.
“Yes,” answered Viner. “Is that Mrs. Killenhall?”
“Yes!” came the prompt reply. “Mr. Viner, can you be so very kind? Miss Wickham and I have come down to the City on some business connected with Mr. Ashton, and we do so want somebody’s help. Can you run down at once and join us? So sorry to trouble you, but we really do want a gentleman here.”

===Chinese===

“伦敦生活里确实有些搅扰人心的东西，”维纳承认道。
正在这时又来了一次打断：这是他自治安法庭回来后第二次被叫去听电话。令他大为惊讶的是，开口招呼他的声音，竟是基伦霍尔太太的。
“是维纳先生吗？”那声音用它一贯干脆爽利的调子问道。
“是我，”维纳答道，“您是基伦霍尔太太吗？”
“是我！”回答来得干脆利落。“维纳先生，能否请您行个好？威克姆小姐和我到伦敦城来办一点与阿什顿先生相关的事，眼下实在需要有人帮衬。您能不能立刻赶过来同我们会合？真不好意思麻烦您，可我们这儿确实需要有位先生在场。”

===Original===

“Certainly!” responded Viner. “I’ll come to you at once. But where are you?”
“Come to 23 Mirrapore Street, off Whitechapel Road,” answered Mrs. Killenhall. “There is someone here who knew Mr. Ashton, and I should like you to see him. Can you come at once? And have you the address right?”
“A moment﻿—repeat it, please,” replied Viner, pulling out a memorandum book. He noted the address and spoke again: “I’ll be there in half an hour, Mrs. Killenhall,” he said. “Sooner, if it’s possible.”
“Thank you so much,” responded Mrs. Killenhall’s steady voice. “So good of you﻿—goodbye for the present, then.”

===Chinese===

“当然可以！”维纳应道，“我马上就来。只是你们在哪儿？”
